Sorrow we are ready to endure﻿—death is less than nothing to us﻿—we will but follow in the footsteps of our King and of our Queen and of many whom we care for and whom you and your proconsul and your colleagues have brutally murdered. Shame cannot touch us, and our honour and our pride are so far beyond your reach that your impious and bloodstained hands can never sully them.”
She had spoken very slowly and very quietly. There were no heroics about her attitude. Even Martin-Roget﻿—callous brute though he was﻿—felt that she had only spoken just as she felt, and that nothing that he might say, no plea that he might urge, would ever shake her determination.
“Then it seems to me,” he said, “that I am only wasting my time by trying to make you see reason and commonsense. You look upon me as a brute. Well! perhaps I am. At any rate I am that which your father and you have made me.

===Chinese===

“看来你，”他反唇相讥道，“根本忘了依照法令，我有权凭借强力强行占有你顽固抗拒的一切。”
“我刚才不是说过了吗，”她回答道，“我选择死亡。同你生活在一起，生不如死，纯属耻辱。”
“我大可找个神甫在无需你首肯的情形下强行替咱们主婚；而你的教义严令禁止你自绝性命，”他冷笑着威胁道。
对于这番话，她未置一词，但他心知肚明自己已经得到了回答。强压下一句咒骂，过了片刻他又说道：
“这么说，你宁愿拉着你的亲生父亲一道黄泉赴死？然而他曾苦苦恳求你权衡利弊、听从理性。他已经亲口同意了这桩婚事。”
“让我见见我的父亲，”她斩钉截铁地反驳道，“让我亲耳听他当面开口讲出这些话来。
“哈！”她见马丹-罗热别过头去、装出一副若无其事的模样耸耸肩膀，便立刻补充道，“你根本不敢、也绝无可能让我去见他。整整三天来你把我们分隔两地，想必用连篇鬼话两头欺瞒。我父亲是德·凯尔诺冈公爵、特朗特穆尔侯爵，”她傲然挺起胸膛说道，“他宁愿与他的女儿并肩赴死，也绝不愿看着她嫁给一个卑鄙的罪犯。”
“而你呢，姑娘，”马丹-罗热冷酷地回敬道，“你难道忍心看着你的生身父亲被烙上重刑犯的耻辱印记，同窃贼绑在一处，沉入卢瓦尔河溺毙殒命吗？”
“我的父亲，”她毅然决然地回答，“生为英豪，死亦鬼雄，他将以一位英勇而尊贵的绅士之躯坦然赴死。恶徒的污名绝不可能玷污他的清誉。苦难我们早已做好准备承受——死亡对我们而言更轻如鸿毛——我们不过是步先王与先后的后尘，追随那些被你、你的总督以及你们同党惨绝人寰杀戮的至亲挚友的足迹罢了。耻辱休想沾染我们分毫，我们的尊严与骄傲高悬于九天之上，凭你们那双大逆不道、沾满鲜血的肮脏魔爪，永远休想玷污它们。”
她语速极缓，语调极度从容平静，身上没有半点做作的英雄做派。哪怕像马丹-罗热这般冷酷残忍的粗汉，也深切感受到她字字句句发自肺腑；他明白自己无论说尽千言万语，无论提出何等威逼利诱，都绝不可能动摇她分毫的决心。
“那么依我看，”他说道，“我试图劝你遵从常理与理性，纯粹是在白费口舌。你把我当成一个禽兽畜生。好嘛！或许我确实是。但无论如何，正是你和你那好父亲一手把我逼成今天这副模样的。

===Original===

Four years ago, when you had power over me and over mine, you brutalised us. Today we﻿—the people﻿—are your masters and we make you suffer, not for all﻿—that were impossible﻿—but for part of what you made us suffer. That, after all, is only bare justice. By making you my wife I would have saved you from death﻿—not from humiliation, for that you must endure, and at my hands in a full measure﻿—but I would have made you my wife because I still have pleasant recollections of that kiss which I snatched from you on that never-to-be-forgotten night and in the darkness﻿—a kiss for which you would gladly have seen me hang then, if you could have laid hands on me.”
He paused, trying to read what was going on behind those fine eyes of hers, with their vacant, farseeing gaze which seemed like another barrier between her and him. At this rough allusion to that moment of horror and of shame, she had not moved a muscle, nor did her gaze lose its fixity.
He laughed.
“It is an unpleasant recollection, eh, my proud lady? The first kiss of passion was not implanted on your exquisite lips by that fine gentleman whom you deemed worthy of your hand and your love, but by Pierre Adet, the miller’s son, what? a creature not quite so human as your horse or your pet dog. Neither you nor I are like to forget that methinks.﻿ ﻿…”
Yvonne vouchsafed no reply to the taunt, and for a moment there was silence in the room, until Chauvelin’s thin, suave voice broke in quite gently:
“Do not lose your patience with the wench, citizen Martin-Roget. Your time is too precious to be wasted in useless recriminations.”
“I have finished with her,” retorted the other sullenly. “She shall be dealt with now as I think best. I agree with citizen Carrier. He is right after all. To the Loire with the lot of that foul brood!”
“Nay!” here rejoined Chauvelin with placid urbanity, “are you not a little harsh, citizen, with our fair Yvonne? Remember! Women have moods and megrims. What they indignantly refuse to yield to us one day, they will grant with a smile the next. Our beautiful Yvonne is no exception to this rule, I’ll warrant.”
Even while he spoke he threw a glance of warning on his colleague. There was something enigmatic in his manner at this moment, in the strange suavity wherewith he spoke these words of conciliation and of gentleness. Martin-Roget was as usual ready with an impatient retort.

===Chinese===

四年前，当你们对我以及我的骨肉握有生杀大权时，你们曾像对待牲口一样残害我们。如今我们——广大人民——翻身做主成了你们的主人，我们要让你们尝尝当年强加于我们身上的苦楚，即便不能加倍奉还——那本非人力所及——也至少要讨还其中的一部分。归根结底，这不过是天经地义的公道罢了。娶你为妻，本可以饶你不死——虽然免不了要受屈辱，那是你必须承受的，而且注定要原原本本由我亲手施加——但我之所以要娶你，是因为在那刻骨铭心、黑灯瞎火的黑夜里，我强吻你的那一幕至今仍让我回味无穷——当时只要你能逮住我，你准会巴不得亲眼看着我被活活吊死。”
他止住话头，试图透过她那双美丽的眼睛窥探她内心的波澜，而她那空茫而辽远的凝视仿佛成了横亘在两人之间的一道难以逾越的壁垒。听到对方这般粗暴提及当年那屈辱惊恐的瞬间，她的面部肌肉甚至没有抽动一下，目光也丝毫未曾移开。
他放声冷笑起来。
“这回忆不大舒坦吧，傲慢的贵族小姐？留在你那娇艳欲滴嘴唇上的初吻，并非出自你心目中配得上你垂青的尊贵绅士，而是被磨坊主的儿子皮埃尔·阿代捷足先登了，可不是吗？一个在你眼里地位甚至不如你跨下骏马或爱犬的下贱胚子。我想，你我都一辈子忘不了那一刻吧……”
面对这恶毒的挑衅，伊冯娜连半句反驳都不屑施舍，阁楼里陷入了死寂，直到肖维兰那尖细文雅的嗓音轻柔地打破了沉默：
“别对那小妞失了耐心，马丹-罗热公民。你的时间宝贵得很，何必浪费在毫无意义的陈年旧怨上。”
“我已经跟她把话说尽了，”对方阴郁地反驳道，“接下来该怎么处置她全由我说了算。我完全赞成卡里耶公民的断决。他终归是对的。把这帮万恶的余孽统统丢进卢瓦尔河里去！”
“慢着！”肖维兰带着温和文雅的腔调反驳道，“你对咱们美丽的伊冯娜未免太过苛刻了吧，公民？记着！女人总有闹脾气和阴晴不定的时候。今日她们愤然拒绝的好意，明日说不定就会笑吟吟地双手奉上。我敢担保，咱们美丽的伊冯娜也绝不会例外。”
说话间，他朝同僚投去充满警告意味的一瞥。他此刻的言谈举止透着一股难以言喻的高深莫测，尤其是他在吐露这些圆场与温和责备的说辞时展现出的异样温雅，令马丹-罗热大惑不解并彻底被镇住了。

===Original===

He was in a mood to bully and to brutalise, to heap threat upon threat, to win by frightfulness that which he could not gain by persuasion. Perhaps that at this moment he desired Yvonne de Kernogan for wife, more even than he desired her death. At any rate his headstrong temper was ready to chafe against any warning or advice. But once again Chauvelin’s stronger mentality dominated over his less resolute colleague. Martin-Roget﻿—the fowler﻿—was in his turn caught in the net of a keener snarer than himself, and whilst﻿—with the obstinacy of the weak﻿—he was making mental resolutions to rebuke Chauvelin for his interference later on, he had already fallen in with the latter’s attitude.
“The wench has had three whole days wherein to alter her present mood,” he said more quietly, “and you know yourself, citizen, that the proconsul will not wait after today.”
“The day is young yet,” rejoined Chauvelin. “It still hath six hours to its credit.﻿ ﻿… Six hours.﻿ ﻿… Three hundred and sixty minutes!” he continued with a pleasant little laugh; “time enough for a woman to change her mind three hundred and sixty times. Let me advise you, citizen, to leave the wench to her own meditations for the present, and I trust that she will accept the advice of a man who has a sincere regard for her beauty and her charms and who is old enough to be her father, and seriously think the situation over in a conciliatory spirit. M. le duc de Kernogan will be grateful to her, for of a truth he is not over happy either at the moment﻿ ﻿… and will be still less happy in the depot tomorrow: it is overcrowded, and typhus, I fear me, is rampant among the prisoners. He has, I am convinced﻿—in spite of what the citizeness says to the contrary﻿—a rooted objection to being hurled into the Loire, or to be arraigned before the bar of the Convention, not as an aristocrat and a traitor but as an unit of an undesirable herd of criminals sent up to Paris for trial, by an anxious and harried proconsul. There! there!” he added benignly, “we will not worry our fair Yvonne any longer, will we, citizen? I think she has grasped the alternative and will soon realise that marriage with an honourable patriot is not such an untoward fate after all.”
“And now, citizen Martin-Roget,” he concluded, “I pray you allow me to take my leave of the fair lady and to give you the wise recommendation to do likewise.

===Chinese===

他此刻满脑子只想逞凶肆虐、把威胁层层加码，用血腥恐怖强行夺取凭劝诱所无法企及的一切。或许在这一刻，比起渴望伊冯娜·凯尔诺冈丧命，他甚至更渴望娶她为妻。无论如何，他那顽固蛮横的脾气随时准备抗拒任何告诫与劝导。然而再一次，肖维兰那更强大的心智完全压制住了他那不够坚定的同伙。马丹-罗热——这个自以为是的捕鸟人——反过来落入了一个比自己老练得多的捕鸟大师的罗网；尽管出于弱者的固执，他暗自在心底盘算着事后定要为肖维兰的横加干涉讨个说法，可在行动上他却已经顺从了后者的安排。
“这小妞已经有整整三天时间来改弦更张了，”他放低了声调说道，“而且你自己也清楚，公民，总督在今天之后绝不会再宽限片刻。”
“今日还长着呢，”肖维兰反驳道，“今天还有六个时辰的宽裕……六个时辰……整整三百六十分钟！”他伴着一声快意的轻笑继续说道，“足够一个妇人改换三百六十次心意了。听我一句劝，公民，眼下不妨让这位姑娘独自清静清静，我也深信她定会采纳一个既由衷欣赏她的美貌才情、年纪又足以当她父辈之人的由衷劝告，以息事宁人的态度重新严肃权衡眼前的局势。德·凯尔诺冈公爵也会对她感激涕零的，毕竟说老实话，他老人家眼下的日子也绝不好过……而要是明天被押进保税仓库，那日子准会更加难熬：那里面拥挤不堪，依我看，斑疹伤寒已经在囚犯中猖獗蔓延开了。我坚信——尽管女公民刚才口口声声矢口否认——无论是被径直丢进卢瓦尔河溺死，还是不以贵族与叛徒的名义、反倒作为被焦头烂额的总督打包送交巴黎受审的低贱罪犯群中的一员被押上国民公会的法庭，公爵大人打心眼里必定是极不情愿的。好啦！好啦！”他慈眉善目地补充道，“咱们就别再叨扰咱们美丽的伊冯娜了，你说呢，公民？我想她已经彻底看清了何去何从，很快便会想通，嫁给一位光荣的爱国者终究不是什么坏下场。”
“那么，马丹-罗热公民，”他最后总结道，“请容我向这位美丽的女士告退，并恳请你也听从这番金玉良言，同我一道离去。

===Original===

She will be far better alone for awhile. Night brings good counsel, so they say.”
He watched the girl keenly while he spoke. Her impassivity had not deserted her for a single moment: but whether her calmness was of hope or of despair he was unable to decide. On the whole he thought it must be the latter: hope would have kindled a spark in those dark, purple-rimmed eyes, it would have brought moisture to the lips, a tremor to the hand.
The Scarlet Pimpernel was in Nantes﻿—that fact was established beyond a doubt﻿—but Chauvelin had come to the conclusion that so far as Yvonne Dewhurst herself was concerned, she knew nothing of the mysterious agencies that were working on her behalf.
Chauvelin’s hand closed with a nervous contraction over the packet of papers in his pocket. Something of the secret of that enigmatic English adventurer lay revealed within its folds. Chauvelin had not yet had the opportunity of examining them: the interview with Yvonne had been the most important business for the moment.
From somewhere in the distance a city clock struck six. The afternoon was wearing on. The keenest brain in Europe was on the watch to drag one woman and one man from the deadly trap which had been so successfully set for them. A few hours more and Chauvelin in his turn would be pitting his wits against the resources of that intricate brain, and he felt like a warhorse scenting blood and battle. He was aching to get to work﻿—aching to form his plans﻿—to lay his snares﻿—to dispose his trap so that the noble English quarry should not fail to be caught within its meshes.
He gave a last look to Yvonne, who was still sitting quite impassive, gazing through the squalid walls into some beautiful distance, the reflection of which gave to her pale, wan face an added beauty.
“Let us go, citizen Martin-Roget,” he said peremptorily. “There is nothing else that we can do here.”
And Martin-Roget, the weaker morally of the two, yielded to the stronger personality of his colleague. He would have liked to stay on for awhile, to gloat for a few moments longer over the helplessness of the woman who to him represented the root of every evil which had ever befallen him and his family. But Chauvelin commanded and he felt impelled to obey.

===Chinese===

独自一人待上一阵子对她再好不过。俗话说得好，黑夜能给人带来清醒的睿智。”
说话间，他锐利的目光一刻不停地打量着少女。她的镇定自若从始至终未曾动摇分毫；然而这种从容究竟是源于希望还是出于绝望，连他也拿捏不准。总的来看，他断定多半是后者：希望定会在那双泛着青紫眼圈的幽暗明眸中点燃火花，定会给干裂的嘴唇带来滋润，定会让指尖微微战栗。
红花侠就在南特——这一事实早已确凿无疑——然而肖维兰得出的结论是，单就伊冯娜·迪尤赫斯特本人而言，她对暗中正为营救她奔走的那股神秘力量完全一无所知。
肖维兰的手神经质地收拢，紧紧按在大衣口袋里的那叠文书上。那位神神秘秘的英国冒险家的部分秘密，正隐秘地封存在这叠纸张之中。肖维兰尚未腾出工夫仔细审阅；面见伊冯娜才是眼下最十万火急的正事。
远处的钟楼敲响了六下，夜色正渐次加深。全欧洲最敏锐超凡的头脑正密切窥伺着，企图把这一男一女从已然天衣无缝布下的致命陷阱中搭救出去。再过几个小时，肖维兰就将与那颗深谋远虑的头脑一决雌雄，他犹如嗅到了血腥与沙场气息的战马般热血沸腾。他急不可耐地渴望投入行动——迫不及待地想要制定周密计划、布下天罗地网、设好机巧圈套，务求将那只高贵的英国猎物万无一失地一网打尽。
他最后看了一眼伊冯娜，她依然木然端坐着，目光穿透破败凄凉的四壁凝视着遥远而美丽的远方，那远方的倒影为她苍白憔悴的容颜增添了一抹别样的动人光彩。
“咱们走吧，马丹-罗热公民，”他不容置疑地说道，“留在这儿咱们也无计可施了。”
在意志品格上稍逊一筹的马丹-罗热，在同僚强大的气场面前再次甘拜下风。他本想再逗留一阵，对眼前这个在他看来代表着降临在他及其家族头上一切苦难罪魁祸首的软弱妇人再多幸灾乐祸片刻。然而肖维兰下了号令，他不得不言听计从。

===Original===

He gave one long, last look on Yvonne﻿—a look that was as full of triumph as of mockery﻿—he looked round the four dank walls, the unglazed window, the broken pitcher, the mouldy bread. Revenge was of a truth the sweetest emotion of the human heart. Pierre Adet﻿—son of the miller who had been hanged by orders of the Duc de Kernogan for a crime which he had never committed﻿—would not at this moment have changed places with Fortune’s Benjamin.

===Chinese===

他最后深深凝望了伊冯娜一眼——那目光中既充斥着得胜的狂喜，又满是残酷的讥嘲——他环顾了一下潮湿阴冷的四壁、无遮无拦的窗口、残破的瓦罐以及发霉的黑面包。复仇当真是人世间最甘美醇厚的滋味。皮埃尔·阿代——那位因莫须有罪名被德·凯尔诺冈公爵下令处绞刑的磨坊主之子——在这一刻即便拿命运之神的宠儿来换，他也绝不退让分毫。

#### IV / 第四节
===Original===

Downstairs in Louise Adet’s kitchen, Martin-Roget seized his colleague by the arm.
“Sit down a moment, citizen,” he said persuasively, “and tell me what you think of it all.”
Chauvelin sat down at the other’s invitation. All his movements were slow, deliberate, perfectly calm.
“I think,” he said drily, “as far as your marriage with the wench is concerned, that you are beaten, my friend.”
“Tshaw!” The exclamation, raucous and surcharged with hate came from Louise Adet. She, too, like Pierre﻿—more so than Pierre mayhap﻿—had cause to hate the Kernogans. She, too, like Pierre had lived the last three days in the full enjoyment of the thought that Fate and Chance were about to level things at last between herself and those detested aristos. Silent and sullen she was shuffling about in the room, among her pots and pans, but she kept an eye upon her brother’s movements and an ear on what he said. Men were apt to lose grit where a pretty wench was concerned. It takes a woman’s rancour and a woman’s determination to carry a scheme of vengeance against another to a successful end.
Martin-Roget rejoined more calmly:
“I knew that she would still be obstinate,” he said. “If I forced her into a marriage, which I have the right to do, she might take her own life and make me look a fool. So I don’t want to do that. I believe in the persuasiveness of the Rat Mort tonight,” he added with a cynical laugh, “and if that fails.﻿ ﻿… Well! I was never really in love with the fair Yvonne, and now she has even ceased to be desirable.﻿ ﻿… If the Rat Mort fails to act on her sensibilities as I would wish, I can easily console myself by following Carrier’s herd to Paris. Louise shall come with me﻿—eh, little sister?﻿—and we’ll give ourselves the satisfaction of seeing M. le duc de Kernogan and his exquisite daughter stand in the felon’s dock﻿—tried for malpractices and for evil living. We’ll see them branded as convicts and packed off like so much cattle to Cayenne. That will be a sight,” he concluded with a deep sigh of satisfaction, “which will bring rest to my soul.”
He paused: his face looked sullen and evil under the domination of that passion which tortured him.
Louise Adet had shuffled up close to her brother.

===Chinese===

来到楼下路易丝·阿代的厨房里，马丹-罗热一把扯住了同僚的胳膊。
“坐下歇会儿吧，公民，”他极力劝说道，“同我讲讲你对这整桩事的看法。”
肖维兰依言坐了下来。他的一举一动缓慢、从容，沉着至极。
“依我看，”他干硬地说道，“就你迎娶那小妞的事而言，老兄，你算是彻底输了。”
“啐！”这声满溢着仇恨的刺耳怒啐出自路易丝·阿代之口。同皮埃尔一样——甚至或许比皮埃尔更甚——她也有着刻骨铭心痛恨凯尔诺冈家的深仇大恨。与皮埃尔一样，在过去的三天里，她一直沉醉于命运与机缘终于要让她同那些令人作呕的贵族佬扯平旧账的狂喜中。她一声不吭、阴沉着脸在屋里的锅碗瓢盆间挪动收拾，却始终留心观察着弟弟的一举一动，侧耳倾听着他的只言片语。一碰上美貌小妞，大老爷们往往容易心慈手软丢了骨气。唯有女人的仇怨与女人的坚韧，才能把针对仇敌的复仇计划贯彻到底。
马丹-罗热心平气和了许多反驳道：
“我早就料到她依旧冥顽不灵，”他说道，“若凭仗手头的权力强行逼她成婚，她很可能会自寻短见，反倒让我沦为天下人的笑柄。所以我不想走那一步。我相信今晚‘死耗子’酒馆的说服力，”他冷笑着补充道，“而要是这一手也落了空……哼！我压根就从没真正爱过那个漂亮的伊冯娜，如今她甚至已经不再让我着迷……要是‘死耗子’没能如我所愿彻底摧毁她的骨气，我大可跟着卡里耶押解的那帮牲口一道回巴黎聊以自慰。路易丝也会随我同去——你说呢，妹子？——我们将亲眼目睹德·凯尔诺冈公爵和他那娇滴滴的千金站在重罪犯审判席上的壮观景象——因欺诈中饱私囊与伤风败俗受审。我们将亲眼看着他们被烙上苦役犯的烙印，像牲口一样打包发配到卡宴去。那番盛景，”他带着深沉惬意的长叹总结道，“足以告慰我的九泉先祖与灵魂了。”
他顿住了话头：在折磨着他的那股复仇狂热的驱使下，他的面孔显得阴鸷而凶狠。
路易丝·阿代拖着脚步凑到了弟弟身旁。

===Original===

In one hand she held the wooden spoon wherewith she had been stirring the soup: with the other she brushed away the dark, lank hair which hung in strands over her high, pale forehead. In appearance she was a woman immeasurably older than her years. Her face had the colour of yellow parchment, her skin was stretched tightly over her high cheekbones﻿—her lips were colourless and her eyes large, wide-open, were pale in hue and circled with red. Just now a deep frown of puzzlement between her brows added a sinister expression to her cadaverous face:
“The Rat Mort?” she queried in that tired voice of hers, “Cayenne? What is all that about?”
“A splendid scheme of Carrier’s, my Louise,” replied Martin-Roget airily. “We convey the Kernogan woman to the Rat Mort. Tonight a descent will be made on that tavern of ill-fame by a company of Marats and every man, woman and child within it will be arrested and sent to Paris as undesirable inhabitants of this most moral city: in Paris they will be tried as malefactors or evildoers﻿—cutthroats, thieves, what? and deported as convicts to Cayenne, or else sent to the guillotine. The Kernogans among that herd! What sayest thou to that, little sister? Thy father, thy lover, hung as thieves! M. le Duc and Mademoiselle branded as convicts! ’Tis pleasant to think on, eh?”
Louise made no reply. She stood looking at her brother, her pale, red-rimmed eyes seemed to drink in every word that he uttered, while her bony hand wandered mechanically across and across her forehead as if in a pathetic endeavour to clear the brain from everything save of the satisfying thoughts which this prospect of revenge had engendered.
Chauvelin’s gentle voice broke in on her meditations.
“In the meanwhile,” he said placidly, “remember my warning, citizen Martin-Roget. There are passing clever and mighty agencies at work, even at this hour, to wrest your prey from you. How will you convey the wench to the Rat Mort? Carrier has warned you of spies﻿—but I have warned you against a crowd of English adventurers far more dangerous than an army of spies. Three pairs of eyes﻿—probably more, and one pair the keenest in Europe﻿—will be on the watch to seize upon the woman and to carry her off under your very nose.”
Martin-Roget uttered a savage oath.
“That brute Carrier has left me in the lurch,” he said roughly.

===Chinese===

在这一只手里她拿着方才用来搅汤的木勺，另一只手把垂落在苍白高额头上那缕缕又黑又直的头发撩到脑后。从外貌上看，她显得比实际年龄衰老得不可思议。她的面皮泛着黄羊皮纸般枯槁的色泽，紧绷在突出的颧骨上——嘴唇毫无血色，一双睁得大大的眼睛眼眶通红、眼珠色淡。此刻，双眉间一道困惑紧锁的深沟，更给她那形同僵尸的面孔平添了几分阴森怖人之色：
“‘死耗子’酒馆？”她用疲惫不堪的嗓音追问道，“卡宴？这到底是怎么一回事？”
“这是卡里耶想出的一记绝妙好计，我的好路易丝，”马丹-罗热满不在乎地答道，“咱们把凯尔诺冈家的小娘们弄到‘死耗子’去。今晚‘马拉连’将突袭清剿那座风月黑窝，把里面的所有男女老幼统统抓获，以危害这座模范风化之城的害群之马的罪名发配巴黎受审；在巴黎，他们将按恶徒与罪犯——凶手、窃贼之类的货色过堂，然后作为苦役犯流放卡宴，要么就直接送上断头台。凯尔诺冈家也混杂在这群牲口里头！你说怎么样，妹子？当年咱爹和你的情郎被当作盗贼活活吊死！如今公爵老爷和他的千金小姐却要被当面烙上苦役印记！光是想想就痛快，对吧？”
路易丝未置可否。她定定地看着弟弟，那双泛红失神的眼睛仿佛将他吐露的每一个字眼都贪婪地吞了下去，骨瘦如柴的手机械地一次又一次在额头上摩挲着，仿佛徒劳地试图清空大脑，唯独留下这幅大仇得报图景所带来的无上快慰。
肖维兰温和的嗓音打断了她的沉思。
“在此期间，”他心平气和地说道，“切莫忘了我的告诫，马丹-罗热公民。哪怕在此时此刻，正有一股机敏绝顶且手眼通天的神秘势力在暗中运作，企图将你的猎物从掌心中夺走。你究竟打算如何把那小妞弄进‘死耗子’？卡里耶曾警告你小心密探——但我警示你要提防的，却是一帮远比大批密探危险百倍的英国冒险家。三双眼睛——或许还不止，其中一双更是全欧洲最锐利的眼睛——正虎视眈眈窥伺着，随时准备当着你的面将那女子劫走。”
马丹-罗热恶狠狠地骂了一句。
“卡里耶那个畜生在关键时刻把我撂下了，”他没好气地说道。

===Original===

“I don’t believe in your nightmares and your English adventurers, still it would have been better if I could have had the woman conveyed to the tavern under armed escort.”
“Armed escort has been denied you, and anyway it would not be much use. You and I, citizen Martin-Roget, must act independently of Carrier. Your friends down there,” he added, indicating the street with a jerk of the head, “must redouble their watchfulness. The village lads of Vertou are of a truth no match intellectually with our English adventurers, but they have vigorous fists in case there is an attack on the wench while she walks across to the Rat Mort.”
“It would be simpler,” here interposed Louise roughly, “if we were to knock the wench on the head and then let the lads carry her across.”
“It would not be simpler,” retorted Chauvelin drily, “for Carrier might at any moment turn against us. Commandant Fleury with half a company of Marats will be posted round the Rat Mort, remember. They may interfere with the lads and arrest them and snatch the wench from us, when all our plans may fall to the ground﻿ ﻿… one never knows what double game Carrier may be playing. No! no! the girl must not be dragged or carried to the Rat Mort. She must walk into the trap of her own free will.”
“But name of a dog! how is it to be done?” ejaculated Martin-Roget, and he brought his clenched fist crashing down upon the table. “The woman will not follow me﻿—or Louise either﻿—anywhere willingly.”
“She must follow a stranger then﻿—or one whom she thinks a stranger﻿—someone who will have gained her confidence.﻿ ﻿…”
“Impossible.”
“Oh! nothing is impossible, citizen,” rejoined Chauvelin blandly.
“Do you know a way then?” queried the other with a sneer.
“I think I do. If you will trust me that is﻿—”
“I don’t know that I do. Your mind is so intent on those English adventurers, you are like as not to let the aristos slip through your fingers.”
“Well, citizen,” retorted Chauvelin imperturbably, “will you take the risk of conveying the fair Yvonne to the Rat Mort by twelve o’clock tonight? I have very many things to see to, I confess that I should be glad if you will ease me from that responsibility.”
“I have already told you that I see no way,” retorted Martin-Roget with a snarl.
“Then why not let me act?”
“What are you going to do?”

===Chinese===

“我虽不信你那些梦魇和英国冒险家的鬼话，但要是有武装卫队护送那女人去小酒馆，终归是要保险得多。”
“武装护卫既然已经指望不上，而且说实话也没多大用处。马丹-罗热公民，你我必须撇开卡里耶自行设法。下头你的那些弟兄，”他朝着街巷一甩头补充道，“必须倍加警惕。韦尔图村的小伙子们在脑筋上固然斗不过咱们那些英国冒险家，但要是那小妞在走去‘死耗子’的路上遭遇突袭，他们那双硬拳头倒能派上用场。”
“干脆一棒子敲晕那小贱人的脑袋，让小伙子们直接把她抬过去，岂不是更省事，”路易丝粗暴地插话道。
“绝不会更省事，”肖维兰干巴巴地反驳道，“因为卡里耶随时可能翻脸不认人。别忘了，弗勒里指挥官将率领半个连的‘马拉连’埋伏在‘死耗子’四周。他们可能会插手盘查小伙子们，把他们当场扣押，再把那小妞从我们手中夺走，届时咱们的全盘算盘都将毁于一旦……谁也料不准卡里耶背后在玩什么两面三刀的把戏。绝不行！那姑娘绝不能被硬拖或者硬抬去‘死耗子’。她必须自愿踏进这个圈套。”
“可见鬼了！这该怎么办成？”马丹-罗热失声叫嚷起来，紧握的铁拳重重砸在桌面上，“那女人绝不可能自愿跟着我——或者跟着路易丝——去任何地方。”
“那就必须让她跟着一个陌生人走——或者跟着一个她自以为是生面孔的人走——一个能博取她完全信任的人……”
“绝无可能。”
“哈！世上没有什么是不可能的，公民，”肖维兰温和地答道。
“难道你有锦囊妙计？”对方冷笑着质问道。
“我想我确实有。前提是你得信任我才行——”
“我可吃不准信不信得过你。你的全副心思都扑在那些英国冒险家身上，闹不好会让那些贵族佬从你指缝里溜之大吉。”
“那好吧，公民，”肖维兰不怒反笑道，“你敢不敢担下在今夜十二点前把美丽的伊冯娜弄进‘死耗子’的风险？我手头还有大把紧要差事要办，坦白讲，如果你能替我分担这份重担，我倒求之不得。”
“我刚才不是说过了吗，我根本想不出半点辙来，”马丹-罗热没好气地吼道。
“那为何不放手让我去操办？”
“你究竟打算怎么干？”

===Original===

“For the moment I am going for a walk on the quay and once more will commune with the Northwest wind.”
“Tshaw!” ejaculated Martin-Roget savagely.
“Nay, citizen,” resumed Chauvelin blandly, “the winds of heaven are excellent counsellors. I told you so just now and you agreed with me. They blow away the cobwebs of the mind and clear the brain for serious thinking. You want the Kernogan girl to be arrested inside the Rat Mort and you see no way of conveying her thither save by the use of violence, which for obvious reasons is to be deprecated: Carrier, for equally obvious reasons, will not have her taken to the place by force. On the other hand you admit that the wench would not follow you willingly﻿—Well, citizen, we must find a way out of that impasse, for it is too unimportant an one to stand in the way of our plans: for this I must hold a consultation with the Northwest wind.”
“I won’t allow you to do anything without consulting me.”
“Am I likely to do that? To begin with I shall have need of your cooperation and that of the citizeness.”
“In that case﻿ ﻿…” muttered Martin-Roget grudgingly. “But remember,” he added with a return to his usual self-assured manner, “remember that Yvonne and her father belong to me and not to you. I brought them into Nantes for mine own purposes﻿—not for yours. I will not have my revenge jeopardised so that your schemes may be furthered.”
“Who spoke of my schemes, citizen Martin-Roget?” broke in Chauvelin with perfect urbanity. “Surely not I? What am I but an humble tool in the service of the Republic?﻿ ﻿… a tool that has proved useless﻿—a failure, what? My only desire is to help you to the best of my abilities. Your enemies are the enemies of the Republic: my ambition is to help you in destroying them.”
For a moment longer Martin-Roget hesitated: he abominated this suggestion of becoming a mere instrument in the hands of this man whom he still would have affected to despise﻿—had he dared. But here came the difficulty: he no longer dared to despise Chauvelin. He felt the strength of the man﻿—the clearness of his intellect, and though he﻿—Martin-Roget﻿—still chose to disregard every warning in connection with the English spies, he could not wholly divest his mind from the possibility of their presence in Nantes.

===Chinese===

“眼下嘛，我打算去码头上散散步，再次领略领略西北风的指引。”
“啐！”马丹-罗热恶狠狠地啐道。
“非也，公民，”肖维兰温文尔雅地继续说道，“天上的长风向来是极好的参谋。刚才我就对你讲过，当时你也首肯认同。它们能吹散心头的纷扰乱麻，让头脑清醒以便深思熟虑。你一心想让凯尔诺冈家的小妞在‘死耗子’被捕，除了诉诸暴力却别无他法，而显而易见，诉诸暴力极不可取；卡里耶同样出于显而易见的原因，绝不容许硬把她押过去。另一方面，你也承认那小娘们绝不会心甘情愿跟着你走——好嘛，公民，咱们必须打破这个僵局，毕竟区区小事绝不配阻碍咱们的大计：为此，我必须同西北风好好商讨一番。”
“我不准你背着我自作主张。”
“我难道会干那种事吗？首先，我还需要你与女公民的全力配合呢。”
“若是那样的话……”马丹-罗热勉强嘟囔道。“但给我记着，”他又恢复了惯常那副趾高气扬的架势补充道，“记着，伊冯娜和她老子归我管，不归你管。我把他们弄进南特是为了达成我自个儿的目的——不是为你张目。我绝不容许为了成全你的算盘而坏了我的复仇大计。”
“谁提过我的算盘了，马丹-罗热公民？”肖维兰以无可挑剔的儒雅姿态打断道，“反正不是我。在下不过是为共和国效犬马之劳的一枚微不足道的棋子罢了……一枚早已证明毫无用处的废棋——一个丧家之犬，不是吗？我唯一的夙愿就是竭尽绵薄之力助你一臂之力。你的仇敌就是共和国的死敌，我的心愿就是帮你将他们彻底铲除。”
马丹-罗热又迟疑了片刻：他极度反感自己沦为眼前这个男人的掌中傀儡——若换作以往，他本该毫不掩饰地蔑视这个家伙，只要他敢的话。可难处恰恰在于：他如今再也不敢小觑肖维兰了。他深切感受到了这个男人的可怕力量与清晰敏锐的头脑；尽管马丹-罗热嘴上仍对关于英国间谍的警告不屑一顾，心底却再也无法完全抹杀他们潜伏在南特城内的可能性。

===Original===

Carrier’s scheme was so magnificent, so satisfying, that the ex-miller’s son was ready to humble his pride and set his arrogance aside in order to see it carried through successfully.
So after a moment or two, despite the fact that he positively ached to shut Chauvelin out of the whole business, Martin-Roget gave a grudging assent to his proposal.
“Very well!” he said, “you see to it. So long as it does not interfere with my plans.﻿ ﻿…”
“It can but help them,” rejoined Chauvelin suavely. “If you will act as I shall direct I pledge you my word that the wench will walk to the Rat Mort of her free will and at the hour when you want her. What else is there to say?”
“When and where shall we meet again?”
“Within the hour I will return here and explain to you and to the citizeness what I want you to do. We will get the aristos inside the Rat Mort, never fear; and after that I think that we may safely leave Carrier to do the rest, what?”
He picked up his hat and wrapped his mantle round him. He took no further heed of Martin-Roget or of Louise, for suddenly he had felt the crackling of crisp paper inside the breast-pocket of his coat and in a moment the spirit of the man had gone a-roaming out of the narrow confines of this squalid abode. It had crossed the English Channel and wandered once more into a brilliantly-lighted ballroom where an exquisitely dressed dandy declaimed inanities and doggrel rhymes for the delectation of a flippant assembly: it heard once more the lazy, drawling speech, the inane, affected laugh, it caught the glance of a pair of lazy, grey eyes fixed mockingly upon him. Chauvelin’s thin claw-like hand went back to his pocket: it felt that packet of papers, it closed over it like a vulture’s talon does upon a prey. He no longer heard Martin-Roget’s obstinate murmurings, he no longer felt himself to be the disgraced, humiliated servant of the State: rather did he feel once more the master, the leader, the successful weaver of an hundred clever intrigues. The enemy who had baffled him so often had chosen once more to throw down the glove of mocking defiance. So be it! The battle would be fought this night﻿—a decisive one﻿—and long live the Republic and the power of the people!

===Chinese===

卡里耶的计策如此宏大诱人、令人心满意足，以至于昔日磨坊主的儿子甘愿放下一身傲气与自尊，只求能让这桩大计顺顺当当付诸实现。
因此略一思忖，尽管马丹-罗热打心眼里巴不得将肖维兰彻底踢出局，最终还是勉强点头应允了他的提议。
“那好嘛！”他说道，“你去办吧。只要不碍着我的事就行……”
“这只会对你的计划大有裨益，”肖维兰圆滑地附和道，“只要你按我的吩咐行事，我敢向你打包票，那小妞定会在你指定的时辰心甘情愿地自行走入‘死耗子’。还有什么可说的？”
“咱们何时何地再碰头？”
“一个时辰之内我自会折返回来，向你和女公民交代你们的具体任务。咱们定能把那些贵族佬弄进‘死耗子’里头，放一百个心吧；至于剩下的事，我想大可安安心心交给卡里耶去发落了，你说呢？”
他拿起帽子，紧了紧身上的斗篷。他再也懒得理会马丹-罗热或是路易丝，因为大衣胸袋里硬脆纸张的沙沙摩擦声猝然触动了他的神经，顷刻间，他的神思早已飞离了这间狭窄破败的陋室。它越过了英吉利海峡，再次漫游进那座灯火辉煌的舞厅中，在那里，一位身着华服的花花公子正为了取悦轻浮的宾客而吟诵着荒唐的打油诗与无聊俏皮话；他再次听到了那慵懒拖沓的腔调、那做作傻气的笑声，并捕捉到了一双半睁半闭的灰色眼眸正带着嘲弄的笑意凝视着自己。肖维兰干瘦如鹰爪般的手再次探入口袋，触碰着那叠文书，像秃鹫的利爪扑抓猎物般将其死死扣在掌中。他再也听不见马丹-罗热固执的嘀咕，再也不觉得自己是一个蒙受奇耻大辱、失势落魄的公家差役；相反，他再次感觉自己成了呼风唤雨的统帅、运筹帷幄的领袖以及编织过无数机巧阴谋的得胜元勋。那个曾屡次将他玩弄于股掌之间的宿敌，今夜竟再次挑衅般向他掷下了战书。那就来吧！今夜必将有一场殊死决战——一场一决雌雄的决战——共和国万岁，人民的伟力万岁！

===Original===

With a curt nod of the head Chauvelin turned on his heel and without waiting for Martin-Roget to follow him, or for Louise to light him on his way, he strode from the room, and out of the house, and had soon disappeared in the darkness in the direction of the quay.

===Chinese===

肖维兰短促地点了点头便猛然转身，不等马丹-罗热跟上，也不等路易丝替他掌灯引路，便大步流星跨出了房门，走出宅邸，转瞬便没入了通向码头的深沉暮色之中。

#### V / 第五节
===Original===

Once more free from the encumbering companionship of Martin-Roget, Chauvelin felt free to breathe and to think. He, the obscure and impassive servant of the Republic, the cold-blooded Terrorist who had gone through every phrase of an exciting career without moving a muscle of his grave countenance, felt as if every one of his arteries was on fire. He strode along the quay in the teeth of the northwesterly wind, grateful for the cold blast which lashed his face and cooled his throbbing temples.
The packet of papers inside his coat seemed to sear his breast.
Before turning to go along the quay he paused, hesitating for a moment what he would do. His very humble lodgings were at the far end of the town, and every minute of time was precious. Inside Le Bouffay, where he had a small room allotted to him as a minor representative in Nantes of the Committee of Public Safety, there was the ever present danger of prying eyes.
On the whole﻿—since time was so precious﻿—he decided on returning to Le Bouffay. The concierge and the clerk fortunately let him through without those official delays which he﻿—Chauvelin﻿—was wont to find so galling ever since his disgrace had put a bar against the opening of every door at the bare mention of his name or the display of his tricolour scarf.
He strode rapidly across the hall: the men on guard eyed him with lazy indifference as he passed. Once inside his own sanctum he looked carefully around him; he drew the curtain closer across the window and dragged the table and a chair well away from the range which might be covered by an eye at the keyhole. It was only when he had thoroughly assured himself that no searching eye or inquisitive ear could possibly be watching over him that he at last drew the precious packet of papers from his pocket. He undid the red ribbon which held it together and spread the papers out on the table before him. Then he examined them carefully one by one.
As he did so an exclamation of wrath or of impatience escaped him from time to time, once he laughed﻿—involuntarily﻿—aloud.
The examination of the papers took him some time. When he had finished he gathered them all together again, retied the bit of ribbon round them and slipped the packet back into the pocket of his coat. There was a look of grim determination on his face, even though a bitter sigh escaped his set lips.
“Oh!

===Chinese===

摆脱了马丹-罗热那令人窒息的纠缠，肖维兰终于得以畅快地呼吸与沉思。他，这个默默无闻、喜怒不形于色的共和国仆从，这个经历过惊心动魄生涯的每一幕而面部肌肉不曾抽动一丝的冷酷恐怖主义者，此刻只觉得浑身的每一条血管都在熊熊燃烧。他顶着凛冽的西北风大步沿着码头疾行，暗自庆幸这道抽打在面颊上的冷冽狂风能冷却他那剧烈跳动的太阳穴。
怀中大衣里的那叠文书仿佛灼烧着他的胸膛。
在折向码头前他驻足片刻，略一迟疑自己究竟该去往何处。他那简陋至极的下榻处位于城郊偏远的一角，而此刻分秒必争。而在布费城楼内部，尽管作为救国委员会在南特的下级特派员他分得了一间狭小的公事房，却无时无刻不面临着隔墙有耳、遭人窥探的风险。
总的权衡下来——鉴于时间紧迫——他还是决意折返布费城楼。所幸门房与书记员放他通行时并未多加盘查刁难；自从失势以后，单凭他的姓名或三色领巾再也无法像往常那样令每一扇大门应声而开，这曾让他深感屈辱难堪。
他健步流星穿过前厅，值守的卫兵带着懒散漠然的神色目送他走过。一跨进自己的公事房，他便警惕地环视四周；他将窗帘拉得更严实了些，又把桌椅挪开，远远避开了从钥匙孔可能窥探到的视线范围。直到确信绝无任何窥伺的目光或偷听的耳朵在暗中窥探，他才终于从口袋里掏出了那包珍贵至极的文书。他解开系着纸卷的红丝带，将文书一一平摊在眼前的桌面上。随后，他神情凝重地逐一仔细审视起来。
审阅期间，不耐烦与愤懑的咒骂不时从他齿缝间逸出，有一次他甚至情不自禁地放声大笑起来。
仔细研读这叠文书耗费了他颇多工夫。等他全部看完，便将纸张重新整整齐齐拢好，用红丝带重新系紧，塞回了大衣口袋。尽管抿紧的双唇间逸出一声苦涩的叹息，他的脸上却泛起了一抹坚毅决绝的冷峻之色。
“啊！

===Original===

for the power,” he muttered to himself, “which I had a year ago! for the power to deal with mine enemy myself. So you have come to Nantes, my valiant Sir Percy Blakeney?” he added while a short, sardonic laugh escaped his thin, set lips: “and you are determined that I shall know how and why you came! Do you reckon, I wonder, that I have no longer the power to deal with you? Well!﻿ ﻿…”
He sighed again but with more satisfaction this time.
“Well!﻿ ﻿…” he reiterated with obvious complacency. “Unless that oaf Carrier is a bigger fool than I imagine him to be I think I have you this time, my elusive Scarlet Pimpernel.”

===Chinese===

要是能重新拥有，”他自言自语地喃喃道，“一年前我所掌握的无上权势该有多好！好让我能亲手处置我的生死宿敌。这么说，你真跑到南特来了，我那英勇盖世的珀西·布莱克尼爵士？”紧抿的双唇间漏出一声短促而讥诮的冷笑，他接着说道：“而且你偏偏要让我弄明白你是如何来的、为何而来的！我倒想知道，你真以为我如今再也无力收拾你了吗？哼！……”
他再次叹了口气，然而这一回却透着更多的快意与自得。
“哼！……”他带着显而易见的自鸣得意再次重复道，“除非那个笨汉卡里耶比我想象的还要蠢笨，否则这一回我看你往哪里逃，我那神出鬼没的红花侠。”

## IV The Net / 第四章 罗网
===Original===



===Chinese===

第四章 罗网

#### I / 第一节
===Original===

It was not an easy thing to obtain an audience of the great proconsul at this hour of the night, nor was Chauvelin, the disgraced servant of the Committee of Public Safety, a man to be considered. Carrier, with his love of ostentation and of tyranny, found great delight in keeping his colleagues waiting upon his pleasure, and he knew that he could trust young Jacques Lalouët to be as insolent as any tyrant’s flunkey of yore.
“I must speak with the proconsul at once,” had been Chauvelin’s urgent request of Fleury, the commandant of the great man’s bodyguard.
“The proconsul dines at this hour,” had been Fleury’s curt reply.
“ ’Tis a matter which concerns the welfare and the safety of the State!”
“The proconsul’s health is the concern of the State too, and he dines at this hour and must not be disturbed.”
“Commandant Fleury!” urged Chauvelin, “you risk being implicated in a disaster. Danger and disgrace threaten the proconsul and all his adherents. I must speak with citizen Carrier at once.”
Fortunately for Chauvelin there were two keys which, when all else failed, were apt to open the doors of Carrier’s stronghold: the key of fear and that of cupidity. He tried both and succeeded. He bribed and he threatened: he endured Fleury’s brutality and Lalouët’s impertinence but he got his way. After an hour’s weary waiting and ceaseless parleyings he was once more ushered into the antechamber where he had sat earlier in the day. The doors leading to the inner sanctuary were open. Young Jacques Lalouët stood by them on guard. Carrier, fuming and raging at having been disturbed, vented his spleen and ill-temper on Chauvelin.
“If the news that you bring me is not worth my consideration,” he cried savagely, “I’ll send you to moulder in Le Bouffay or to drink the waters of the Loire.”
Chauvelin silent, self-effaced, allowed the flood of the great man’s wrath to spend itself in threats. Then he said quietly:
“Citizen proconsul I have come to tell you that the English spy, who is called the Scarlet Pimpernel, is now in Nantes. There is a reward of twenty thousand francs for his capture and I want your help to lay him by the heels.”
Carrier suddenly paused in his ravings. He sank into a chair and a livid hue spread over his face.
“It’s not true!” he murmured hoarsely.
“I saw him﻿—not an hour ago.﻿ ﻿…”
“What proof have you?”

===Chinese===

在这夜深时分，要想获得这位显赫总督的接见绝非易事；况且肖维兰作为救国委员会失宠失势的仆役，更是不值一提的人物。卡里耶生性好大喜功、暴虐成性，最乐于让同僚听凭他的喜怒枯候，而且他深知年轻的雅克·拉卢埃定会像昔日暴君身边的狗腿奴才一样傲慢无礼，这一点尽可放心。

“我必须立刻面见总督，”这是肖维兰向大人物卫队指挥官弗勒里提出的紧急请求。

“总督此刻正在用膳，”弗勒里生硬冷淡地回绝。

“此事关乎国家的安危与福祉！”

“总督的贵体同样关乎国家安危，他正在用膳，不得惊扰。”

“弗勒里指挥官！”肖维兰催促道，“你这是在冒大祸临头的危险。危险与耻辱正威胁着总督和他所有的追随者。我必须立刻与卡里耶公民面谈。”

对肖维兰而言万幸的是，在其他手段统统失灵之时，尚有两把钥匙足以开启卡里耶的老巢大门：一把是恐惧，另一把是贪婪。他双管齐下，果然奏效。他既行贿又恐吓；既忍受了弗勒里的粗暴野蛮，也容忍了拉卢埃的目中无人，但终究达成了目的。在焦灼地枯候了一个小时、历经了没完没了的交涉周旋之后，他再次被领进了白天曾坐过的那间前厅。通往内室密室的大门敞开着。年轻的雅克·拉卢埃守在门边戒备。卡里耶因被打扰而怒火中烧、暴跳如雷，把满腔的怨气与恶劣脾气全发泄在肖维兰身上。

“要是你带来的消息不值一听，”他凶狠地咆哮道，“我就把你关进布费城楼发霉烂掉，或是送你去喝卢瓦尔河的浑水。”

肖维兰沉默无言，神情谦卑，任凭这位大人物如潮的怒火在恐吓中宣泄殆尽。随后他平静地说道：

“总督公民，我是来告诉你，那个被称为‘红花侠’的英国间谍如今就在南特。悬赏捉拿他的赏金足有两万法郎，我需要你的协助将他缉捕归案。”

卡里耶的狂怒骤然止住。他瘫坐在椅子上，一张脸泛起了惨白死色。

“这不可能！”他声音嘶哑地喃喃道。

“我亲眼见到了他——就在不到一个小时前……”

“你有什么证据？”

===Original===

“I’ll show them to you﻿—but not across this threshold. Let me enter, citizen proconsul, and close your sanctuary doors behind me rather than before. What I have come hither to tell you, can only be said between four walls.”
“I’ll make you tell me,” broke in Carrier in a raucous voice, which excitement and fear caused almost to choke in his throat. “I’ll make you﻿ ﻿… curse you for the traitor that you are.﻿ ﻿… Curse you!” he cried more vigorously, “I’ll make you speak. Will you shield a spy by your silence, you miserable traitor? If you do I’ll send you to rot in the mud of the Loire with other traitors less accursed than yourself.”
“If you only knew,” was Chauvelin’s calm rejoinder to the other’s ravings, “how little I care for life. I only live to be even one day with an enemy whom I hate. That enemy is now in Nantes, but I am like a bird of prey whose wings have been clipped. If you do not help me mine enemy will again go free﻿—and death in that case matters little or nothing to me.”
For a moment longer Carrier hesitated. Fear had gripped him by the throat. Chauvelin’s earnestness seemed to vouch for the truth of his assertion, and if this were so﻿—if those English spies were indeed in Nantes﻿—then his own life was in deadly danger. He﻿—like every one of those bloodthirsty tyrants who had misused the sacred names of Fraternity and of Equality﻿—had learned to dread the machinations of those mysterious Englishmen and of their unconquerable leader. Popular superstition had it that they were spies of the English Government and that they were not only bent on saving traitors from well-merited punishment but that they were hired assassins paid by Mr. Pitt to murder every faithful servant of the Republic. The name of the Scarlet Pimpernel, so significantly uttered by Chauvelin, had turned Carrier’s sallow cheeks to a livid hue. Sick with terror now he called Lalouët to him. He clung to the boy with both arms as to the one being in this world whom he trusted.
“What shall we do, Jacques?” he murmured hoarsely, “shall we let him in?”
The boy roughly shook himself free from the embrace of the great proconsul.
“If you want twenty thousand francs,” he said with a dry laugh, “I should listen quietly to what citizen Chauvelin has to say.”
Terror and rapacity were ranged on one side against inordinate vanity.

===Chinese===

“我会拿给你看的——但不能隔着门槛展示。让我进去吧，总督公民，把你的密室大门合拢在我身后，而不是挡在我面前。我此番前来告知你的事情，只能在密室四壁之内讲。”

“我会逼你招出来的，”卡里耶粗哑着嗓子打断道，激动与恐惧几乎卡住了他的喉咙。“我会逼你……该死的叛徒，诅咒你！……诅咒你！”他越发歇斯底里地吼道，“我会逼你开口。你这个可怜的叛徒，难道还想用沉默庇护间谍吗？要是你敢这么做，我就把你扔进卢瓦尔河的烂泥里去腐烂，跟那些罪孽不如你深重的叛徒作伴！”

“你要是知道，”面对对方的胡言乱语，肖维兰平静地反驳道，“我是多么不在乎自己的性命。我活着，只为了有朝一日能跟那个我切齿痛恨的死敌算清旧账。那个敌人如今就在南特，但我此刻却如同一只被剪去双翼的猛禽。若没有你的协助，我的死敌又将逍遥法外——倘若如此，死对我而言便毫无意义，不足挂齿。”

卡里耶迟疑了片刻。恐惧扼住了他的喉咙。肖维兰那严肃恳切的模样，似乎足以印证其言非虚；倘若真是如此——倘若那些英国间谍真在南特——那么他自己的性命便危在旦夕。他——正如每一个假借“博爱”与“平等”神圣之名作恶的嗜血暴君一样——早已学会了畏惧那些神秘英国人及其不可战胜的首领的阴谋手段。坊间迷信传言，他们是英国政府的密探，不仅一心要从罪有应得的惩处下营救叛徒，更是小皮特先生花重金雇来的刺客，专门谋杀共和国的每一位忠实仆人。“红花侠”这个名字经肖维兰意味深长地说出，令卡里耶蜡黄的面颊褪成了惨青色。此刻他被吓得魂不附体，叫来了拉卢埃。他双臂死死抓着那少年，仿佛那是世上他唯一信赖的人。

“我们该怎么办，雅克？”他沙哑地呢喃着，“我们该让他进来吗？”

少年粗鲁地甩开了显赫总督的搂抱。

“要是你想赚两万法郎，”他干笑一声说道，“我看就该安安静静听听肖维兰公民怎么说。”

恐惧与贪婪并肩站在了一侧，与那膨胀无度的虚荣心抗衡。

===Original===

The thought of twenty thousand francs made Carrier’s ugly mouth water. Money was ever scarce these days: also the fear of assassination was a spectre which haunted him at all hours of the day and night. On the other hand he positively worshipped the mystery wherewith he surrounded himself. It had been his boast for some time now that no one save the chosen few had crossed the threshold of his private chamber: and he was miserably afraid not only of Chauvelin’s possible evil intentions, but also that this despicable ex-aristo and equally despicable failure would boast in the future of an ascendancy over him.
He thought the matter over for fully five minutes, during which there was dead silence in the two rooms﻿—silence only broken by the stertorous breathing of that wretched coward, and the measured ticking of the fine Buhl clock behind him. Chauvelin’s pale eyes were fixed upon the darkness, through which he could vaguely discern the uncouth figure of the proconsul, sprawling over his desk. Which way would his passions sway him? Chauvelin as he watched and waited felt that his habitual self-control was perhaps more severely taxed at this moment than it had ever been before. Upon the swaying of those passions, the passions of a man infinitely craven and infinitely base, depended all his﻿—Chauvelin’s﻿—hopes of getting even at last with a daring and resourceful foe. Terror and rapacity were the counsellors which ranged themselves on the side of his schemes, but mere vanity and caprice fought a hard battle too.
In the end it was rapacity that gained the victory. An impatient exclamation from young Lalouët roused Carrier from his sombre brooding and hastened on a decision which was destined to have such momentous consequences for the future of both these men.
“Introduce citizen Chauvelin in here, Lalouët,” said the proconsul grudgingly. “I will listen to what he has to say.”

===Chinese===

一想到两万法郎，卡里耶那张丑陋的嘴就直淌口水。如今钱财向来匮乏；况且对刺杀的恐惧更是一道幽灵，日日夜夜纠缠着他。但另一方面，他又对自己营造出的神秘光环崇拜得五体投地。这段时间以来他一直夸口称，除了极少数亲信，谁也不曾跨过他私人起居室的门槛；他既可怜巴巴地提防着肖维兰可能心怀叵测，又唯恐这个卑鄙的前贵族、同样卑鄙的失败者日后会夸口凌驾于他之上。

他前前后后思量了足足五分钟，其间两间屋子里一片死寂——唯有那个可怜懦夫粗重的喘息声，以及他身后那座精致布尔钟有节奏的滴答声打破了寂静。肖维兰淡色的眼眸凝视着黑暗，穿透阴暗，他依稀能辨出总督那瘫在写字台上的粗野身躯。欲望的天平究竟会倾向何方？肖维兰冷眼旁观、静待时机，感到自己惯常的克制力在此刻所经受的考验，或许比以往任何时候都要严峻。这个卑劣至极、懦弱至极的狂徒的心思摆动，决定着他肖维兰能否最终跟那个胆大包天、智计百出的强敌算清总账。恐惧与贪欲是站在他这一边的谋士，但虚荣与反复无常也在激烈交锋。

最终，贪婪占据了上风。年轻的拉卢埃一声不耐烦的嘀咕将卡里耶从阴沉的沉思中唤醒，促使他匆匆作出了决断，而这一决断注定将对这两个人的未来产生无可估量的深远影响。

“把肖维兰公民领进来，拉卢埃，”总督极不情愿地吩咐道，“我倒要听听他有些什么话说。”

#### II / 第二节
===Original===

Chauvelin crossed the threshold of the tyrant’s sanctuary, in no way awed by the majesty of that dreaded presence or confused by the air of mystery which hung about the room.
He did not even bestow a glance on the multitudinous objects of art and the priceless furniture which littered the tiger’s lair. His pale face remained quite expressionless as he bowed solemnly before Carrier and then took the chair which was indicated to him. Young Lalouët fetched a candelabra from the anteroom and carried it into the audience chamber: then he closed the communicating doors. The candelabra he placed on a console-table immediately behind Carrier’s desk and chair, so that the latter’s face remained in complete shadow, whilst the light fell full upon Chauvelin.
“Well! what is it?” queried the proconsul roughly. “What is this story of English spies inside Nantes? How did they get here? Who is responsible for keeping such rabble out of our city? Name of a dog, but someone has been careless of duty! and carelessness these days is closely allied to treason.”
He talked loudly and volubly﻿—his inordinate terror causing the words to come tumbling, almost incoherently, out of his mouth. Finally he turned on Chauvelin with a snarl like an angry cat:
“And how comes it, citizen,” he added savagely, “that you alone here in Nantes are acquainted with the whereabouts of those dangerous spies?”
“I caught sight of them,” rejoined Chauvelin calmly, “this afternoon after I left you. I knew we should have them here, the moment citizen Martin-Roget brought the Kernogans into the city. The woman is the wife of one of them.”
“Curse that blundering fool Martin-Roget for bringing that rabble about our ears, and those assassins inside our gates.”
“Nay! Why should you complain, citizen proconsul,” rejoined Chauvelin in his blandest manner. “Surely you are not going to let the English spies escape this time? And if you succeed in laying them by the heels﻿—there where everyone else has failed﻿—you will have earned twenty thousand francs and the thanks of the entire Committee of Public Safety.”
He paused: and young Lalouët interposed with his impudent laugh:
“Go on, citizen Chauvelin,” he said, “if there is twenty thousand francs to be made out of this game, I’ll warrant that the proconsul will take a hand in it﻿—eh, Carrier?”

===Chinese===

肖维兰跨过了暴君密室的门槛，丝毫没有被那令人胆寒的威严所震慑，也没有被弥漫在室内的神秘氛围所惑。

他甚至未曾看一眼散落在这虎穴之中的无数艺术珍品和价值连城的名贵家具。他苍白的面容依旧毫无表情，庄重地向卡里耶躬身行礼，随后在指给他的椅子上落座。年轻的拉卢埃从前厅取来一具多枝烛台端进会客室，接着关上了隔门。他把烛台放在紧靠卡里耶办公桌与座椅后方的附墙供桌上，如此一来，后者的面孔完全隐没在阴影之中，而光线则径直倾泻在肖维兰身上。

“说吧！究竟怎么回事？”总督粗暴地质问道，“英国间谍潜入南特到底是怎么一回事？他们是怎么溜进来的？是谁负责把这帮下流匪类挡在城外的？见鬼，定是有人玩忽职守！而在当下，玩忽职守就等同于叛国。”

他嗓门极大、滔滔不绝——极度的恐慌让他语无伦次，字句几乎是连滚带爬地从嘴里蹦出来。最后，他像一只被激怒的野猫般龇牙咧嘴地冲着肖维兰咆哮道：

“还有，公民，怎么整座南特城里，”他凶狠地补充道，“偏偏只有你一个人摸得清那帮危险间谍的行踪？”

“今天下午离开你这里之后，”肖维兰平静地答道，“我无意中撞见了他们。早在马丹-罗热公民把凯尔诺冈家带进城的那一刻，我就料定他们必定会赶来此地。那个女人正是他们其中一人的妻子。”

“该死的马丹-罗热，成事不足败事有余的蠢材！居然把这帮下贱东西招引到我们身边，把那些刺客引进了城门。”

“哎！总督公民，你何苦抱怨呢，”肖维兰用最和蔼的语气回应道，“难不成你这次还打算放那些英国间谍溜走不成？倘若你能成功将他们绳之以法——在所有人都铩羽而归的关头——你不仅能斩获两万法郎，更将赢得整个救国委员会的由衷感激。”

他顿住了；年轻的拉卢埃带着放肆的笑声插话道：

“接着说啊，肖维兰公民，”他说道，“要是这盘棋里能捞到两万法郎，我敢打包票，总督大人肯定乐意插上一脚——对吧，卡里耶？”

===Original===

And with the insolent familiarity of a terrier teasing a grizzly he tweaked the great man’s ear.
Chauvelin in the meanwhile had drawn the packet of papers from his pocket and untied the ribbon that held them together. He now spread the papers out on the desk.
“What are these?” queried Carrier.
“A few papers,” replied Chauvelin, “which one of your Marats, Paul Friche by name, picked up in the wake of the Englishmen. I caught sight of them in the far distance, and sent the Marats after them. For awhile Paul Friche kept on their track, but after that they disappeared in the darkness.”
“Who were the senseless louts,” growled Carrier, “who allowed a pack of foreign assassins to escape? I’ll soon make them disappear﻿ ﻿… in the Loire.”
“You will do what you like about that, citizen Carrier,” retorted Chauvelin drily; “in the meanwhile you would do well to examine these papers.”
He sorted these out, examined them one by one, then passed them across to Carrier. Lalouët, impudent and inquisitive, sat on the corner of the desk, dangling his legs. With scant ceremony he snatched one paper after another out of Carrier’s hands and examined them curiously.
“Can you understand all this gibberish?” he asked airily. “Jean Baptiste, my friend, how much English do you know?”
“Not much,” replied the proconsul, “but enough to recognise that abominable doggrel rhyme which has gone the round of the Committees of Public Safety throughout the country.”
“I know it by heart,” rejoined young Lalouët. “I was in Paris once, when citizen Robespierre received a copy of it. Name of a dog!” added the youngster with a coarse laugh, “how he cursed!”
It is doubtful however if citizen Robespierre did on that occasion curse quite so volubly as Carrier did now.
“If I only knew why that *satané* Englishman throws so much calligraphy about,” he said, “I would be easier in my mind. Now this senseless rhyme﻿ ﻿… I don’t see.﻿ ﻿…”
“Its importance?” broke in Chauvelin quietly. “I dare say not. On the face of it, it appears foolish and childish: but it is intended as a taunt and is really a poor attempt at humour. They are a queer people these English. If you knew them as I do, you would not be surprised to see a man scribbling off a cheap joke before embarking on an enterprise which may cost him his head.”
“And this inane rubbish is of that sort,” concluded young Lalouët.

===Chinese===

说着，他带着小猎犬戏弄大灰熊般放肆亲昵的举动，扯了扯这位大人物的耳朵。

此时肖维兰已从口袋里掏出那一叠文件，解开了系扎的丝带。他将那些文件一一摊在写字台上。

“这些是什么？”卡里耶盘问道。

“几页文件，”肖维兰回答，“是你们马拉连里一个名叫保罗·弗利什的弟兄，顺着那几个英国人的踪迹捡到的。我远远瞧见了他们，便派马拉连卫兵追了上去。保罗·弗利什尾随追踪了一阵，但随后他们便隐没在夜色中不见了踪影。”

“是哪个蠢笨如猪的混账，”卡里耶咆哮道，“竟然放跑了一帮外国刺客？我非叫他们也消失不可……消失在卢瓦尔河里。”

“那随你的便，卡里耶公民，”肖维兰冷冷地反驳道，“眼下你最好还是先过目一下这些文件。”

他理了理文件，逐一过目，然后递到了卡里耶面前。傲慢而好奇的拉卢埃坐在桌角，晃荡着两条腿。他毫不客气地接二连三从卡里耶手里夺过一张张纸，好奇地审视着。

“你认得懂这堆鬼画符吗？”他轻浮地问道，“让-巴蒂斯特老兄，你到底懂几句英文？”

“懂得不多，”总督答道，“但足够认出那首传遍全国各地救国委员会的该死打油诗。”

“我早就烂熟于心了，”年轻的拉卢埃搭话道，“有一回我在巴黎，正好赶上罗伯斯庇尔公民收到抄件。见鬼！”少年粗俗地大笑起来，“当时他骂得那叫一个凶！”

不过，当时罗伯斯庇尔公民痛骂的滔滔不绝之势，恐也未必能比得上此刻的卡里耶。

“我要是能弄明白那个该死的英国佬干嘛到处乱撒墨宝，”他说道，“心里还能踏实些。可这首莫名其妙的打油诗……我实在瞧不出……”

“瞧不出要紧之处？”肖维兰平静地打断道，“我想你确实看不出来。表面上看，它既荒唐又幼稚；但这本就是用来嘲弄人的，不过是些拙劣的幽默罢了。英国人是一帮古怪的家伙。要是你像我一样了解他们，就不会奇怪为什么有人在冒着掉脑袋的风险行事之前，还有闲心涂鸦这些下流的笑话。”

“这堆胡言乱语也是那一类货色，”年轻的拉卢埃断言道。

===Original===

And in his thin high treble he began reciting:

> “We seek him here;

We seek him there!

Those Frenchies seek him everywhere.

Is he in heaven?

Is he in h‑ll?

That demmed elusive Pimpernel?”

“Pointless and offensive,” he said as he tossed the paper back on the table.
“A cursed aristo that Englishman of yours,” growled Carrier. “Oh! when I get him.﻿ ﻿…”
He made an expressive gesture which made Lalouët laugh.
“What else have we got in the way of documents, citizen Chauvelin?” he asked.
“There is a letter,” replied the latter.
“Read it,” commanded Carrier. “Or rather translate it as you read. I don’t understand the whole of the gibberish.”
And Chauvelin, taking up a sheet of paper which was covered with neat, minute writing, began to read aloud, translating the English into French as he went along:

> “ ‘Here we are at last, my dear Tony! Didn’t I tell you that we can get in anywhere despite all precautions taken against us!’ ”

“The impudent devils!” broke in Carrier.

> —“ ‘Did you really think that they could keep us out of Nantes while Lady Anthony Dewhurst is a prisoner in their hands?’ ”

“Who is that?”
“The Kernogan woman. As I told you just now, she is married to an Englishman who is named Dewhurst and who is one of the members of that thrice cursed League.”
Then he continued to read:

> “ ‘And did you really suppose that they would spot half a dozen English gentlemen in the guise of peat-gatherers, returning at dusk and covered with grime from their work? Not like, friend Tony! Not like! If you happen to meet mine engaging friend M. Chambertin before I have that privilege myself, tell him I pray you, with my regards, that I am looking forward to the pleasure of making a long nose at him once more. Calais, Boulogne, Paris﻿—now Nantes﻿—the scenes of his triumphs multiply exceedingly.’ ”

===Chinese===

随即，他扯着尖细高亢的嗓音念诵起来：

> “我们在此处寻他；
我们在彼处寻他！
法国佬到处把他找寻。
他是在天堂？
还是在地狱？
那该死的、神出鬼没的红花侠？”

“莫名其妙又令人作呕，”他说着把纸片掷回桌上。
“你说的那个英国佬真是个该死的贵族，”卡里耶低吼道，“哼！等我逮着他……”
他做了一个意味深长的手势，引得拉卢埃哈哈大笑。
“咱们手里还有什么别的文件吗，肖维兰公民？”他问道。
“还有一封信，”后者答道。
“念念看，”卡里耶命令道，“或者边念边翻译。那一整套鸟语我一个字也看不懂。”
肖维兰拿起一张写满工整纤细字迹的信纸，开始朗声宣读，一边读一边将英文翻译成法文：

> “‘我们终于到了，我亲爱的托尼！我不是早就跟你说过，任凭防范多么森严，我们也能来去自如！’”

“这帮无法无天的恶鬼！”卡里耶插话骂道。

> ——“‘安东尼·迪尤赫斯特勋爵夫人既已落入他们魔掌，你难道真以为他们能把我们挡在南特城外？’”

“那是谁？”
“就是凯尔诺冈家的女人。方才我已对你说过，她嫁给了一个名叫迪尤赫斯特的英国人，那人正是那该死的同盟里的一员。”
随后他继续往下念：

> “‘你难道当真以为，他们会识破半打乔装成泥炭采掘工的英国绅士吗？他们在黄昏时分归来，劳作归来满面尘垢。不可能的，托尼老兄！绝不可能！假若你在我之前有幸撞见我那惹人怜爱的老友肖贝尔坦先生，烦请代我向他致意，就说我极其盼望能再次当面向他做鬼脸。加来、布洛涅、巴黎——如今又到了南特——他大显身手的大捷之地可真是与日俱增啊。’”

===Original===

“What in the devil’s name does all this mean?” queried Carrier with an oath.
“You don’t understand it?” rejoined Chauvelin quietly.
“No. I do not.”
“Yet I translated quite clearly.”
“It is not the language that puzzles me. The contents seem to me such drivel. The man wants secrecy, what? He is supposed to be astute, resourceful, above all mysterious and enigmatic. Yet he writes to his friend﻿—matter of no importance between them, recollections of the past, known to them both﻿—and threats for the future, equally futile and senseless. I cannot reconcile it all. It puzzles me.”
“And it would puzzle me,” rejoined Chauvelin, while the ghost of a smile curled his thin lips, “did I not know the man. Futile? Senseless, you say? Well, he does futile and senseless things one moment and amazing deeds of personal bravery and of astuteness the next. He is three parts a braggart too. He wanted you, me﻿—all of us to know how he and his followers succeeded in eluding our vigilance and entered our closely-guarded city in the guise of grimy peat-gatherers. Now I come to think of it, it was easy enough for them to do that. Those peat-gatherers who live inside the city boundaries return from their work as the night falls in. Those cursed English adventurers are passing clever at disguise﻿—they are born mountebanks the lot of them. Money and impudence they have in plenty. They could easily borrow or purchase some filthy rags from the cottages on the dunes, then mix with the crowd on its return to the city. I dare say it was cleverly done. That Scarlet Pimpernel is just a clever adventurer and nothing more. So far his marvellous good luck has carried him through. Now we shall see.”
Carrier had listened in silence. Something of his colleague’s calm had by this time communicated itself to him too. He was no longer raving like an infuriated bull﻿—his terror no longer made a half-cringing, wholly savage brute of him. He was sprawling across the desk﻿—his arms folded, his deep-set eyes studying closely the well-nigh inscrutable face of Chauvelin. Young Lalouët too had lost something of his impudence.

===Chinese===

“这鬼话到底是什么意思？”卡里耶骂骂咧咧地质问道。

“你听不明白？”肖维兰平静地反问。

“听不明白，一点也不明白。”

“可我的翻译已经足够清楚了。”

“把我搞糊涂的不是语言。信上的内容在我看来纯属胡言乱语。这家伙明明要保密，不是吗？人们都说他机敏狡黠、诡计多端，最关键的是神秘莫测。可他写给朋友的信——尽是些鸡毛蒜皮的琐事，两人都心知肚明的昔日回忆——外加对未来的恐吓威胁，同样徒劳无益、不知所云。我实在想不通。这简直让我莫名其妙。”

“若非我深知此人的底细，”肖维兰嘴角泛起一丝若隐若现的冷笑，“我也会和你一样莫名其妙。徒劳？不知所云？哼，他上一刻还在做些徒劳无益、不可理喻的蠢事，下一刻就能干出令人咋舌的勇武壮举与狡黠之举。他更有七分爱卖弄夸耀的习气。他是想让你、让我——让我们所有人都知道，他和同党是如何避开严密戒备，扮成满身污垢的采炭工潜入这座重兵把守的城池的。如今想来，他们办成这事并不难。那些家住城内的采炭工往往在夜幕降临时收工返城。那些该死的英国冒险分子极擅乔装改扮——一个个天生就是江湖骗子。他们既不缺银钱，又不缺厚颜。他们完全可以从沙丘旁边的村舍里借来或买下肮脏破烂的衣衫，然后混在回城的人流中混进城来。我敢说这招玩得很漂亮。那个红花侠不过是个狡黠的亡命徒罢了。迄今为止，全凭他那令人难以置信的鸿运帮他化险为夷。如今咱们且拭目以待。”

卡里耶默默倾听着。同僚身上的沉着从容此刻也渐渐感染了他。他不再像一头被激怒的公牛般狂哮怒吼——恐惧也不再让他沦为一个半是畏缩退缩、全凭野蛮本能行事的暴徒。他双臂交叠伏在桌案上，深陷的眼窝紧盯着肖维兰那张几乎深不可测的面孔。年轻的拉卢埃也收敛了几分平日里的傲慢嚣张。

===Original===

That mysterious spell which seemed to emanate from the elusive personality of the bold English adventurer had been cast over these two callous, bestial natures, humbling their arrogance and making them feel that here was no ordinary situation to be dealt with by smashing, senseless hitting and the spilling of innocent blood. Both felt instinctively too that this man Chauvelin, however wholly he may have failed in the past, was nevertheless still the only man who might grapple successfully with the elusive and adventurous foe.
“Are you assuming, citizen Chauvelin,” queried Carrier after awhile, “that this packet of papers was dropped purposely by the Englishman, so that it might get into our hands?”
“There is always such a possibility,” replied Chauvelin drily. “With that type of man one must be prepared to meet the unexpected.”
“Then go on, citizen Chauvelin. What else is there among those *satané* papers?”
“Nothing further of importance. There is a map of Nantes, and one of the coast and of Le Croisic. There is a cutting from *Le Moniteur* dated last September, and one from the *London Gazette* dated three years ago. The *Moniteur* makes reference to the production of *Athalie* at the Théâtre Molière, and the *London Gazette* to the sale of fat cattle at an Agricultural Show. There is a receipted account from a London tailor for two hundred pounds’ worth of clothes supplied, and one from a Lyons mercer for an hundred francs worth of silk cravats. Then there is the one letter which alone amidst all this rubbish appears to be of any consequence.﻿ ﻿…”
He took up the last paper; his hand was still quite steady.
“Read the letter,” said Carrier.
“It is addressed in the English fashion to Lady Anthony Dewhurst,” continued Chauvelin slowly, “the Kernogan woman, you know, citizen. It says:

> “ ‘Keep up your courage. Your friends are inside the city and on the watch. Try the door of your prison every evening at one hour before midnight. Once you will find it yield. Slip out and creep noiselessly down the stairs. At the bottom a friendly hand will be stretched out to you. Take it with confidence﻿—it will lead you to safety and to freedom. Courage and secrecy.’ ”

===Chinese===

从那位胆大包天的英国冒险分子那神出鬼没的个性中散发出的神秘魔力，笼罩了这两个麻木不仁、野性难驯的凶徒，挫平了他们的狂妄傲气，让他们感到眼前绝非以往那种靠滥施淫威、盲目滥杀无辜鲜血便能了结的寻常局面。两人也本能地意识到，眼前这位肖维兰尽管过去一败涂地，但依然是唯一能够与那个行踪诡秘的冒险劲敌正面抗衡的人选。

“你是认定，肖维兰公民，”过了片刻，卡里耶开口问道，“这包文件是那个英国佬故意扔下的，好落入我们手里？”

“向来有这种可能，”肖维兰冷冷地回答，“对付那种人，必须随时准备应对意料之外的事。”

“那么接着说，肖维兰公民。那些该死的文件里还有些什么？”

“其余的并不要紧。有一张南特地图，还有一张海岸和勒克鲁瓦西克的地图。有一张剪自去年九月份《导报》的剪报，还有一张三年前《伦敦公报》的剪报。《导报》提及了莫里哀剧院上演《阿塔莉》一事，而《伦敦公报》则报道了农展会上肥育肉牛的拍卖。有一张伦敦裁缝开具的价值两百镑衣服的收讫账单，还有一张里昂绸缎商开具的一百法郎丝绸领结收据。除此之外，在这堆废纸之中，唯有一封信看似非同小可……”

他拿起了最后一张信纸；他的手依旧十分沉稳。

“念念那封信，”卡里耶说道。

“抬头按照英国规矩写给安东尼·迪尤赫斯特勋爵夫人，”肖维兰慢条斯理地继续念道，“你知道的，公民，就是那个凯尔诺冈家的女人。上面写着：

> ‘鼓起勇气。你的朋友们已进城潜伏戒备。每晚午夜前一小时试着推一推牢房的门。总会有一次门会被推开。悄悄溜出来，轻手轻脚下楼。在楼梯底部会有一只友善的手伸向你。满怀信心地握住它吧——它将带你走向安全与自由。保持勇气，严守机密。’”

===Original===

Lalouët had been looking over his shoulder while he read: now he pointed to the bottom of the letter.
“And there is the device,” he said, “we have heard so much about of late﻿—a five-petalled flower drawn in red ink﻿ ﻿… the Scarlet Pimpernel, I presume.”
“Aye! the Scarlet Pimpernel,” murmured Chauvelin, “as you say! Braggadocio on his part or accident, his letters are certainly in our hands now and will prove﻿—must prove, the tool whereby we can be even with him once and for all.”
“And you, citizen Chauvelin,” interposed Carrier with a sneer, “are mighty lucky to have me to help you this time. I am not going to be fooled, as Candeille and you were fooled last September, as you were fooled in Calais and Héron in Paris. I shall be seeing this time to the capture of those English adventurers.”
“And that capture should not be difficult,” added Lalouët with a complacent laugh. “Your famous adventurer’s luck hath deserted him this time: an all-powerful proconsul is pitted against him and the loss of his papers hath destroyed the anonymity on which he reckons.”
Chauvelin paid no heed to the fatuous remarks.
How little did this flippant young braggart and this coarse-grained bully understand the subtle workings of that same adventurer’s brain! He himself﻿—one of the most astute men of the day﻿—found it difficult. Even now﻿—the losing of those letters in the open streets of Nantes﻿—it was part of a plan. Chauvelin could have staked his head on that﻿—a part of a plan for the liberation of Lady Anthony Dewhurst﻿—but what plan?﻿—what plan?
He took up the letter which his colleague had thrown down: he fingered it, handled it, letting the paper crackle through his fingers, as if he expected it to yield up the secret which it contained. The time had come﻿—of that he felt no doubt﻿—when he could at last be even with his enemy. He had endured more bitter humiliation at the hands of this elusive Pimpernel than he would have thought himself capable of bearing a couple of years ago. But the time had come at last﻿—if only he kept his every faculty on the alert, if Fate helped him and his own nerves stood the strain. Above all if this blundering, self-satisfied Carrier could be reckoned on!﻿ ﻿…
There lay the one great source of trouble! He﻿—Chauvelin﻿—had no power: he was disgraced﻿—a failure﻿—a nonentity to be sneered at.

===Chinese===

在肖维兰朗读时，拉卢埃一直在他肩头探头张望；此时他指着信纸的底部。

“这就是最近屡见不鲜的标记了，”他说道，“用红墨水画的一朵五瓣小花……我猜这就是红花侠了。”

“不错！正如你所言，是红花侠！”肖维兰喃喃自语道，“不管是他狂妄夸耀还是出于偶然，他的亲笔信如今确凿落在了我们手中，而这必定——必将成为我们跟他彻底算总账的利器。”

“而你，肖维兰公民，”卡里耶冷笑着插话道，“这次能有我相助可真是天大的造化。我可不会像去年九月坎代耶和你那样被耍得团团转，也不会像你在加来、埃隆在巴黎那样栽跟头。这一次我一定要亲自把那些英国冒险分子捉拿归案。”

“而且捉拿他们想必易如反掌，”拉卢埃得意洋洋地笑着补充道，“你们那位大名鼎鼎的冒险家这次鸿运到头了：跟他交手的是权倾一方的总督大人，而遗失了这些信件，彻底打碎了他赖以藏身的匿名隐蔽。”

肖维兰根本没理会这些愚不可及的蠢话。

这个轻浮狂妄的无知少年和这个粗鄙鲁莽的恶霸，哪里懂那位冒险家缜密如丝的心思！就连他自己——当世数一数二的老谋深算之士——都觉得难以捉摸。即便在当下——把信件丢在南特的闹市街头——这也是全盘计划的一环。肖维兰敢拿项上人头担保——这是营救安东尼·迪尤赫斯特勋爵夫人计划的一部分——可究竟是什么计划？……到底是什么计划？

他捡起被同僚扔在一旁的信件，在指间细细摩挲捻弄，信纸在指端沙沙作响，仿佛他指望从中挤出所深藏的秘密。时机已然成熟——对此他深信不疑——他终于可以同宿敌一决雌雄了。在这位神出鬼没的红花侠手里，他经受了奇耻大辱，换作两年前，他绝难想象自己竟能忍辱负重至此。但这一天终于到来了——只要他全神贯注、保持警觉，只要命运助他一臂之力，且自己的神经能够扛住重压。最重要的是，只要眼前这个鲁莽自负的卡里耶能够靠得住！……

而这恰恰是最大的隐患所在！他——肖维兰——手无寸权：他失宠受黜——是个遭人耻笑的失败者，一个无足轻重的小角色。

===Original===

He might protest, entreat, wring his hands, weep tears of blood and not one man would stir a finger to help him: this brute who sprawled here across his desk would not lend him half a dozen men to enable him to lay by the heels the most powerful enemy the Government of the Terror had ever known. Chauvelin inwardly ground his teeth with rage at his own impotence, at his own dependence on this clumsy lout, who was at this moment possessed of powers which he himself would give half his life to obtain.
But on the other hand he did possess a power which no one could take from him﻿—the power to use others for the furtherance of his own aims﻿—to efface himself while others danced as puppets to his piping. Carrier had the power: he had spies, Marats, prison-guards at his disposal. He was greedy for the reward, and cupidity and fear would make of him a willing instrument. All that Chauvelin need do was to use that instrument for his own ends. One would be the head to direct, the other﻿—a mere insentient tool.
From this moment onwards every minute, every second and every fraction of a second would be full of portent, full of possibilities. Sir Percy Blakeney was in Nantes with at least three or four members of his League: he was at this very moment taxing every fibre of his resourceful brain in order to devise a means whereby he could rescue his friend’s wife from the fate which was awaiting her: to gain this end he would dare everything, risk everything﻿—risk and dare a great deal more than he had ever dared and risked before.
Chauvelin was finding a grim pleasure in reviewing the situation, in envisaging the danger of failure which he knew lay in wait for him, unless he too was able to call to his aid all the astuteness, all the daring, all the resource of his own fertile brain. He studied his colleague’s face keenly﻿—that sullen, savage expression in it, the arrogance, the blundering vanity. It was terrible to have to humour and fawn to a creature of that stamp when all one’s hopes, all one’s future, one’s ideals and the welfare of one’s country were at stake.
But this additional difficulty only served to whet the man’s appetite for action. He drew in a long breath of delight, like a captive who first after many days and months of weary anguish scents freedom and ozone. He straightened out his shoulders.

===Chinese===

他就算抗议、哀求、顿足捶胸、泣血痛哭，也不会有一个人动一动手指来帮他：伏在桌前这个粗蛮恶棍绝不会借给他半打人马，好让他去抓捕恐怖政府有史以来遭遇过的最强劲敌。肖维兰暗自咬牙切齿，为自己的无能为力而愤恨，也为自己不得不仰仗眼前这个笨拙粗胚而憋屈——偏偏此人此刻手中掌握着的大权，是他甘愿用半条命去换取的。

但反过来说，他自身也拥有一股任谁都夺不走的力量——那便是操控他人来实现自身目的的能耐——他隐身幕后，操控他人如提线木偶般随着他的笛声起舞。卡里耶掌握着实权：他有密探、马拉连卫兵和狱卒听凭调遣。他贪恋赏金，贪欲与恐惧定会使他甘为驱使。肖维兰所要做的，无非是将这具工具玩弄于股掌之间。一人发号施令运筹帷幄，另一人则充当毫无知觉的盲目鹰犬。

从这一刻起，每一分钟、每一秒钟、哪怕每一刹那都充满玄机，暗藏无限可能。珀西·布莱克尼爵士正带着同盟里的至少三四名成员潜伏在南特城中：此际他定然正在殚精竭虑、绞尽脑汁，寻思着解救挚友之妻脱离险境的妙计；为了达成这一目的，他敢于孤注一掷、不惜一切代价——冒比以往任何时候都要大得多的险。

肖维兰在审视局势时感到了一种冷酷的快感，在设想那稍有不慎便会扑面而来的失败危机时，他也深知唯有倾尽自身全部的机敏、魄力与多谋，方能与之抗衡。他锐利地端详着同僚的面孔——那张阴沉凶残的嘴脸、那副傲慢自大与愚不可及的虚荣。当一个人的一切希望、全部前程、崇高理想乃至祖国的福祉全都悬于一线时，却不得不曲意逢迎、巴结讨好这样一头牲口，着实令人作呕。

但这层额外的阻碍反倒激起了他的斗志。他畅快地深吸了一口气，宛如一个在暗无天日的煎熬中度过漫长岁月后、初次嗅到自由与新鲜空气的囚徒。他挺直了双肩。

===Original===

A gleam of triumph and of hope shot out of his keen pale eyes. He studied Carrier and he studied Lalouët and he felt that he could master them both﻿—quietly, diplomatically, with subtle skill that would not alarm the proconsul’s rampant self-esteem: and whilst this coarse-fibred brute gloated in anticipatory pleasure over the handling of a few thousand francs, and whilst Martin-Roget dreamed of a clumsy revenge against one woman and one man who had wronged him four years ago, he﻿—Chauvelin﻿—would pursue his work of striking at the enemy of the Revolution﻿—of bringing to his knees the man who spent life and fortune in combating its ideals and in frustrating its aims. The destruction of such a foe was worthy a patriot’s ambition.
On the other hand some of Carrier’s bullying arrogance had gone. He was terrified to the very depths of his cowardly heart, and for once he was turning away from his favourite Jacques Lalouët and inclined to lean on Chauvelin for advice. Robespierre had been known to tremble at sight of that small scarlet device, how much more had he﻿—Carrier﻿—cause to be afraid. He knew his own limitations and he was terrified of the assassin’s dagger. As Marat had perished, so he too might end his days, and the English spies were credited with murderous intentions and superhuman power. In his innermost self Carrier knew that despite countless failures Chauvelin was mentally his superior, and though he never would own to this and at this moment did not attempt to shed his overbearing manner, he was watching the other keenly and anxiously, ready to follow the guidance of an intellect stronger than his own.

===Chinese===

他那双锐利淡色的眼眸中闪烁出一抹胜利与希望的光芒。他打量着卡里耶，打量着拉卢埃，深信自己完全能掌控这两人——神不知鬼不觉地、运用外交辞令与精妙手腕，既不触犯这位总督狂妄的自尊；在这头粗鄙凶残的野兽正提前陶醉于摆弄几千法郎的快感之中，在马丹-罗热还在做着向四年前得罪过他的一男一女施加拙劣报复的春梦之时，他——肖维兰——将继续推进他痛击革命死敌的大业——将那个倾家荡产、舍生忘死对抗革命理想、破坏革命宗旨的男人彻底打倒在地。铲除这样的死敌，方不负一个爱国者的雄心壮志。

另一方面，卡里耶身上那股飞扬跋扈的骄横之气也敛去了不少。在他懦弱内心的最深处早已吓得魂飞魄散，破天荒地不再一味依从宠儿雅克·拉卢埃，反而倾向于听取肖维兰的谋划。众所周知，罗伯斯庇尔一见到那小小的猩红标记都会不寒而栗，他卡里耶又怎能不心惊胆战。他深知自己的斤两，更对刺客的匕首怕到了骨子里。马拉死于非命，他自己也随时可能落得同样的下场，而且坊间都传那些英国间谍满怀弑杀之念，身怀通天彻地之能。在内心深处，卡里耶明白尽管肖维兰屡战屡败，但论智谋远在自己之上；尽管他绝不肯口头上认输，此刻也并未收敛飞扬跋扈的做派，但他正急切不安地紧盯着对方，随时准备顺从一个远比自己强大的头脑的指引。

#### III / 第三节
===Original===

At last Carrier elected to speak.
“And now, citizen Chauvelin,” he said, “we know how we stand. We know that the English assassins are in Nantes. The question is how are we going to lay them by the heels.”
Chauvelin gave him no direct reply. He was busy collecting his precious papers together and thrusting them back into the pocket of his coat. Then he said quietly:
“It is through the Kernogan woman that we can get hold of him.”
“How?”
“Where she is, there will the Englishmen be. They are in Nantes for the sole purpose of getting the woman and her father out of your clutches.﻿ ﻿…”
“Then it will be a fine haul inside the Rat Mort,” ejaculated Carrier with a chuckle. “Eh, Jacques, you young scamp? You and I must go and see that, what? You have been complaining that life was getting monotonous. Drownages﻿—Republican marriages! They have all palled in their turn on your jaded appetite.﻿ ﻿… But the capture of the English assassins, eh?﻿ ﻿… of that League of the Scarlet Pimpernel which has even caused citizen Robespierre much uneasiness﻿—that will stir up your sluggish blood, you lazy young vermin!﻿ ﻿… Go on, go on, citizen Chauvelin, I am vastly interested!”
He rubbed his dry, bony hands together and cackled with glee. Chauvelin interposed quietly:
“Inside the Rat Mort, eh, citizen?” he queried.
“Why, yes. Citizen Martin-Roget means to convey the Kernogan woman to the Rat Mort, doesn’t he?”
“He does.”
“And you say that where the Kernogan woman is there the Englishmen will be.﻿ ﻿…”
“The inference is obvious.”
“Which means ten thousand francs from that fool Martin-Roget for having the wench and her father arrested inside the Rat Mort! and twenty thousand for the capture of the English spies.﻿ ﻿… Have you forgotten, citizen Chauvelin,” he added with a raucous cry of triumph, “that commandant Fleury has my orders to make a raid on the Rat Mort this night with half a company of my Marats, and to arrest everyone whom they find inside?”
“The Kernogan wench is not at the Rat Mort yet,” quoth Chauvelin drily, “and you have refused to lend a hand in having her conveyed thither.”
“I can’t do it, my little Chauvelin,” rejoined Carrier, somewhat sobered by this reminder. “I can’t do it﻿ ﻿… you understand﻿ ﻿… my Marats taking an aristo to a house of ill-fame where presently I have her arrested﻿ ﻿… it won’t do﻿ ﻿… it won’t do﻿ ﻿… you don’t know how I am spied upon just now.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

卡里耶终于开口了。

“那么，肖维兰公民，”他说道，“我们现在的处境一清二楚了。我们知道那些英国刺客就在南特城里。问题在于我们该如何把他们一网打尽。”

肖维兰并未立刻正面作答。他正忙着把珍贵的文件拢在一起，重新揣回外衣口袋里。随后他波澜不惊地说道：

“要抓住他，必须通过凯尔诺冈家的女人。”

“怎么讲？”

“她在哪里，英国人就会出现在哪里。他们潜入南特的唯一目的，就是为了把那个女人和她父亲从你的手掌心里救出去……”

“那在‘死耗子’酒馆里可就能抓个满堂彩了，”卡里耶咯咯怪笑着脱口而出。“是吧，雅克，你这小浑球？你我非得亲自去看这场好戏不可，你说呢？你不是老抱怨日子过得索然无味吗？溺刑——‘共和国婚礼’！这些把戏轮番上阵，早把你倒胃口的胃口给腻烦透了……但这回可是抓捕英国刺客啊，嗯？……抓捕那个甚至连罗伯斯庇尔公民都寝食难安的红花同盟——这总能叫你麻木的血液沸腾起来吧，你这好吃懒做的小崽子！……接着说，快接着说，肖维兰公民，我可太感兴趣了！”

他搓着干瘪枯瘦的双手，发出咯咯的尖笑。肖维兰平静地插话道：

“在‘死耗子’酒馆里，是吗，公民？”他反问道。

“可不是嘛。马丹-罗热公民打算把凯尔诺冈家的女人弄到‘死耗子’酒馆去，不是吗？”

“确实如此。”

“而且你说过，凯尔诺冈家的女人在哪儿，英国人就会在哪儿……”

“理当如此。”

“这意味着从马丹-罗热那个蠢货手里稳赚一万法郎，因为我们会在‘死耗子’酒馆里逮捕那贱人和她父亲！抓到英国间谍还能再拿两万法郎……你莫非忘了，肖维兰公民，”他带着得意忘形的粗哑怪叫补充道，“弗勒里指挥官今晚早就接到了我的命令，要带着半个连的马拉连卫兵去突击搜查‘死耗子’酒馆，把里面所有人统统抓起来？”

“凯尔诺冈家那丫头眼下可还没到‘死耗子’酒馆呢，”肖维兰冷淡地说道，“而且你方才还拒绝插手把她押送过去。”

“我不能插手，我的小肖维兰，”经这一提醒，卡里耶稍微清醒了些，回应道，“我真插不得手……你明白的……要是我手下的马拉连卫兵把一个女贵族押到那种下流妓馆，然后我再派人去抓她……这成何体统……这万万使不得……你根本不知道眼下有多少双眼睛在暗中盯梢我……”

===Original===

It really would not do.﻿ ﻿… I can’t be mixed up in that part of the affair. The wench must go to the Rat Mort of her own free will, or the whole plan falls to the ground.﻿ ﻿… That fool Martin-Roget must think of a way﻿ ﻿… it’s his affair, after all. He must see to it.﻿ ﻿… Or you can think of a way,” he added, assuming the coaxing ways of a tiger-cat; “you are so clever, my little Chauvelin.”
“Yes,” replied Chauvelin quietly, “I can think of a way. The Kernogan wench shall leave the house of citizeness Adet and walk into the tavern of the Rat Mort of her own free will. Your reputation, citizen Carrier,” he added without the slightest apparent trace of a sneer, “your reputation shall be safeguarded in this matter. But supposing that in the interval of going from the one house to the other the English adventurer succeeds in kidnapping her.﻿ ﻿…”
“Pah! is that likely?” quoth Carrier with a shrug of the shoulders.
“Exceedingly likely, citizen; and you would not doubt it if you knew this Scarlet Pimpernel as I do. I have seen him at his nefarious work. I know what he can do. There is nothing that he would not venture﻿ ﻿… there are few ventures in which he does not succeed. He is as strong as an ox, as agile as a cat. He can see in the dark and he can always vanish in a crowd. Here, there and everywhere, you never know where he will appear. He is a past master in the art of disguise and he is a born mountebank. Believe me, citizen, we shall want all the resources of our joint intellects to frustrate the machinations of such a foe.”
Carrier mused for a moment in silence.
“H’m!” he said after awhile, and with a sardonic laugh. “You may be right, citizen Chauvelin. You have had experience with the rascal﻿ ﻿… you ought to know him. We won’t leave anything to chance﻿—don’t be afraid of that. My Marats will be keen on the capture. We’ll promise commandant Fleury a thousand francs for himself and another thousand to be distributed among his men if we lay hands on the English assassins tonight. We’ll leave nothing to chance,” he reiterated with an oath.
“In which case, citizen Carrier, you must on your side agree to two things,” rejoined Chauvelin firmly.
“What are they?”
“You must order Commandant Fleury to place himself and half a company of his Marats at my disposal.”
“What else?”

===Chinese===

“这真的万万使不得……我绝不能被牵扯进那一截里去。那丫头必须自愿走进‘死耗子’酒馆，否则全盘计划都得泡汤……那个笨蛋马丹-罗热必须自己想出法子来……归根结底这是他的勾当。他必须去办妥……或者你想个法子，”他补充道，脸上露出了斑猫般讨好的神气；“你那么机灵，我的小肖维兰。”

“行，”肖维兰平静地答道，“我能想出法子。凯尔诺冈家的丫头会乖乖离开阿代女公民的宅子，心甘情愿走进‘死耗子’酒馆。你的清誉，卡里耶公民，”他补充道，神色间丝毫看不出半点讥诮，“你的名声在这一节上定能万无一失。可要是从这所屋子走到另一所屋子的当口，那个英国冒险家半路把她劫走了呢……”

“呸！那怎么可能？”卡里耶耸了耸肩说道。

“极有可能，公民；要是你像我这般了解这位红花侠，就不会对此有半分怀疑了。我曾亲眼见识过他的卑劣行径。我深知他的本事。没有什么是他不敢冒险去干的……而且鲜少有冒险是他办不成的。他壮如奔牛，捷如狡猫。他在黑暗中能明察秋毫，在人群中总能凭空隐遁。此方彼处，神出鬼没，你永远猜不到他会在何处现身。他是乔装改扮的绝顶行家，更是天生的江湖术士。相信我吧，公民，要想挫败这等劲敌的阴谋，我们必须集中你我两人的全盘智慧。”

卡里耶默默沉思了片刻。

“唔！”过了一会儿，他带着冷酷的嘲笑说道，“或许你是对的，肖维兰公民。你跟那个无赖交过手……你理应最了解他。我们绝不给意外留半分空子——这你尽可放心。我手下的马拉连卫兵定会抢着拿人。今晚要是能把那些英国刺客捉拿归案，我们许诺弗勒里指挥官赏一千法郎归他自己，另赏一千法郎分发给他手下弟兄。我们绝不听天由命，”他赌咒发誓般重申道。

“既然如此，卡里耶公民，你也必须答应我两件事，”肖维兰坚定地回应道。

“哪两件事？”

“你必须下令弗勒里指挥官，让他本人带着半个连的马拉连卫兵听候我的调遣。”

“还有呢？”

===Original===

“You must allow them to lend a hand if there is an attempt to kidnap the Kernogan wench while she is being conveyed to the Rat Mort.﻿ ﻿…”
Carrier hesitated for a second or two, but only for form’s sake: it was his nature whenever he was forced to yield to do so grudgingly.
“Very well!” he said at last. “I’ll order Fleury to be on the watch and to interfere if there is any street-brawling outside or near the Rat Mort. Will that suit you?”
“Perfectly. I shall be on the watch too﻿—somewhere close by.﻿ ﻿… I’ll warn commandant Fleury if I suspect that the English are making ready for a coup outside the tavern. Personally I think it unlikely﻿—because the duc de Kernogan will be inside the Rat Mort all the time, and he too will be the object of the Englishmen’s attacks on his behalf. Citizen Martin-Roget too has about a score or so of his friends posted outside his sister’s house: they are lads from his village who hate the Kernogans as much as he does himself. Still! I shall feel easier in my mind now that I am certain of commandant Fleury’s cooperation.”
“Then it seems to me that we have arranged everything satisfactorily, what?”
“Everything, except the exact moment when Commandant Fleury shall advance with his men to the door of the tavern and demand admittance in the name of the Republic.”
“Yes, he will have to make quite sure that the whole of our quarry is inside the net, eh?﻿ ﻿… before he draws the strings﻿ ﻿… or all our pretty plans fall to nought.”
“As you say,” rejoined Chauvelin, “we must make sure. Supposing therefore that we get the wench safely into the tavern, that we have her there with her father, what we shall want will be someone in observation﻿—someone who can help us to draw our birds into the snare just when we are ready for them. Now there is a man whom I have in my mind: he hath name Paul Friche and is one of your Marats﻿—a surly, ill-conditioned giant﻿ ﻿… he was on guard outside Le Bouffay this afternoon.﻿ ﻿… I spoke to him﻿ ﻿… he would suit our purpose admirably.”
“What do you want him to do?”
“Only to make himself look as like a Nantese cutthroat as he can.﻿ ﻿…”
“He looks like one already,” broke in Jacques Lalouët with a laugh.
“So much the better. He’ll excite no suspicion in that case in the minds of the frequenters of the Rat Mort. Then I’ll instruct him to start a brawl﻿—a fracas﻿—soon after the arrival of the Kernogan wench.

===Chinese===

“在凯尔诺冈家丫头被带往‘死耗子’酒馆的途中，若有人试图劫人，你必须准许他们插手相助……”

卡里耶略微犹豫了一两秒，但不过是走走过场：只要是被迫让步，他的本性向来是心不甘情愿。

“好吧！”他终于开口道，“我会吩咐弗勒里密切监视，倘若‘死耗子’酒馆外头或附近发生当街斗殴，就立刻介入。这合你的意了吧？”

“万分妥当。我也将在附近暗中监视……要是我怀疑英国人在酒馆外头图谋不轨，自会向弗勒里指挥官发出警示。依我个人之见，这种可能性并不大——因为凯尔诺冈公爵始终待在‘死耗子’酒馆里，他也同样是英国人意图搭救的目标。此外，马丹-罗热公民在姐姐屋外还安插了二十来个熟人弟兄：全是他同村的后生，对凯尔诺冈家的仇恨绝不亚于他本人。不过！如今确信能有弗勒里指挥官鼎力相助，我心里总算踏实了。”

“那么依我看，咱们一切都布置得妥妥当当了，如何？”

“万事俱备，唯独除了弗勒里指挥官率众推进到酒馆门前、以共和国之名勒令开门的准确时机。”

“对，他必须万分笃定咱们的全套猎物都已钻进了网罗，嗯？……然后才能收紧网绳……否则咱们精心构思的妙计可就全落空了。”

“正如你所言，”肖维兰接茬道，“我们必须十拿九稳。因此，假定我们顺利把那丫头弄进了酒馆，让她与她父亲齐聚一处，我们所需要的便是一个能在暗中窥伺的人——一个能在万事俱备之时、帮我们把这群鸟儿诱进圈套的人。眼下我心里倒是有个合适人选：他名叫保罗·弗利什，是你手下的马拉连卫兵——一个脾气粗暴、凶神恶煞的彪形大汉……今天下午他正在布费城楼外当值……我和他说过话……他来干这桩差事再合适不过。”

“你想让他干什么？”

“只需让他尽可能装出一副南特亡命徒的模样……”

“他本来就长那副德行，”雅克·拉卢埃笑着插嘴道。

“那再好不过了。这样一来，出入‘死耗子’酒馆的常客绝不会对他起疑。随后我会指使他在凯尔诺冈家丫头进门后不久，挑起一场斗殴——闹出一场骚乱。”

===Original===

The row will inevitably draw the English adventurers hot-haste to the spot, either in the hope of getting the Kernogans away during the melee or with a view to protecting them. As soon as they have appeared upon the scene, the half company of the Marats will descend on the house and arrest everyone inside it.”
“It all sounds remarkably simple,” rejoined Carrier, and with a leer of satisfaction he turned to Jacques Lalouët.
“What think you of it, citizen?” he asked.
“That it sounds so remarkably simple,” replied young Lalouët, “that personally I should be half afraid﻿ ﻿…”
“Of what?” queried Chauvelin blandly.
“If you fail, citizen Chauvelin.﻿ ﻿…”
“Impossible!”
“If the Englishmen do not appear?”
“Even so the citizen proconsul will have lost nothing. He will merely have failed to gain the twenty thousand francs. But the Kernogans will still be in his power and citizen Martin-Roget’s ten thousand francs are in any case assured.”
“Friend Jean-Baptiste,” concluded Lalouët with his habitual insolent familiarity, “you had better do what citizen Chauvelin wants. Ten thousand francs are good﻿ ﻿… and thirty better still. Our privy purse has been empty far too long, and I for one would like the handling of a few brisk notes.”
“It will only be twenty-eight, citizen Lalouët,” interposed Chauvelin blandly, “for commandant Fleury will want one thousand francs and his men another thousand to stimulate their zeal. Still! I imagine that these hard times twenty-eight thousand francs are worth fighting for.”
“You seem to be fighting and planning and scheming for nothing, citizen Chauvelin,” retorted young Lalouët with a sneer. “What are you going to gain, I should like to know, by the capture of that daredevil Englishman?”
“Oh!” replied Chauvelin suavely, “I shall gain the citizen proconsul’s regard, I hope﻿—and yours too, citizen Lalouët. I want nothing more except the success of my plan.”
Young Lalouët jumped down to his feet. He shrugged his shoulders and through his fine eyes shot a glance of mockery and scorn on the thin, shrunken figure of the Terrorist.
“How you do hate that Englishman, citizen Chauvelin,” he said with a light laugh.

===Chinese===

“这阵骚乱势必会引得那些英国冒险分子十万火急赶到现场，要么是企图趁乱带走凯尔诺冈父女，要么是为了保护他们。只要他们一在现场露面，马拉连的半连人马便会包围房屋，将里面的所有人一网打尽。”

“听上去倒真是滴水不漏，”卡里耶回应道，带着心满意足的奸笑转向雅克·拉卢埃。

“你觉得如何，公民？”他问道。

“听上去实在太万无一失了，”年轻的拉卢埃回答道，“以至于换作我个人，难免会有些疑虑……”

“疑虑什么？”肖维兰神态自若地反问。

“万一你失手了呢，肖维兰公民……”

“绝无可能！”

“万一英国人根本没露面呢？”

“即便如此，总督公民也没有任何损失。他充其量只是没赚到那两万法郎罢了。但凯尔诺冈父女依然掌握在他手心里，马丹-罗热公民那一万法郎反正是板上钉钉的。”

“让-巴蒂斯特老兄，”拉卢埃带着一贯傲慢不逊的亲昵口气总结道，“你最好照肖维兰公民的意思办。一万法郎固然好……三万法郎自然更妙。咱们的小金库空瘪得太久了，连我都巴不得经手几张崭新的大钞呢。”

“满打满算只有两万八千法郎，拉卢埃公民，”肖维兰温和地插话道，“因为弗勒里指挥官要分走一千法郎，他手下的弟兄也得拿一千法郎来犒赏军心。不过！我想在当下的艰难世道里，两万八千法郎也值得好好搏上一搏了。”

“你争前恐后谋划算计，倒像是图个两手空空，肖维兰公民，”年轻的拉卢埃冷笑着讥讽道，“抓到了那个天不怕地不怕的英国佬，我倒想知道，你究竟能落着什么好处？”

“噢！”肖维兰温文尔雅地回答，“我希望能博得总督公民的青睐——还有你的好感，拉卢埃公民。除了计划成功，我别无所求。”

年轻的拉卢埃跳下桌子站起身来。他耸了耸肩，那双清秀的眼眸里向着那个枯瘦单薄的恐怖分子射出嘲弄与鄙夷的目光。

“你可真够恨那个英国佬的，肖维兰公民，”他轻声笑着说道。

#### IV / 第四节
===Original===

Carrier having fully realised that he in any case stood to make a vast sum of money out of the capture of the band of English spies, gave his support generously to Chauvelin’s scheme. Fleury, summoned into his presence, was ordered to place himself and half a company of Marats at the disposal of citizen Chauvelin. He demurred and growled like a bear with a sore head at being placed under the orders of a civilian, but it was not easy to run counter to the proconsul’s will. A good deal of swearing, one or two overt threats and the citizen commandant was reduced to submission. The promise of a thousand francs, when the reward for the capture of the English spies was paid out by a grateful Government, overcame his last objections.
“I think you should rid yourself of that obstinate oaf,” was young Lalouët’s cynical comment, when Fleury had finally left the audience chamber; “he is too argumentative for my taste.”
Chauvelin smiled quietly to himself. He cared little what became of every one of these Nantese louts once his great object had been attained.
“I need not trouble you further, citizen Carrier,” he said as he finally rose to take his leave. “I shall have my hands full until I myself lay that meddlesome Englishman bound and gagged at your feet.”
The phrase delighted Carrier’s insensate vanity. He was overgracious to Chauvelin now.
“You shall do that at the Rat Mort, citizen Chauvelin,” he said with marked affability, “and I myself will commend you for your zeal to the Committee of Public Safety.”
“Always supposing,” interposed Jacques Lalouët with his cynical laugh, “that citizen Chauvelin does not let the whole rabble slip through his fingers.”
“If I do,” concluded Chauvelin drily, “you may drag the Loire for my body tomorrow.”
“Oh!” laughed Carrier, “we won’t trouble to do that. Au revoir, citizen Chauvelin,” he added with one of his grandiloquent gestures of dismissal, “I wish you luck at the Rat Mort tonight.”
Jacques Lalouët ushered Chauvelin out. When he was finally left standing alone at the head of the stairs and young Lalouët’s footsteps had ceased to resound across the floors of the rooms beyond, he remained quite still for awhile, his eyes fixed into vacancy, his face set and expressionless; and through his lips there came a long-drawn-out sigh of intense satisfaction.

===Chinese===

卡里耶终于充分意识到，不论怎样，抓捕那帮英国间谍都能让自己大赚一笔横财，于是对肖维兰的计划给予了全力支持。弗勒里奉召前来，领命率领半个连的马拉连卫兵听任肖维兰公民调遣。他满腹牢骚，像一头被打破了脑袋的狗熊般咕哝抱怨自己竟然要受一个文官驱使，但违逆总督的意志谈何容易。在一通怒骂斥责和一两句明目张胆的恐吓之下，指挥官公民立刻服软顺从了。而一旦感恩戴德的政府兑现了擒获英国间谍的赏金便分他一千法郎的许诺，更是打消了他最后的抵触情绪。

“我看你早晚该把那个冥顽不灵的蠢货给除掉，”弗勒里最终退出会客室后，年轻的拉卢埃尖酸地评论道，“他那爱抬杠的德行实在叫我倒胃口。”

肖维兰暗自微微一笑。一旦他达成了崇高的目标，这帮南特蠢汉是死是活他根本毫不关心。

“我不再多打扰你了，卡里耶公民，”他终于起身告辞道，“在亲手将那个爱管闲事的英国佬捆个结实、堵上嘴巴扔在你的脚下之前，我可是要忙得不可开交了。”

这番话极大迎合了卡里耶狂妄愚蠢的虚荣心。此刻他对肖维兰客气得无以复加。

“那咱们就在‘死耗子’酒馆见分晓吧，肖维兰公民，”他格外和颜悦色地说道，“我定会亲自向救国委员会保奏你的忠勤。”

“但愿如此吧，”雅克·拉卢埃带着冷嘲热讽的笑声插话道，“只要肖维兰公民别让整帮贼寇从眼皮子底下溜走就行。”

“要是我放跑了人，”肖维兰冷冷地断言道，“明日你们大可去卢瓦尔河里捞我的尸首。”

“哈哈！”卡里耶大笑道，“我们可犯不着费那门子事。再会吧，肖维兰公民，”他做了一个夸张浮夸的送客手势补充道，“祝你今晚在‘死耗子’酒馆旗开得胜。”

雅克·拉卢埃将肖维兰送了出来。当肖维兰最终独自伫立在楼梯口，年轻拉卢埃穿过内室地板的脚步声也渐行渐远直至消逝之时，他伫立良久，纹丝不动，双眼凝视着虚空，面孔僵凝而毫无表情；从他的双唇间，吐出了一声悠长而满怀快意的喟叹。

===Original===

“And now, my fine Scarlet Pimpernel,” he murmured softly, “once more *à nous deux*.”
Then he ran swiftly down the stairs and a moment later was once more speeding toward Le Bouffay.

===Chinese===

“现在，我那了不起的红花侠，”他轻声自语道，“咱们又要一较高下了。”

随后他快步跑下楼梯，片刻之后，再度朝着布费城楼飞奔而去。
