与此同时，那帮自认明哲保身才是上策的人已摸到了门口。领头的一个男人推开房门，小心翼翼地探头朝门外的夜色中张望。他转过身冲身后的人将手指竖在嘴唇上，招手示意大伙儿悄悄跟上。
“伊冯娜，快走！”公爵一把抓紧了女儿的手，低声催促道。
“可是父亲……”
“快走吧！”他可怜兮兮地反复哀求，“要是继续待在这儿，我会死的！”
“不会等太久的，亲爱的父亲，”她苦苦哀求道，“要是爵爷同他的朋友赶来，却发现咱们不见了，那咱们不仅害了自己，还会连累他丢了性命啊！”
“我不信，”他带着弱者固执的偏执反驳道，“我才不信你那什么口信……爵爷也好，别人也罢，怎么可能跑来救你啊，我的孩子？……谁会知道你落在了南特这个人间地狱里。”
伊冯娜带着绝望的力量死死抱住他。她同样惊恐到了极点，然而深入骨髓的恐惧并未完全摧毁她对那道神秘口信的信任，未曾磨灭她对那位领她前来的高大向导的信赖，更未动摇她对那个猩红印记——象征着世间最崇高英勇之气的五瓣红花的笃信。
她竭尽全力想要留在这儿——抛开一切，抛开身旁肆虐不休、令人作呕的凶残殴斗，抛开眼下这番可怖至极的景象——她只想留在这儿等下去。她双臂紧紧环抱住父亲：每当他试图挪动脚步，她便死命将他往回拽。然而一种异乎寻常的怪力似乎压过了公爵先前的虚弱。他挣扎逃跑的企图变得越来越坚决，也越来越狂躁失常。
“走吧，伊冯娜！咱们得走！”他嘴里断断续续地念叨着，态度愈发冥顽不灵，“没人会注意咱们的……我在楼上阁楼里听见了动静……我蹑手蹑脚溜下来的……我就知道没人会留神我……走吧——咱们必须走……趁现在正是时候。”
“亲爱的父亲，咱们又能逃往何处呢？

===Original===

Once in the streets of Nantes what would happen to us?”
“We can find our way to the Loire!” he retorted almost brutally. He shook himself free from her restraining arms and gripped her firmly by the hand. He tried to drag her toward the door, whilst she still struggled to keep him back. He had just caught sight of the group of men and women at the front door: their leader was standing upon the threshold and was still peering out into the darkness.
But the next moment they all came to a halt: what their leader had perceived through the darkness did not evidently quite satisfy him: he turned and held a whispered consultation with the others. M. le duc strove with all his might to join in with that group. He felt that in its wake would lie the road to freedom. He would have struck Yvonne for standing in the way of her own safety.
“Father dear,” she contrived finally to say to him, “if you go hence, you will go alone. Nothing will move me from here, because I know that milor will come.”
“Curse you for your obstinacy,” retorted the duc, “you jeopardise my life and yours.”
Then suddenly from the angle of the room where wrangling and fighting were at their fiercest, there came a loud call:
“Look out, père Lemoine, your aristos are running away. You are losing your last chance of those fifty francs.”
It was Paul Friche who had shouted. His position on the table was giving him a commanding view over the heads of the threatening, shouting, perspiring crowd, and he had just caught sight of M. le duc dragging his daughter by force toward the door.
“The authors of all this pother,” he added with an oath, “and they will get away whilst we have the police about our ears.”
“Name of a name of a dog,” swore Lemoine from behind his bar, “that shall not be. Come along, maman, let us bring those aristos along here. Quick now.”
It was all done in a second. Lemoine and his wife, with the weight and authority of the masters of the establishment, contrived to elbow their way through the crowd. The next moment Yvonne felt herself forcibly dragged away from her father.
“This way, my girl, and no screaming,” a bibulous voice said in her ear, “no screaming, or I’ll smash some of those front teeth of yours. You said some rich friends were coming along for you presently. Well then! come and wait for them out of the crowd!”
Indeed Yvonne had no desire to struggle or to scream.

===Chinese===

一旦流落到南特的大街上，咱们会有什么下场？”
“咱们可以摸去卢瓦尔河边！”他近乎粗暴地顶撞道。他猛力甩脱了她阻拦的臂膀，一把死死钳住她的手。他使劲拉扯着她往门口拖，而她则拼死往后坠扯着他。他一眼瞥见了正门前那群男女：领头的那人正立在门槛上，依然在警惕地朝门外的夜色张望。
然而紧接着，那帮人全都不约而同地顿住了脚：领头人在夜色中瞧见的动静显然让他放心不下，他转过身同身后众人压低嗓门交头接耳起来。公爵先生使出吃奶的力气试图凑进那群人里。他认定紧随其后便是通往自由的大道。他甚至恨不得动手狠揍伊冯娜，只因她竟敢阻拦她自己的生路。
“亲爱的父亲，”她终于设法对他说出这句话，“如果您一定要走，那就只能独自一人去。什么也休想让我挪动半步，因为我知道爵爷一定会来。”
“你这该死的不肖顽固种，”公爵怒骂道，“你这是在拿我的命和你自己的命胡闹！”
就在这时，从争吵与厮打最为激烈的大厅一角，突然炸响了一声洪亮的呼喊：
“当心啊，勒穆瓦纳老爹，你的贵族佬要溜啦！那五十法郎你可要鸡飞蛋打喽！”
喊话的正是保罗·弗利什。他高踞在桌台上，视野居高临下，正好能越过那群叫骂恐吓、汗流浃背的人头，一眼捕捉到公爵先生正硬拽着女儿往大门方向拖。
“惹出这一摊子祸事的罪魁祸首，”他啐骂着补了一句，“咱们在这儿替他们顶着巡警，他们倒想趁机溜之大吉！”
“真他娘的见鬼了，”勒穆瓦纳在柜台后破口大骂，“没门儿！走，老太婆，咱们把那俩贵族佬弄到这儿来。动作麻利点！”
这一切不过发生在眨眼之间。勒穆瓦纳夫妇仗着店主人的架势与分量，硬生生用手肘在人群中挤出一条道来。紧接着，伊冯娜便觉察到自己被人猛力从父亲身边一把扯开。
“这边走，丫头片子，甭叫唤，”一个带着浓重酒气的嗓门在她耳边恶狠狠地威胁道，“敢叫一声，老子敲碎你满嘴门牙！你刚才不是吹嘘待会儿有阔朋友来找你吗？那好！滚到人群外面等他们去！”
实际上，伊冯娜根本无心挣扎，也无意呼喊。

===Original===

Salvation she thought had come to her and to her father in this rough guise. In another moment mayhap he would have forced her to follow him, to leave milor in the lurch, to jeopardise forever every chance of safety.
“It is all for the best, father dear,” she managed to cry out over her shoulder, for she had just caught sight of him being seized round the shoulders by Lemoine and heard him protesting loudly:
“I’ll not go! I’ll not go! Let me go!” he shouted hoarsely. “My daughter! Yvonne! Let me go! You devil!”
But Lemoine had twice the vigour of the duc de Kernogan, nor did he care one jot about the other’s protests. He hated all this row inside his house, but there had been rows in it before and he was beginning to hope that nothing serious would come of it. On the other hand, Paul Friche might be right about these aristos; there might be forty or fifty francs to be made out of them, and in any case they had one or two things upon their persons which might be worth a few francs﻿—and who knows? they might even have something in their pockets worth taking.
This hope and thought gave Lemoine additional strength, and seeing that the aristo struggled so desperately, he thought to silence him by bringing his heavy fist with a crash upon the old man’s head.
“Yvonne! *A moi!*” shouted M. le duc ere he fell back senseless.
That awful cry, Yvonne heard it as she was being dragged through the noisome crowd. It mingled in her ear with the other awful sounds﻿—the oaths and blasphemies which filled the air with their hideousness. It died away just as a formidable crash against the entrance door suddenly silenced every cry within.
“All hands up!” came with a peremptory word of command from the doorway.
“Mercy on us!” murmured the woman Lemoine, who still had Yvonne by the hand, “we are undone this time.”
There was a clatter and grounding of arms﻿—a scurrying of bare feet and sabots upon the floor, the mingled sounds of men trying to fly and being caught in the act and hurled back: screams of terror from the women, one or two pitiable calls, a few shrill cries from frightened children, a few dull thuds as of human bodies falling.﻿ ﻿… It was all so confused, so unspeakably horrible. Yvonne was hardly conscious. Near her someone whispered hurriedly:
“Put the aristos away somewhere, maman Lemoine﻿ ﻿… the whole thing may only be a scare﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

她心想，救赎或许正是披着这般粗暴的外衣降临在她和父亲身上的。若再耽搁片刻，父亲保不齐真会强行逼她跟着逃走，把爵爷抛下不管，彻底葬送一切得救的希望。
“这一切都是最好的安排，亲爱的父亲！”她设法扭头高喊了一声，因为她正好瞥见勒穆瓦纳一把反剪住了公爵的双肩，并听见父亲在大声抗议：
“我不去！我不去！放开我！”他嘶哑地怒吼道，“我的女儿！伊冯娜！放开我！你这个恶魔！”
然而勒穆瓦纳的气力足有凯尔诺冈公爵的两倍，对后者的抗议根本不屑一顾。他打心底里厌恶店里这番吵闹，但以往店里也不是没闹过乱子，他开始心存侥幸，觉得兴许不会捅出什么大篓子。另一方面，保罗·弗利什关于这俩贵族佬的话没准真说对了；在他们身上没准真能捞到四五十个法郎呢，退一步讲，他们身上少说也有一两件值几个法郎的行头——天晓得呢？没准他们兜里还揣着什么值钱的稀罕玩意儿呢。
这番盘算与贪念让勒穆瓦纳力气陡增，见这个贵族佬拼命挣扎不休，他索性抡起沉重如铁的拳头，狠狠砸在老人的头颅上，好教他闭嘴。
“伊冯娜！救我！”公爵先生在仰面栽倒、失去知觉前惨叫了一声。
那声撕心裂肺的呼救，被拖拽着穿过恶臭人群的伊冯娜听得真真切切。那声音同周遭其他刺耳骇人的声响交织在她的耳膜里——污言秽语与恶毒咒骂以其狰狞的面目充塞着空气。紧接着，正门上骤然传来的一声惊天动地的猛烈撞击，瞬间令屋内的所有叫嚷戛然而止，那呼救声也随之湮没无闻。
“统统举起手来！”门口传来一声不容置疑的严厉喝令。
“老天爷保佑！”仍死死攥着伊冯娜双手的勒穆瓦纳婆子倒抽一口凉气喃喃道，“这回咱们全完了。”
四周随即响起了兵器的铿锵撞击声与顿地声——光脚板和木屐在地面上慌乱杂沓的奔逃声，妄图逃跑者当场被擒拿并狠狠摔回地面的撕扯声；女人们惊恐万状的尖叫，一两声凄楚的求饶，受惊孩童尖锐刺耳的哭嚎，还有肉身沉闷倒地的扑通声……一切都混乱得令人窒息，可怖得无以复加。伊冯娜几乎陷入了昏迷。在她身旁，有人急促地低声耳语道：
“快把贵族佬藏到什么地方去，勒穆瓦纳大妈……这阵势没准只是虚惊一场……

===Original===

the Marats may only be here about the aristos﻿ ﻿… they will probably leave you alone if you give them up﻿ ﻿… perhaps you’ll get a reward﻿ ﻿… Put them away till some of this row subsides﻿ ﻿… I’ll talk to commandant Fleury if I can.”
Yvonne felt her knees giving way under her. There was nothing more to hope for now﻿—nothing. She felt herself lifted from the ground﻿—she was too sick and faint to realise what was happening: through the din which filled her ears she vainly tried to distinguish her father’s voice again.

===Chinese===

马拉连没准只是冲着贵族佬来的……要是你把人交出去，他们多半不会为难你……说不定还能赏你几个钱呢……先把人藏严实，等这阵风头过去……我看看能不能同弗勒里指挥官递上话。”
伊冯娜感到双膝一软，再也支撑不住。如今再也没有什么指望了——万念俱灰。她感觉自己被人从地上架了起来——她恶心眩晕，甚至分辨不清究竟发生了什么：在充斥耳鼓的轰鸣嘈杂声中，她徒劳地试图再次辨听出父亲的声音。

#### V / 第五节
===Original===

A moment or two later she found herself squatting somewhere on the ground. How she got here she did not know﻿—where she was she knew still less. She was in total darkness. A fusty, close smell of food and wine gave her a wretched feeling of nausea﻿—her head ached intolerably, her eyes were hot, her throat dry: there was a constant buzzing in her ears.
The terrible sounds of fighting and screaming and cursing, the crash of broken glass and overturned benches came to her as through a partition﻿—close by but muffled.
In the immediate nearness all was silence and darkness.

===Chinese===

过了一两分钟，她发觉自己正蜷缩在某个阴暗的地坑里。她究竟是如何来到此地的，她全然不知——身处何方，她更是一无所知。四周一片漆黑，伸手不见五指。食物发馊的气味与混浊的酒气混杂成一股窒闷的霉味，教她一阵阵反胃作呕——她的头痛得无法忍受，双眼灼痛，喉咙干渴：耳畔嗡嗡轰鸣不绝。
扭打、尖叫、怒骂的可怕声浪，碎玻璃迸裂与长凳掀翻的巨响，透过隔板隐隐传来——近在咫尺，却又显得沉闷发虚。
在近在身旁的天地里，唯有一片死寂与漆黑。

## VIII The English Adventurers / 第八章 英国冒险者
===Original===



===Chinese===

第八章 英国冒险者

#### I / 第一节
===Original===

It was with that muffled din still ringing in her ear and with the conception of all that was going on, on the other side of the partition, standing like an awesome spectre of evil before her mind, that Yvonne woke to the consciousness that her father was dead.
He lay along the last half-dozen steps of a narrow wooden staircase which had its base in the narrow, cupboard-like landing on to which the Lemoines had just thrust them both. Through a small heart-shaped hole cut in the door of the partition-wall, a shaft of feeble light struck straight across to the foot of the stairs: it lit up the recumbent figure of the last of the ducs de Kernogan, killed in a brawl in a house of evil fame.
Weakened by starvation, by the hardships of the past few days, his constitution undermined by privations and mayhap too by gnawing remorse, he had succumbed to the stunning blow dealt to him by a half drunken brute. His cry: “Yvonne! *A moi!*” was the last despairing call of a soul racked with remorse to the daughter whom he had so cruelly wronged.
When first that feeble shaft of light had revealed to her the presence of that inert form upon the steps, she had struggled to her feet and﻿—dazed﻿—had tottered up to it. Even before she had touched the face, the hands, before she had bent her ear to the half-closed mouth and failed to catch the slightest breath, she knew the full extent of her misery. The look in the wide-open eyes did not terrify her, but they told her the truth, and since then she had cowered beside her dead father on the bottom step of the narrow stairs, her fingers tightly closed over that one hand which never would be raised against her.
An unspeakable sense of horror filled her soul. The thought that he﻿—the proud father, the haughty aristocrat, should lie like this and in such a spot, dragged in and thrown down﻿—no doubt by Lemoine﻿—like a parcel of rubbish and left here to be dragged away again and thrown again like a dog into some unhallowed ground﻿—that thought was so horrible, so monstrous, that at first it dominated even sorrow. Then came the heartrending sense of loneliness. Yvonne Dewhurst had endured so much these past few days that awhile ago she would have affirmed that nothing could appal her in the future. But this was indeed the awful and overwhelming climax to what had already been a surfeit of misery.
This!

===Chinese===

耳畔仍回荡着那沉闷的嘈杂喧嚣，隔板另一侧所发生的一切宛如可怖的罪恶梦魇在脑海中挥之不去，就在此时，伊冯娜恍惚意识到，父亲已经死了。
他横卧在狭窄木楼梯最底下的五六级台阶上，楼梯底部连着一处壁橱般逼仄的楼梯平台，刚才勒穆瓦纳夫妇正是把他们俩推搡进了这里。隔板门上开着一个心形小孔，一束微弱的光线直射在楼梯脚下，照亮了最后一位凯尔诺冈公爵倒卧的身躯——他在这座声名狼藉的下流酒馆的一场斗殴中丧了命。
连日来的饥寒交迫与艰难困苦耗尽了他的气力，匮乏的折磨、或许还有啃噬心灵的悔恨彻底垮掉了他的身子骨；面对一个半醉凶徒挥来的致命重击，他终于支撑不住倒了下去。他那声呼喊——“伊冯娜！救我！”——是一个饱受悔恨煎熬的灵魂，向被他狠狠伤害的女儿发出的最后绝望呼唤。
最初那一束微光让她看清台阶上瘫卧的人影时，她挣扎着站起身，头晕目眩地踉跄走上前去。甚至在她触碰他的脸庞与双手之前，在她俯下身将耳朵凑近那半张的嘴唇却探不到一丝鼻息之前，她便已深知自身痛苦的深重。那双圆睁的眼睛里的神情并未吓退她，却将真相昭示无遗；自那一刻起，她便蜷缩在死去的父亲身旁，坐在狭窄楼梯的最下一级台阶上，手指紧紧攥住那只再也不会向她挥起的枯手。
一种难以言表的惊骇充塞了她的灵魂。一想到他——那位骄傲的父亲、高傲的贵族——竟会落得这般下场，横尸在如此肮脏龌龊之地，像一包垃圾似地被拖进来扔在地上（毫无疑问是勒穆瓦纳干的），被丢弃在这里，待会儿又要像条死狗一样被拖走，扔进某处未经祝福的不洁荒冢——这念头是如此骇人、如此可怖，以至于起初它甚至压倒了悲伤。紧接着，一阵撕心裂肺的孤独感袭上心头。过去几天里，伊冯娜·迪尤赫斯特饱受磨难，片刻之前她尚敢断言，往后世间再无任何变故能令她惊惧。但这惨烈的景象，委实是将早已满溢的苦难推向了可怖而摧毁一切的顶点。
这等绝境！

===Original===

she, Yvonne, cowering beside her dead father, with no one to stand between her and any insult, any outrage which might be put upon her, with nothing now but a few laths between her and that yelling, screeching mob outside.
Oh! the loneliness! the utter, utter loneliness!
She kissed the inert hand, the pale forehead: with gentle, reverent fingers she tried to smooth out those lines of horror and of fear which gave such a pitiful expression to the face. Of all the wrongs which her father had done her she never thought for a moment. It was he who had brought her to this terrible pass: he who had betrayed her into the hands of her deadliest enemy: he who had torn her from the protecting arms of her dear milor and flung her and himself at the mercy of a set of inhuman wretches who knew neither compunction nor pity.
But all this she forgot, as she knelt beside the lifeless form﻿—the last thing on earth that belonged to her﻿—the last protection to which she might have clung.

===Chinese===

她，伊冯娜，蜷缩在死去的父亲身旁，再无人能挡在她身前替她抵御即将降临的任何羞辱与蹂躏；如今将她与门外狂呼乱叫的暴徒隔开的，唯有区区几根薄木板条。
呵！这无边的孤独！这彻彻底底、令人窒息的孤独！
她亲吻着那只冰凉僵硬的手，亲吻着那苍白的额头；她用温柔而虔敬的手指，试图抚平那张脸上凝固的惊恐与畏惧的皱纹，那神情显得如此凄惶可怜。父亲对她所犯下的一切过错，她此刻连一刹那都未曾想起。是他把她带到了这般绝境；是他把她出卖到了不共戴天的死敌手中；是他将她从亲爱的勋爵丈夫那遮风挡雨的怀抱中硬生生拽走，把她和他自己都抛到了这群毫无人性、不知悔恨亦无怜悯的恶徒掌心，任由宰割。
但这一切她都抛诸脑后了，她跪倒在这具了无生气的遗体旁——这是世间属于她的最后一件依托，是她原本或许能够紧紧依附的最后一道庇护。

#### II / 第二节
===Original===

Out of the confusion of sounds which came﻿—deadened by the intervening partition﻿—to her ear, it was impossible to distinguish anything very clearly. All that Yvonne could do, as soon as she had in a measure collected her scattered senses, was to try and piece together the events of the last few minutes﻿—minutes which indeed seemed like days and even years to her.
Instinctively she gave to the inert hand which she held an additional tender touch. At any rate her father was out of it all. He was at rest and at peace. As for the rest, it was in God’s hands. Having only herself to think of now, she ceased to care what became of her. He was out of it all: and those wretches after all could not do more than kill her. A complete numbness of senses and of mind had succeeded the feverish excitement of the past few hours: whether hope still survived at this moment in Yvonne Dewhurst’s mind it were impossible to say. Certain it is that it lay dormant﻿—buried beneath the overwhelming misery of her loneliness.
She took the fichu from her shoulders and laid it reverently over the dead man’s face: she folded the hands across the breast. She could not cry: she could only pray, and that quite mechanically.
The thought of her dear milor, of his clever friend, of the message which she had received in prison, of the guide who had led her to this awful place, was relegated﻿—almost as a memory﻿—in the furthermost cell of her brain.

===Chinese===

隔板将声音削弱了许多，从那片杂乱传来的声响中，根本无法分辨出任何清晰的动静。伊冯娜稍微收拢起涣散的心神后，所能做的唯有竭力拼凑起过去短短几分钟内发生的事情——这几分钟对她而言，真如同过了几天、甚至几年那般漫长。
她本能地将手中那只冰凉僵硬的手握得更轻柔温存了些。无论如何，父亲总算超脱了这一切。他安息了，得到了宁静。至于其余的事情，全在上帝的掌管之中。如今只需考虑自己一人，她对自身的命运反倒不再挂怀。他已经脱离了这苦海，而那些恶棍充其量也不过是杀了她罢了。感官与心智的彻底麻木取代了过去数小时那狂热紧绷的情绪；此时此刻，伊冯娜·迪尤赫斯特的心中是否还存有一线希望，委实难以断言。可以确定的是，希望已然沉睡——深埋于无边孤独的沉重悲戚之下。
她解下肩头的三角披肩，虔敬地覆在死者的面容上；她把他的双手交叠安放在胸前。她哭不出来，只能祈祷，而且完全是机械木然地祈求。
关于她亲爱的勋爵丈夫、勋爵那位机智过人的朋友、她在狱中收到的密信，以及将她引向这处魔窟的神秘向导的思绪，都被远远抛到了脑海中最幽深偏僻的角落，宛如一段模糊褪色的回忆。

#### III / 第三节
===Original===

But after awhile outraged nature, still full of vitality and of youth, reasserted itself. She felt numb and cold and struggled to her feet. From somewhere close to her a continuous current of air indicated the presence of some sort of window. Yvonne, faint with the close and sickly smell, which even that current failed to disperse, felt her way all round the walls of the narrow landing.
The window was in the wall between the partition and the staircase, it was small and quite low down. It was crossed with heavy iron bars. Yvonne leaned up against it, grateful for the breath of pure air.
For awhile yet she remained unconscious of everything save the confused din which still went on inside the tavern, and at first the sounds which came through the grated window mingled with those on the other side of the partition. But gradually as she contrived to fill her lungs with the cold breath of heaven, it seemed as if a curtain was being slowly drawn away from her atrophied senses.
Just below the window two men were speaking. She could hear them quite distinctly now﻿—and soon one of the voices﻿—clearer than the other﻿—struck her ear with unmistakable familiarity.
“I told Paul Friche to come out here and speak to me,” Yvonne heard that same voice say.
“Then he should be here,” replied the other, “and if I am not mistaken.﻿ ﻿…”
There was a pause, and then the first voice was raised again.
“Halt! Is that Paul Friche?”
“At your service, citizen,” came in reply.
“Well! Is everything working smoothly inside?”
“Quite smoothly; but your Englishmen are not there.”
“How do you know?”
“Bah! I know most of the faces that are to be found inside the Rat Mort at this hour: there are no strangers among them.”
The voice that had sounded so familiar to Yvonne was raised now in loud and coarse laughter.
“Name of a dog! I never for a moment thought that there were any Englishmen about. Citizen Chauvelin was suffering from nightmare.”
“It is early yet,” came in response from a gentle bland voice, “you must have patience, citizen.”
“Patience? Bah!” ejaculated the other roughly. “As I told you before ’tis but little I care about your English spies. ’Tis the Kernogans I am interested in. What have you done with them, citizen?”
“I got that blundering fool Lemoine to lock them up on the landing at the bottom of the stairs.”
“Is that safe?”
“Absolutely.

===Chinese===

但过了片刻，遭受重创却依然充满青春活力的生命本能重新苏醒了。她感到浑身发僵冰凉，挣扎着站起身来。身旁不知何处持续吹来的一股气流，暗示着附近有某种窗户存在。那股沉闷作呕的恶臭即便有气流吹拂也驱散不掉，伊冯娜熏得一阵眩晕，便沿着逼仄楼梯平台的墙壁摸索起来。
窗户位于隔板与楼梯之间的墙壁上，位置很低且很小，上面装着粗重的铁栅栏。伊冯娜靠在窗前，为能呼吸到一口新鲜空气而暗自庆幸。
在随后的片刻工夫里，除了酒馆内持续不断的嘈杂喧闹，她对周围的一切仍浑然不觉；起初，穿过铁栅窗传进来的声响与隔板另一侧的喧嚣混杂在一起。然而，随着她努力将冰冷清冽的空气吸入肺腑，原本麻木的感官仿佛被缓缓揭开了一层帷幕。
就在窗下正下方，有两名男子正在交谈。她现在能听得相当真切——很快，其中一个较清晰的声音传入耳中，带着绝不会听错的熟悉感。
“我叫保罗·弗里什出来跟我回话的，”伊冯娜听见那个声音说道。
“那他就该在这儿了，”另一人答道，“要是我没看错的话……”
一阵停顿，随后第一个声音又提高了调门。
“站住！是保罗·弗里什吗？”
“听凭差遣，公民，”有人应声答道。
“怎么样！里面一切顺利吗？”
“相当顺利；但你们要找的那些英国佬不在里面。”
“你怎么知道？”
“呸！这个点儿待在死耗子酒馆里的面孔我大多认得，里面根本没有生面孔。”
那个对伊冯娜来说如此熟悉的声音，此刻爆发出一阵粗鲁刺耳的大笑。
“见鬼！我从一开始就压根不信有什么英国佬在附近。分明是肖维兰公民在疑神疑鬼做噩梦。”
“时辰尚早，”一个温和圆滑的声音慢条斯理地回应道，“您得有耐心，公民。”
“耐心？呸！”另一人粗暴地嚷道，“我早就跟你挑明过，我压根不在乎你那些英国间谍。我关心的只有凯尔诺冈家的人。公民，你把他们弄到哪儿去了？”
“我让勒穆瓦纳那个笨蛋把他们锁在楼梯底下的平台上了。”
“那地方稳当吗？”
“万无一失。

===Original===

It has no egress save into the taproom and up the stairs, to the rooms above. Your English spies if they came now would have to fly in and out of those top windows ere they could get to the aristos.”
“Then in Satan’s name keep them there awhile,” urged the more gentle, insinuating voice, “until we can make sure of the English spies.”
“Tshaw! What foolery!” interjected the other, who appeared to be in a towering passion. “Bring them out at once, citizen Friche﻿ ﻿… bring them out﻿ ﻿… right into the middle of the rabble in the taproom.﻿ ﻿… Commandant Fleury is directing the perquisition﻿—he is taking down the names of all that cattle which he is arresting inside the premises﻿—let the ci-devant duc de Kernogan and his exquisite daughter figure among the vilest cutthroats of Nantes.”
“Citizen, let me urge on you once more﻿ ﻿…” came in earnest persuasive accents from that gentle voice.
“Nothing!” broke in the other savagely. “To h‑ll with your English spies. It is the Kernogans that I want.”
Yvonne, half-crazed with horror, had heard the whole of this abominable conversation wherein she had not failed to recognise the voice of Martin-Roget or Pierre-Adet, as she now knew him to be. Who the other two men were she could easily conjecture. The soft bland voice she had heard twice during these past few days, which had been so full of misery, of terror and of surprise: once she had heard it on board the ship which had taken her away from England and once again a few hours since, inside the narrow room which had been her prison. The third man who had subsequently arrived on the scene was that coarse and grimy creature who had seemed to be the moving evil spirit of that awful brawl in the tavern.
What the conversation meant to her she could not fail to guess. Pierre Adet had by what he said made the whole of his abominable intrigue against her palpably clear. Her father had been right, after all. It was Pierre Adet who through some clever trickery had lured her to this place of evil. How it was all done she could not guess. The message﻿ ﻿… the device﻿ ﻿… her walk across the street﻿ ﻿… the silence﻿ ﻿… the mysterious guide﻿ ﻿… which of these had been the trickery?﻿ ﻿… which had been concocted by her enemy?﻿ ﻿… which devised by her dear milor?

===Chinese===

除了通往大堂和顺着楼梯上到顶层的房间之外，那里根本没有别的出路。就算你的英国间谍这会儿赶来，也得从顶层窗户飞进飞出，才能接近那些贵族佬。”
“那就看在撒旦的分上，先把他们关在那儿一阵子，”那个更加温和、带着逢迎诱导的声音催促道，“等我们拿稳了那些英国间谍再说。”
“呸！真是胡闹！”另一人插话道，显然已是勃然大怒，“立刻把他们押出来，弗里什公民……押出来……直接押到大堂的人堆里去……弗勒里指挥官正在主持搜查——他正把店里抓到的这帮畜生的名字一一登记在册——让前凯尔诺冈公爵和他那娇贵的千金，混在南特最下流的一帮亡命之徒里一起登记。”
“公民，请容我再劝你一句……”那个温和的声音带着恳切劝诱的口吻说道。
“休想！”另一人蛮横地打断道，“去他妈的英国间谍。我要的是凯尔诺冈家的人。”
伊冯娜吓得几乎发狂，将这番令人作呕的对话听得一清二楚；她毫不费力便听出了马丹-罗热的声音——如今她已知道他其实就是皮埃尔·阿代。另外两人是谁，她也能轻易猜出。那轻柔温顺的嗓音，在过去这几天充斥着苦难、恐怖与惊骇的日子里，她曾听过两次：一次是在将她掳离英格兰的船上，另一次就在几个小时前，在她被囚禁的那间狭窄屋子里。至于后来赶到的第三个男人，则是那个粗俗邋遢的家伙，方才酒馆里那场可怕的斗殴，似乎就是由他在暗中挑唆作祟。
这番对话对她意味着什么，她不可能猜不出来。皮埃尔·阿代方才的话语，已然将针对她的卑鄙阴谋暴露得一览无遗。原来父亲终究是对的。正是皮埃尔·阿代耍弄了奸计，将她诱骗到了这处魔窟。这一切究竟是如何布下的，她无从知晓。那张字条……那个暗号印记……她穿过街道的那段路……那死一般的沉寂……那个神秘的向导……这当中究竟哪一样是陷阱？……哪一样是仇敌捏造的诡计？……又有哪一样当真是她亲爱的勋爵丈夫安排的营救？

===Original===

Enough that the whole thing was a trap, a trap all the more hideous as she, Yvonne, who would have given her heart’s blood for her beloved, was obviously the bait wherewith these friends meant to capture him and his noble chief. They knew evidently of the presence of the gallant Scarlet Pimpernel and his band of heroes here in Nantes﻿—they seemed to expect their appearance at this abominable place tonight. She, Yvonne, was to be the decoy which was to lure to this hideous lair those noble eagles who were still out of reach.
And if that was so﻿—if indeed her beloved and his valiant friends had followed her hither, then some part of the message of hope must have come from them or from their chief﻿ ﻿… and milor and his friend must even now be somewhere close by, watching their opportunity to come to her rescue﻿ ﻿… heedless of the awful danger which lay in wait for them﻿ ﻿… ignorant mayhap of the abominable trap which had been so cunningly set for them by these astute and ferocious brutes.
Yvonne a prisoner in this narrow space, clinging to the bars of what was perhaps the most cruel prison in which she had yet been confined, bruised her hands and arms against those bars in a wild desire to get out. She longed with all her might to utter one long, loud and piercing cry of warning to her dear milor not to come nigh her now, to fly, to run while there was yet time; and all the while she knew that if she did utter such a cry he would hurry hot-haste to her side. One moment she would have had him near﻿—another she wished him an hundred miles away.

===Chinese===

不管怎样，整件事彻头彻尾是一个圈套，而最令人毛骨悚然的是，愿为挚爱之人流尽心头热血的她——伊冯娜，显然正是这帮恶魔用来诱捕他以及他那位高尚首领的诱饵。他们显然知晓英勇的红花侠及其英雄同伴就在南特城中——他们似乎料定他们今夜必定会现身于这处险恶之地。她，伊冯娜，便是用来引诱那些翱翔天际、尚难捕获的高贵雄鹰自投罗网的诱鸟。
假若真是如此——假若她的挚爱与他英勇的朋友们当真尾随她一路追寻到了这里，那么那封满含希望的密信定有几分确实出自他们或其首领之手……而勋爵大人和他的朋友此刻必定就潜伏在附近某处，伺机前来搭救她……全然不顾正严阵以待的凶险杀机……或许还根本料想不到这帮狡诈凶残的恶徒已为他们布下了这等阴狠绝伦的陷阱。
伊冯娜被囚禁在这逼仄的方寸之地，紧紧抓着铁栏——这或许是她迄今为止所被关押的最残酷的囚牢——在急欲逃脱的疯狂渴望中，将自己的双手与臂膀在铁栅栏上撞得青一块紫一块。她竭尽全力想要发出一声凄厉高亢的长声警报，告诫她亲爱的勋爵丈夫千万不可靠近，快逃，趁着还来得及赶快远走高飞；然而她心里又无比清楚，一旦自己发出呼救，他定会不顾一切飞奔至她身旁。前一刻她还渴望他在身边——下一刻她又巴不得他远在百里之外。

#### IV / 第四节
===Original===

In the taproom a more ordered medley of sounds had followed on the wild pandemonium of awhile ago. Brief, peremptory words of command, steady tramping of feet, loud harsh questions and subdued answers, occasionally a moan or a few words of protest quickly suppressed, came through the partition to Yvonne’s straining ears.
“Your name?”
“Where do you live?”
“Your occupation?”
“That’s enough. Silence. The next.”
“Your name?”
“Where do you live?”
Men, women and even children were being questioned, classified, packed off, God knew whither. Sometimes a child would cry, a man utter an oath, a woman shriek: then would come harsh orders delivered in a gruff voice, more swearing, the grounding of arms and more often than not a dull, flat sound like a blow struck against human flesh, followed by a volley of curses, or a cry of pain.
“Your name?”
“George Amédé Lemoine.”
“Where do you live?”
“In this house.”
“Your occupation?”
“I am the proprietor of the tavern, citizen. I am an honest man and a patriot. The Republic﻿ ﻿…”
“That’s enough.”
“But I protest.”
“Silence. The next.”
All with dreary, ceaseless monotony: and Yvonne like a trapped bird was bruising her wings against the bars of her cage. Outside the window Chauvelin and Martin-Roget were still speaking in whispers: the fowlers were still watching for their prey. The third man had apparently gone away. What went on beyond the range of her prison window﻿—out in the darkness of the night which Yvonne’s aching eyes could not pierce﻿—she, the miserable watcher, the bait set here to catch the noble game, could not even conjecture. The window was small and her vision was further obstructed by heavy bars. She could see nothing﻿—hear nothing save those two men talking in whispers. Now and again she caught a few words:
“A little while longer, citizen﻿ ﻿… you lose nothing by waiting. Your Kernogans are safe enough. Paul Friche has assured you that the landing where they are now has no egress save through the taproom, and to the floor above. Wait at least until commandant Fleury has got the crowd together, after which he will send his Marats to search the house. It won’t be too late then to lay hands on your aristos, if in the meanwhile﻿ ﻿…”
“ ’Tis futile to wait,” here interrupted Martin-Roget roughly, “and you are a fool, citizen, if you think that those Englishmen exist elsewhere than in your imagination.”
“Hark!”

===Chinese===

大堂里，方才那种近乎狂乱的鼎沸喧嚣渐渐平息，取而代之的是一阵更有条理的杂乱声浪。简短专断的号令声、整齐沉重的脚步声、粗粝严苛的盘问与低声下气的应答，其间偶尔夹杂着几声呻吟或转瞬被压制下去的抗议，穿过隔板传进伊冯娜屏息倾听的耳中。
“姓名？”
“住在哪里？”
“职业？”
“够了。闭嘴。下一个。”
“姓名？”
“住在哪里？”
男人、女人甚至小孩都在被逐一盘问、分类，然后被押送离开，天晓得要押往何处。时而传来孩童的啼哭、男人的咒骂、女人的尖叫；随之而来的便是粗鲁嗓门发出的严厉训斥、更多的喝骂、枪托顿地的沉闷声响，以及往往伴随而来的拳脚落在皮肉上的沉闷钝击声，紧接着便是一连串咒骂或痛苦的哀号。
“姓名？”
“乔治·阿梅代·勒穆瓦纳。”
“住在哪里？”
“就住这所房子里。”
“职业？”
“我是这家酒馆的老板，公民。我是老实人，是个爱国者。共和国……”
“够了。”
“可我要抗议。”
“闭嘴。下一个。”
一切都在枯燥无休的单调重复中进行着；而伊冯娜宛如一只落入罗网的困鸟，在笼条上撞得羽翼残损、伤痕累累。窗外，肖维兰与马丹-罗热仍在低声耳语，捕鸟人依旧耐心地窥伺着猎物。第三个男人显然已经离去。在她囚室窗户视线所不及的范围之外——在伊冯娜酸痛的双眼无法穿透的沉沉黑夜中——正发生着什么，作为这悲惨的旁观者、作为捕获那高贵猎物的诱饵，她甚至无法凭空揣度。窗户狭小，视野又被粗重的铁栏死死挡住。她什么也看不见——除了那两人低语的窸窣声，什么也听不见。时不时地，几句交谈飘进了她的耳际：
“再等等，公民……多等片刻你毫无损失。你的凯尔诺冈家人跑不了。保罗·弗里什已经向你保证过，他们眼下待的楼梯平台除了通向大堂和楼上房间，再无别的出路。至少等到弗勒里指挥官把大堂里的人集中收拢完毕，之后他就会派他的马拉队员搜查整栋房子。到那时再抓拿你的贵族佬也不迟，要是这当口……”
“等下去毫无意义，”马丹-罗热粗暴地打断道，“公民，你要是以为那些英国佬除了存在于你的臆想之中还能在别处冒出来，那你就是个蠢货。”
“听！”

===Original===

broke in the gentle voice abruptly and with forceful command.
And as Yvonne too in instinctive response to that peremptory call was further straining her every sense in order to listen, there came from somewhere, not very far away, right through the stillness of the night, a sound which caused her pulses to still their beating and her throat to choke with the cry which rose from her breast.
It was only the sound of a quaint and drawly voice saying loudly and in English:
“Egad, Tony! ain’t you getting demmed sleepy?”
Just for the space of two or three seconds Yvonne had remained quite still while this unexpected sound sent its dulcet echo on the wings of the northwesterly blast. The next﻿—stumbling in the dark﻿—she had run to the stairs even while she heard Martin-Roget calling loudly and excitedly to Paul Friche.
One reverent pause beside her dead father, one mute prayer commending his soul to the mercy of his Maker, one agonised entreaty to God to protect her beloved and his friend, and then she ran swiftly up the winding steps.
At the top of the stairs, immediately in front of her, a door﻿—slightly ajar﻿—showed a feeble light through its aperture. Yvonne pushed the door further open and slipped into the room beyond. She did not pause to look round but went straight to the window and throwing open the rickety sash she peeped out. For the moment she felt that she would gladly have bartered away twenty years of her life to know exactly whence had come that quaint and drawling voice. She leaned far out of the window trying to see. It gave on the side of the Rat Mort over against Louise Adet’s house﻿—the space below seemed to her to be swarming with men: there were hurried and whispered calls﻿—orders were given to stand at close attention, whilst Martin-Roget had apparently been questioning Paul Friche, for Yvonne heard the latter declare emphatically:
“I am certain that it came either from inside the house or from the roof. And with your permission, citizen, I would like to make assurance doubly sure.”
Then one of the men must suddenly have caught sight of the vague silhouette leaning out of the window, for Martin-Roget and Friche uttered a simultaneous cry, whilst Chauvelin said hurriedly:
“You are right, citizen, something is going on inside the house.”
“What can we do?” queried Martin-Roget excitedly.
“Nothing for the moment but wait.

===Chinese===

那个温和的声音骤然厉声打断，带着不容抗拒的威严。
伊冯娜在那声专断喝止的本能驱使下，也愈发屏息凝神倾听起来；就在这时，从不远处的某处，穿透寂静的夜色，传来了一声呼唤，那声音令她脉搏骤停，胸中涌起的呼喊刹那间哽在喉头。
那不过是一阵古怪而带着慵懒拖腔的话音，用响亮的英语说道：
“我说，托尼！你难道不觉得困得要命吗？”
在这出人意料的声响乘着西北狂风的羽翼送来悦耳回响的短短两三秒间，伊冯娜整个人僵在原地，动弹不得。紧接着——她在黑暗中跌跌撞撞地奔向楼梯，耳边同时传来了马丹-罗热高声而激动地呼唤保罗·弗里什的喊声。
她在死去的父亲身旁虔诚地伫立了一瞬，默默祈祷将他的灵魂托付给仁慈的造物主，痛苦地恳求上帝护佑她的挚爱及其友人，随即飞快地顺着蜿蜒的楼梯向上奔去。
在楼梯顶端正前方，一扇半掩的门缝里透出一缕微弱的光亮。伊冯娜推开房门，悄步闪身溜进屋里。她无暇顾盼打量四周，径直奔向窗前，猛地推开摇摇欲坠的窗扇探头向外窥视。在那一刻，她觉得就算用自己二十年的寿命去换取弄清那古怪慵懒的语调究竟发自何处，她也心甘情愿。她极力探出身子向外张望。窗外对着死耗子酒馆侧面、正对着路易丝·阿代家的宅子——在她看来，下方空地上似乎密密麻麻挤满了人；四下里传来急促压低的呼唤声——人们正下达着保持严密警戒的口令，而马丹-罗热显然正在盘问保罗·弗里什，因为伊冯娜听到后者斩钉截铁地宣称：
“我敢断定，那声音要么是从房子里发出来的，要么就是在屋顶上。只要您允许，公民，我想上去查个水落石出，好做到万无一失。”
这时，底下必定有人突然瞥见了趴在窗口的模糊剪影，马丹-罗热和弗里什同时惊呼出声，而肖维兰则急忙说道：
“你说得对，公民，屋子里确实有动静。”
“我们该怎么办？”马丹-罗热急躁地追问。
“眼下什么也做不了，唯有等待。

===Original===

The Englishmen are caught sure enough like rats in their holes.”
“Wait!” ejaculated Martin-Roget with a savage oath, “wait! always wait! while the quarry slips through one’s fingers.”
“It shall not slip through mine,” retorted Paul Friche. “I was a steeplejack by trade in my day: it won’t be the first time that I have climbed the side of a house by the gutter-pipe. *A moi* Jean-Pierre,” he added, “and may I be drowned in the Loire if between us two we do not lay those cursed English spies low.”
“An hundred francs for each of you,” called Chauvelin lustily, “if you succeed.”
Yvonne did not think to close the window again. Vigorous shouting and laughter from below testified that that hideous creature Friche and his mate had put their project in immediate execution; she turned and ran down the stairs﻿—feeling now like an animal at bay; by the time that she had reached the bottom, she heard a prolonged, hoarse cry of triumph from below and guessed that Paul Friche and his mate had reached the windowsill: the next moment there was a crash overhead of broken window-glass and of furniture kicked from one end of the room to the other, immediately followed by the sound of heavy footsteps running helter-skelter down the stairs.
Yvonne, half-crazed with terror, faint and sick, fell unconscious over the body of her father.

===Chinese===

那些英国佬已然如瓮中之鳖，插翅难飞了。”
“等！”马丹-罗热恶狠狠地骂了一句粗话，“等！总是等！眼睁睁看着猎物从指缝溜走！”
“从我手里绝对溜不掉，”保罗·弗里什反驳道，“我当年可是干修尖顶行当的，顺着落水管爬外墙对我来说可不是头一遭。让-皮埃尔，跟我上！”他接着喊道，“要是我俩拿不下那帮该死的英国间谍，就罚我被沉进卢瓦尔河里淹死！”
“要是你们得手了，”肖维兰扯着嗓子喊道，“每人赏一百法郎！”
伊冯娜根本无暇去重新关上窗户。楼下传来震天的呐喊与哄笑，证明弗里什那个丑陋的家伙和他的同伙已然立即付诸行动；她转身狂奔下楼——此刻的她宛如一头穷途末路的困兽；等她跑到楼梯底部时，楼下传来一声拉长的沙哑胜利欢呼，她猜到保罗·弗里什和他的搭档已经攀上了窗台；紧接着头顶上方传来窗玻璃粉碎的巨响，家具被踢得从房间这一头乱飞到那一头，旋即便是一阵慌乱沉重的脚步声顺着楼梯咚咚咚狂奔而下。
极度惊恐之下，伊冯娜头晕目眩，昏死过去栽倒在父亲的遗体之上。

#### V / 第五节
===Original===

Inside the taproom commandant Fleury was still at work.
“Your name?”
“Where do you live?”
“Your occupation?”
The low room was filled to suffocation: the walls lined with Marats, the doors and windows which were wide open were closely guarded, whilst in the corner of the room, huddled together like bales of rubbish, was the human cattle that had been driven together, preparatory to being sent for a trial to Paris in vindication of Carrier’s brutalities against the city.
Fleury for form’s sake made entries in a notebook﻿—the whole thing was a mere farce﻿—these wretched people were not likely to get a fair trial﻿—what did the whole thing matter? Still! the commandant of the Marats went solemnly through the farce which Carrier had invented with a view to his own justification.
Lemoine and his wife had protested and been silenced: men had struggled and women had fought﻿—some of them like wild cats﻿—in trying to get away. Now there were only half a dozen or so more to docket. Fleury swore, for he was tired and hot.
“This place is like a pesthouse,” he said.
Just then came the sound of that lusty cry of triumph from outside, followed by all the clatter and the breaking of window glass.
“What’s that?” queried Fleury.
The heavy footsteps running down the stairs caused him to look up from his work and to call briefly to a sergeant of the Marats who stood beside his chair:
“Go and see what that *sacré* row is about,” he commanded. “In there,” he added as he indicated the door of the landing with a jerk of the head.
But before the man could reach the door, it was thrown open from within with a vigorous kick from the point of a sabot, and Paul Friche appeared under the lintel with the aristo wench thrown over his shoulder like a sack of potatoes, his thick, muscular arms encircling her knees. His scarlet bonnet was cocked over one eye, his face was smeared with dirt, his breeches were torn at the knees, his shirt hung in strips from his powerful shoulders. Behind him his mate﻿—who had climbed up the gutter-pipe into the house in his wake﻿—was tottering under the load of the ci-devant duc de Kernogan’s body which he had slung across his back and was holding on to by the wrists.
Fleury jumped to his feet﻿—the appearance of these two men, each with his burden, caused him to frown with anger and to demand peremptorily: “What is the meaning of this?”

===Chinese===

大堂内，弗勒里指挥官仍在审讯登记。
“姓名？”
“住在哪里？”
“职业？”
低矮的屋子里闷热得令人窒息：沿墙排满了马拉连队员，大敞的门窗都被重兵严密把守，而房间角落里，像一堆破烂杂物般挤作一团的，正是被驱赶到一处的牛马般的人群，预备被押往巴黎受审，以此为卡里耶在全城施行的暴行寻找托词。
弗勒里在记事本上一笔一划走着过场——整出戏码不过是一场荒唐的闹剧——这些可怜人绝不可能获得公正的审判——这一切又有何相干？然而，这位马拉连指挥官依然煞有介事地执行着卡里耶为替自己开脱而编造的闹剧。
勒穆瓦纳夫妇曾大声抗议，但旋即被喝令噤声；男人们曾拼命挣扎，女人们为了逃脱也曾激烈反抗——有的简直像发了疯的野猫一般抓挠厮打。如今只剩下六七个人需要登记了。弗勒里又累又热，不由得骂骂咧咧起来。
“这地方简直跟瘟疫隔离房一样，”他抱怨道。
恰在此时，外面传来了那声嘹亮的胜利欢呼，紧接着便是一阵嘈杂响动与窗玻璃碎裂的破裂声。
“怎么回事？”弗勒里质问道。
顺着楼梯咚咚跑下来的沉重脚步声引得他停下手头的公事抬起头来，简短地向站在椅子旁的马拉连中士喝道：
“去看看那帮该死的到底在吵吵什么，”他命令道，“就在那里面，”说着他偏了偏头，示意通往楼梯平台的那扇门。
然而中士还没来得及走到门前，门便被一只木屐从里面狠狠一脚踹开，保罗·弗里什出现在门楣之下，肩上像扛麻袋似地扛着那个贵族姑娘，粗壮肌肉贲起的手臂死死箍着她的双膝。他的小红帽歪扣在一只眼睛上，脸上抹满污泥，马裤膝盖处撕破了口子，衬衫被扯成一条条挂在宽阔结实的肩膀上。在他身后，紧随着他顺着落水管爬进屋里的搭档，正背着前凯尔诺冈公爵的尸体踉跄而行，双手紧紧扣着死者的手腕。
弗勒里猛地跳了起来——这两个男人各背一具躯体突然现身，惹得他怒容满面，厉声质问道：“这是什么意思？”

===Original===

“The aristos,” said Paul Friche curtly; “they were trying to escape.”
He strode into the room, carrying the unconscious form of the girl as if it were a load of feathers. He was a huge, massive-looking giant: the girl’s shoulders nearly touched the low ceiling as he swung forward facing the angry commandant.
“How did you get into the house? and by whose orders?” demanded Fleury roughly.
“Climbed in by the window, *pardi*,” retorted the man, “and by the orders of citizen Martin-Roget.”
“A corporal of the Company Marat takes orders only from me; you should know that, citizen Friche.”
“Nay!” interposed the sergeant quickly, “this man is not a corporal of the Company Marat, citizen commandant. As for Corporal Friche, why! he was taken to the infirmary some hours ago with a cracked skull, he.﻿ ﻿…”
“Not Corporal Friche,” exclaimed Fleury with an oath, “then who in the devil’s name is this man?”
“The Scarlet Pimpernel, at your service, citizen commandant,” came loudly and with a merry laugh from the pseudo Friche.
And before either Fleury or the sergeant or any of the Marats could even begin to realise what was happening, he had literally bounded across the room, and as he did so he knocked against the hanging lamp which fell with a crash to the floor, scattering oil and broken glass in every direction and by its fall plunging the place into total darkness. At once there arose a confusion and medley of terrified screams, of piercing shrieks from the women and the children, and of loud imprecations from the men. These mingled with the hasty words of command, with quick orders from Fleury and the sergeant, with the grounding of arms and the tramping of many feet, and with the fall of human bodies that happened to be in the way of the reckless adventurer and his flight.
“He is through the door,” cried the men who had been there on guard.
“After him then!” shouted Fleury. “Curse you all for cowards and for fools.”
The order had no need to be repeated. The confusion, though great, had only been momentary. Within a second or less, Fleury and his sergeant had fought their way through to the door, urging the men to follow.
“After him﻿ ﻿… quick!﻿ ﻿… he is heavily loaded﻿ ﻿… he cannot have got far﻿ ﻿…” commanded Fleury as soon as he had crossed the threshold. “Sergeant, keep order within, and on your life see that no one else escapes.”

===Chinese===

“是贵族佬，”保罗·弗里什简短地答道，“他们想逃跑。”
他大步流星跨进屋里，扛着昏迷不醒的姑娘宛如扛着一堆轻飘飘的羽毛。他身材魁梧伟岸，宛如巨人；随着他阔步向前直面怒火中烧的指挥官，姑娘的肩头几乎碰到了低矮的天花板。
“你们是怎么进这栋屋子的？奉了谁的命令？”弗勒里粗声质问。
“从窗户爬进来的，这还用问，”那人回敬道，“奉了马丹-罗热公民的命令。”
“马拉连的下士只听从我的命令；弗里什公民，这点你应当一清二楚。”
“不对！”中士急忙插言道，“指挥官公民，这个人根本不是马拉连的下士。至于弗里什下士，嗨！他几个小时前被人打裂了脑袋，早就送进医务所了，他……”
“不是弗里什下士？”弗勒里骂了句粗话惊呼道，“那这家伙到底是何方神圣？！”
“红花侠，听凭差遣，指挥官公民，”假弗里什放声大笑，响亮地回答道。
还没等弗勒里、中士或任何一名马拉连队员反应过来究竟发生了什么，他整个人便如离弦之箭般凌空跃过整个大堂，飞身跃过之际顺势猛撞在吊灯上；吊灯哗啦一声巨响砸落在地，灯油与碎玻璃四处飞溅，随着吊灯坠毁，整个屋子瞬间陷入了一片漆黑。刹那间，恐惧的尖叫声、妇孺撕心裂肺的哭嚎声，以及男人们暴怒的诅咒声混作一团，场面登时大乱。这些混乱的声浪与仓促的号令声、弗勒里和中士急促的喝斥声、枪托顿地的撞击声以及杂乱奔跑的脚步声交织在一起，其间还夹杂着挡在这位亡命冒险家逃跑路线上的人被狠狠撞翻倒地的闷响。
“他冲出门去了！”原本守在门口的卫兵大声喊叫。
“那还不快追！”弗勒里怒吼道，“真是一帮该死的窝囊废、蠢货！”
这道命令根本无需重申。混乱虽剧烈，却不过转瞬之间。短短一两秒内，弗勒里和他的中士已然杀开一条血路冲到了门口，厉声喝令士兵们紧随其后。
“快追上去……动作快！……他身上背着重物……跑不了多远……”弗勒里刚跨过门槛便高声命令道，“中士，维持好屋里的秩序，提防着你的脑袋，绝不能再放跑一个人。”

## IX The Proconsul / 第九章 南特总督
===Original===



===Chinese===

第九章 南特总督

#### I / 第一节
===Original===

From round the angle of the house Martin-Roget and Chauvelin were already speeding along at a rapid pace.
“What does it all mean?” queried the latter hastily.
“The Englishman﻿—with the wench on his back? have you seen him?”
“Malediction! what do you mean?”
“Have you seen him?” reiterated Fleury hoarsely.
“No.”
“He couldn’t have passed you?”
“Impossible.”
“Then unless some of us here have eyes like cats that limb of Satan will get away. On to him, my men,” he called once more. “Can you see him?”
The darkness outside was intense. The northwesterly wind was whistling down the narrow street, drowning the sound of every distant footfall: it tore mercilessly round the men’s heads, snatching the bonnets from off their heads, dragging at their loose shirts and breeches, adding to the confusion which already reigned.
“He went this way﻿ ﻿…” shouted one.
“No! that!” cried another.
“There he is!” came finally in chorus from several lusty throats. “Just crossing the bridge.”
“After him,” cried Fleury, “an hundred francs to the man who first lays hands on that devil.”
Then the chase began. The Englishman on ahead was unmistakable with that burden on his shoulder. He had just reached the foot of the bridge where a street lantern fixed on a tall bracket on the corner stone had suddenly thrown him into bold relief. He had less than an hundred metres start of his pursuers and with a wild cry of excitement they started in his wake.
He was now in the middle of the bridge﻿—an unmistakable figure of a giant vaguely silhouetted against the light from the lanterns on the further end of the bridge﻿—seeming preternaturally tall and misshapen with that hump upon his back.
From right and left, from under the doorways of the houses in the Carrefour de la Poissonnerie the Marats who had been left on guard in the street now joined in the chase. Overhead windows were thrown open﻿—the good burghers of Nantes, awakened from their sleep, forgetful for the nonce of all their anxieties, their squalor and their miseries, leaned out to see what this new kind of din might mean. From everywhere﻿—it almost seemed as if some sprang out of the earth﻿—men, either of the town-guard or Marats on patrol duty, or merely idlers and night hawks who happened to be about, yielded to that primeval instinct of brutality which causes men as well as beasts to join in a pursuit against a fellow creature.

===Chinese===

马丹-罗热和肖维兰绕过房角，已经迈开大步飞奔赶来。
“这究竟是怎么回事？”后者急促地问道。
“那个英国佬——背着那个丫头？你们看见他了吗？”
“该死！你这是什么意思？”
“你们到底看见他没有？”弗勒里声音嘶哑地又问了一遍。
“没看见。”
“他不可能从你们身边溜过去吧？”
“绝不可能。”
“那么除非我们当中有人生着猫一样的眼睛，否则那个撒旦的孽障就要溜之大吉了。弟兄们，给我追！”他再次高喊，“你们能看见他吗？”
门外夜色漆黑如墨。西北风顺着狭窄的街道呼啸而过，掩盖了远处一切脚步声；寒风无情地在众人的头上盘旋肆虐，扯掉了他们头上的便帽，撕扯着宽松的衬衣和马裤，令本已一片混乱的场面更加不可开交。
“他往这边跑了……”有人喊道。
“不对！是那边！”另一个人叫嚷。
“他在那儿！”几副粗门大嗓终于异口同声地吼了起来，“刚上了桥！”
“追上去！”弗勒里大喊，“谁第一个逮住那个恶魔，赏一百法郎！”
追捕随即展开。前方的英国佬背着那份重负，身形极易辨认。他刚到桥头，墙角基石高支架上悬挂的一盏街灯骤然将他的身影清晰地勾勒出来。他领先追兵不足一百米，众人发出一阵狂乱而兴奋的呐喊，紧随其后猛扑上去。
此时他已到了桥中央——巨大的身躯隐隐衬在桥对头街灯的光晕中，格外显眼——因为背上驮着重负，显得异常高大且畸形怪异。
在鱼市十字街口，留在街上放哨的马拉连士兵从左右两旁、从沿街房屋的门廊下纷纷涌出，加入了这场追逐。头顶上一扇扇窗户被猛地推开——南特的好市民们从睡梦中惊醒，暂时抛下了所有的焦虑、潦倒与苦难，探出身来想瞧瞧这阵喧嚣究竟是怎么回事。四面八方——简直就像是从地底下冒出来似的——市民卫队、巡逻的马拉连士兵，乃至恰好在附近的闲汉游民与夜猫子，全都屈从于那种驱使人与走兽一同围猎同类的原始残忍本能，纷纷加入了追捕。

===Original===

Fleury was in the rear of his posse. Martin-Roget and Chauvelin, walking as rapidly as they could by his side, tried to glean some information out of the commandant’s breathless and scrappy narrative:
“What happened exactly?”
“It was the man Paul Friche﻿ ﻿… with the aristo wench on his back﻿ ﻿… and another man carrying the ci-devant aristo﻿ ﻿… they were the English spies﻿ ﻿… in disguise﻿ ﻿… they knocked over the lamp﻿ ﻿… and got away﻿ ﻿…”
“Name of a﻿ ﻿…”
“No use swearing, citizen Martin-Roget,” retorted Fleury as hotly as his agitated movements would allow. “You and citizen Chauvelin are responsible for the affair. It was you, citizen Chauvelin, who placed Paul Friche inside that tavern in observation﻿—you told him what to do﻿ ﻿…”
“Well?”
“Paul Friche﻿—the real Paul Friche﻿—was taken to the infirmary some hours ago﻿ ﻿… with a cracked skull, dealt him by your Englishman, I’ve no doubt﻿ ﻿…”
“Impossible,” reiterated Chauvelin with a curse.
“Impossible? why impossible?”
“The man I spoke to outside Le Bouffay﻿ ﻿…”
“Was not Paul Friche.”
“He was on guard in the Place with two other Marats.”
“He was not Paul Friche﻿—the others were not Marats.”
“Then the man who was inside the tavern?﻿ ﻿…”
“Was not Paul Friche.”
“… who climbed the gutter pipe﻿ ﻿… ?”
“Malediction!”
And the chase continued﻿—waxing hotter every minute. The hare had gained slightly on the hounds﻿—there were more than a hundred hot on the trail by now﻿—having crossed the bridge he was on the Isle Feydeau, and without hesitating a moment he plunged at once into the network of narrow streets which cover the island in the rear of La Petite Hollande and the Hôtel de le Villestreux, where lodged Carrier, the representative of the people. The hounds after him had lost some ground by halting﻿—if only for a second or two﻿—first at the head of the bridge, then at the corners of the various streets, while they peered into the darkness to see which way had gone that fleet-footed hare.
“Down this way!”
“No! That!”
“There he goes!”
It always took a few seconds to decide, during which the man on ahead with his burden on his shoulder had time mayhap to reach the end of a street and to turn a corner and once again to plunge into darkness and out of sight.

===Chinese===

弗勒里落在他的追捕队后方。马丹-罗热和肖维兰尽量加快步伐紧随在他身旁，试图从指挥官上气不接下气、支离破碎的叙述中探听些实情：
“究竟出了什么事？”
“是保罗·弗里什那个家伙……背着那个贵族丫头……还有另一个家伙抬着那个前贵族……他们是英国间谍……乔装改扮的……他们打翻了油灯……溜掉了……”
“真他妈的……”
“骂人也没用，马丹-罗热公民，”弗勒里在慌乱的奔跑中上气不接下气地反驳道，“这事儿你和肖维兰公民脱不了干系。是你，肖维兰公民，把保罗·弗里什安插进那家酒馆暗中监视的——是你吩咐他该怎么办的……”
“那又怎样？”
“保罗·弗里什——真正的保罗·弗里什——几个小时前就被送进医务所了……脑袋让人给开了瓢，准保是你们那个英国佬下的手……”
“绝不可能，”肖维兰咒骂着又重复了一遍。
“绝不可能？怎么不可能？”
“我在布费城楼外跟他说过话的那个人……”
“根本就不是保罗·弗里什。”
“当时他在广场上跟另外两名马拉连士兵一起站岗。”
“他不是保罗·弗里什——另外两个也不是马拉连士兵。”
“那么在酒馆里面的那个家伙？……”
“也不是保罗·弗里什。”
“……那个爬水漏管的……？”
“该死！”
追捕仍在继续——气氛每分每秒都愈发白热化。野兔渐渐拉开了与猎犬的一点距离——此时已有百余人紧随其后穷追不舍——渡过桥后，他已经登上了费多岛，毫不迟疑地一头扎进了纵横交错的狭窄街巷中。这片街巷密布在小荷兰广场与人民代表卡里耶下榻的维勒斯特勒府邸后方。跟在后面的猎犬们因为偶尔的停顿——哪怕仅仅一两秒钟——而落后了些许：先是在桥头，继而在各个街角，他们必须瞪大眼睛在黑暗中辨认那只身手矫健的野兔究竟逃向了何方。
“往这边！”
“不对！是那边！”
“他往那儿跑了！”
每一次决断总要耽搁几秒钟，而在这须臾之间，前方肩负重荷的男人或许已经跑到了街尾，拐过弯角，再次隐没在夜色中不见了踪影。

===Original===

The street lanterns were few in this squalid corner of the city, and it was only when perforce the running hare had to cross a circle of light that the hounds were able to keep hot on the trail.
“To the bridges for your lives!” now shouted Fleury to the men nearest to him. “Leave him to wander on the island. He cannot come off it, unless he jumps into the Loire.”
The Marats﻿—intelligent and ferociously keen on the chase﻿—had already grasped the importance of this order: with the bridges guarded that fleet-footed Englishman might run as much as he liked, he was bound to be run to earth like a fox in his burrow. In a moment they had dispersed along the quays, some to one bridgehead, some to another﻿—the Englishman could not double back now, and if he had already crossed to the Isle Gloriette, which was not joined to the left bank of the river by any bridge, he would be equally caught like a rat in a trap.
“Unless he jumps into the Loire,” reiterated Fleury triumphantly.
“The proconsul will have more excitement than he hoped for,” he added with a laugh. “He was looking forward to the capture of the English spy, and in deadly terror lest he escaped. But now meseems that we shall run our fox down in sight of the very gates of la Villestreux.”
Martin-Roget’s thoughts ran on Yvonne and the duc.
“You will remember, citizen commandant,” he contrived to say to Fleury, “that the ci-devant Kernogans were found inside the Rat Mort.”
Fleury uttered an exclamation of rough impatience. What did he, what did anyone care at this moment for a couple of aristos more or less when the noblest game that had ever fallen to the bag of any Terrorist was so near being run to earth? But Chauvelin said nothing. He walked on at a brisk pace, keeping close to commandant Fleury’s side, in the immediate wake of the pursuit. His lips were pressed tightly together and a hissing breath came through his wide-open nostrils. His pale eyes were fixed into the darkness and beyond it, where the most bitter enemy of the cause which he loved was fighting his last battle against Fate.

===Chinese===

在这座城市的这处污秽角落里，街灯寥寥无几，唯有当奔逃的野兔不得不穿过光晕时，猎犬们才能咬住踪迹穷追不舍。
“拼了命也得给我守住桥！”弗勒里这时冲离他最近的手下吼道，“随他在岛上乱窜去吧。只要他不跳进卢瓦尔河，就休想从岛上逃出去！”
马拉连士兵机敏且嗜血狂热，立刻领会了这道命令的关键所在：只要把守住各座桥梁，那个脚底生风的英国佬爱怎么逃就怎么逃，迟早得像钻进洞里的狐狸一样被瓮中捉鳖。转瞬之间，他们便沿着码头四散开去，有的扑向这座桥头，有的奔向另一处桥头——英国佬此刻已无法折返；即便他已经穿过桥到了格洛列特岛，由于那座岛与河流左岸没有任何桥梁相连，他也同样逃不出手掌心，犹如瓮中之鳖。
“除非他跳进卢瓦尔河。”弗勒里洋洋得意地又强调了一遍。
“南特总督这下可有的刺激了，远超他的预料，”他大笑着补充道，“他一心盼着生擒英国间谍，又吓得要死生怕对方溜走。可眼下依我看，咱们要在维勒斯特勒府邸大门眼皮底下把咱们的狐狸按倒在地了。”
马丹-罗热的心思却全在伊冯娜和公爵身上。
“指挥官公民，你可别忘了，”他好不容易插上一句对弗勒里说道，“前凯尔诺冈家的人是在死耗子酒馆里被搜出来的。”
弗勒里粗鲁而不耐烦地咕哝了一声。在这一刻，有史以来落入恐怖分子猎网中最尊贵显赫的猎物眼看就要被逼入绝境，他何曾在乎、又有谁会在乎多一个少一个贵族？肖维兰却一言不发。他迈着急促的步子，紧紧跟在弗勒里指挥官身旁，寸步不离地尾随着追捕队伍。他双唇紧闭，张大的鼻孔中呼哧呼哧地喘着粗气。他那双灰白的眼睛死死盯进夜色深处，越过黑暗，投向他所挚爱之事业的宿敌——那个正与命运展开最后搏杀的人。

#### II / 第二节
===Original===

“He cannot get off the island!” Fleury had said awhile ago. Well! there was of a truth little or nothing now between the hunted hare and capture. The bridges were well guarded: the island swarming with hounds, the Marats at their posts and the Loire an impassable barrier all round.
And Chauvelin, the most tenacious enemy man ever had, Fleury keen on a reward and Martin-Roget with a private grudge to pay off, all within two hundred yards behind him.
True for the moment the Englishman had disappeared. Burden and all, the gloom appeared to have swallowed him up. But there was nowhere he could go; mayhap he had taken refuge under a doorway in one of the narrow streets and hoped perhaps under cover of the darkness to allow his pursuers to slip past him and then to double back.
Fleury was laughing in the best of humours. He was gradually collecting all the Marats together and sending them to the bridgeheads under the command of their various sergeants. Let the Englishman spend the night on the islands if he had a mind. There was a full company of Marats here to account for him as soon as he attempted to come out in the open.
The idlers and night hawks as well as the municipal town guard continued to run excitedly up and down the streets﻿—sometimes there would come a lusty cry from a knot of pursuers who thought they spied the Englishman through the darkness, at others there would be a call of halt, and feverish consultation held at a street corner as to the best policy to adopt.
The town guard, jealous of the Marats, were pining to lay hands on the English spy for the sake of the reward. Fleury, coming across their provost, called him a fool for his pains.
“My Marats will deal with the English spies, citizen,” he said roughly, “he is no concern of yours.”
The provost demurred: an altercation might have ensued when Chauvelin’s suave voice poured oil on the troubled waters.
“Why not,” he said, “let the town guard continue their search on the island, citizen commandant? The men may succeed in digging our rat out of his hole and forcing him out into the open all the sooner. Your Marats will have him quickly enough after that.”
To this suggestion the provost gave a grudging assent. The reward when the English spy was caught could be fought for later on. For the nonce he turned unceremoniously on his heel, and left Fleury cursing him for a meddlesome busybody.

===Chinese===

“他绝不可能逃出这座岛！”弗勒里方才如是说。千真万确！在这只被追捕的野兔与束手就擒之间，如今确实已无任何屏障。各座桥梁严密布控，岛上猎犬云集，马拉连士兵各就各位，卢瓦尔河更在四周构筑了一道不可逾越的屏障。
而肖维兰这个世所罕有的顽固死敌、一心贪图赏金的弗勒里，以及誓报私仇的马丹-罗热，全都紧跟在身后两百码之内。
诚然，这一刻那个英国佬暂时不见了踪影。连人带背负的重物，似乎全被这片漆黑给吞没了。但他根本无处可逃；或许他正躲在狭窄街巷中某扇门洞下，指望借着夜色的掩护让追兵从身边擦身而过，然后再折返回去。
弗勒里心情大好，放声大笑。他渐渐将所有马拉连士兵集合起来，派各排军士率领他们分守各个桥头。随那个英国佬高兴，就让他在岛上呆上一整夜吧。这里驻扎着一整连马拉连士兵，只要他胆敢露面，立刻就能叫他死无葬身之地。
游民、夜猫子以及市政卫队依然情绪亢奋地在沿街各处四处乱窜——有时，一伙追兵以为在黑暗中隐约窥见了英国佬的身影，便爆发出一阵狂呼；有时又是一声喝止，众人在街角仓促聚首，急躁地商讨该采取何种对策。
市政卫队嫉妒马拉连，为了丰厚的赏金，急不可耐地想抢先抓住英国间谍。弗勒里撞见他们的卫队长，没好气地骂对方自讨没趣，是个傻瓜。
“我的马拉连会收拾那些英国间谍，公民，”他粗鲁地说道，“用不着你们瞎操心。”
卫队长满腹不情愿，双方眼看就要爆发口角，肖维兰那圆润圆滑的嗓音及时平息了这场风波。
“为什么不呢，”他说道，“指挥官公民，何不让市政卫队继续在岛上搜查？弟兄们或许能把咱们的老鼠从地洞里掘出来，逼得他更快现身原形。到那时，你的马拉连自会手到擒来。”
卫队长勉强接受了这个提议。抓获英国间谍后的赏金归属，大可留待日后再去争个高下。眼下他毫不客气地一转身走开了，留下弗勒里在背后咒骂他是个多管闲事的讨厌鬼。

===Original===

“So long as he and his rabble does not interfere with my Marats,” growled the commandant.
“Will you see your sergeants, citizen?” queried Chauvelin tentatively. “They will have to keep very much on the alert, and will require constant prodding to their vigilance. If I can be of any service﻿ ﻿…”
“No,” retorted Fleury curtly, “you and citizen Martin-Roget had best try and see the proconsul and tell him what we have done.”
“He’ll be half wild with terror when he hears that the English spy is at large upon the island.”
“You must pacify him as best you can. Tell him I have a score of Marats at every bridge head and that I am looking personally to every arrangement. There is no escape for the devil possible save by drowning himself and the wench in the Loire.”

===Chinese===

“只要他和他的乌合之众别碍我马拉连手下的事就行，”指挥官嘟囔道。
“你要去巡视军士们吗，公民？”肖维兰试探性地问道，“他们必须保持高度警惕，需要不时督促敲打。如果我能帮上什么忙……”
“不必，”弗勒里生硬地回绝道，“你和马丹-罗热公民最好去见见南特总督，向他汇报咱们的部署。”
“他要是听说那个英国间谍正在岛上四处流窜，非得吓得半死不可。”
“你们得想方设法安抚好他。告诉他，我在每座桥头都安排了二十来名马拉连士兵，所有布防我都亲自过问把关。除非那个恶魔带着那个丫头投进卢瓦尔河自尽，否则绝无逃脱的可能。”

#### II / 第三节
===Original===

Chauvelin and Martin-Roget turned from the quay on to the Petite Hollande﻿—the great open ground with its converging row of trees which ends at the very apex of the Isle of Feydeau. Opposite to them at the further corner of the Place was the Hôtel de la Villestreux. One or two of the windows in the hotel were lighted from within. No doubt the proconsul was awake, trembling in the remotest angle of his lair, with the spectre of assassination rampant before him﻿—aroused by the continued disturbance of the night, by the feverishness of this manhunt carried on almost at his gates.
Even through the darkness it was easy to perceive groups of people either rushing backwards and forwards on the Place or congregating in groups under the trees. Excitement was in the air. It could be felt and heard right through the soughing of the northwesterly wind which caused the bare branches of the trees to groan and to crackle, and the dead leaves, which still hung on the twigs, to fly wildly through the night.
In the centre of the Place, two small lights, gleaming like eyes in the midst of the gloom, betrayed the presence of the proconsul’s coach, which stood there as always, ready to take him away to a place of safety﻿—away from this city where he was mortally hated and dreaded﻿—whenever the spectre of terror became more insistent than usual, and drove him hence out of his stronghold. The horses were pawing the frozen ground and champing their bits﻿—the steam from their nostrils caught the rays of the carriage lamps, which also lit up with a feeble flicker the vague outline of the coachman on his box and of the postilion rigid in his saddle.
The citizens of Nantes were never tired of gaping at the carriage﻿—a huge C-springed barouche﻿—at the coachman’s fine caped coat of bottle-green cloth and at the horses with their handsome harness set off with heavy brass bosses: they never tired of bandying words with the successive coachmen as they mounted their box and gathered up the reins, or with the postilions who loved to crack their whips and to appear smart and well-groomed, in the midst of the squalor which reigned in the terror-stricken city. They were the guardians of the mighty proconsul: on their skill, quickness and presence of mind might depend his precious life.

===Chinese===

肖维兰和马丹-罗热从码头转入小荷兰广场——这片开阔地带两侧树木向内聚拢，直抵费多岛的最顶端。在广场另一侧对角处耸立着维勒斯特勒府邸。府邸内有一两扇窗户透出灯光。毫无疑问，南特总督此刻正醒着，在巢穴最深僻的角落里瑟瑟发抖，眼前浮现出遇刺的幽灵恶梦——这深夜连绵不绝的骚动，这几乎在他门前展开的狂乱围猎，早已将他惊醒。
即便透过沉沉夜色，也能轻易瞧见三五成群的人群，有的在广场上来回奔窜，有的聚在树荫底下。空气中弥漫着躁动不安的气息。在西北风的呼啸声中，这种躁动清晰可感、入耳可闻；寒风吹得光秃秃的树枝呜呜作响、噼啪折断，尚挂在细枝上的枯叶在夜空中狂乱乱舞。
广场中央，两盏微弱的光亮犹如黑暗中的双眼，显露出南特总督马车的踪影。那辆马车一如既往地候在那里，随时准备将他带往安全之所——逃离这座对他恨之入骨又畏之如虎的城市——每当恐惧的幽灵比平日更纠缠不休、逼得他不得不弃守巢穴时，便会派上用场。骏马用蹄子刨着冻结的地面，嘴里嚼着马嚼子——鼻孔中喷出的热气迎着车灯的光束化作白雾，车灯微弱闪烁的微光也映照出车座上车夫模糊的身影，以及鞍座上骑手僵直的姿态。
南特市民对这辆马车百看不厌——那是一辆庞大的C形弹簧四轮大敞篷车——车夫身穿墨绿色呢料斗篷大衣，马匹佩戴着镶有厚实黄铜饰钉的精美马具；他们乐此不疲地同接二连三登上车座、握紧缰绳的车夫搭讪打趣，或是同那些喜欢甩响马鞭、在满城恐怖笼罩的破败萧条中打扮得光鲜亮丽的骑手攀谈。他们是那位权倾一时的南特总督的贴身护卫：其尊贵的性命，或许全仰仗他们的技艺、敏捷与沉着。

===Original===

Even when the shadow of death hangs over an entire community, there will be some who will stand and gape and crack jokes at an uncommon sight.
And now when the pall of night hung over the abode of the man-tiger and his lair, and wrapped in its embrace the hunted and the hunters, there still was a knot of people standing round the carriage﻿—between it and the hotel﻿—gazing with lacklustre eyes on the costly appurtenances wherewith the representative of a wretched people loved to surround himself. They could only see the solid mass of the carriage and of the horses, but they could hear the coachman clicking with his tongue and the postilion cracking his whip, and these sights broke the absolute dreary monotony of their lives.
It was from behind this knot of gaffers that there rose gradually a tumult as of a man calling out in wrath and lashing himself into a fury. Chauvelin and Martin-Roget were just then crossing La Petite Hollande from one bank of the river to the other: they were walking rapidly towards the hotel, when they heard the tumult which presently culminated in a hoarse cry and a volley of oaths.
“My coach! my coach at once﻿ ﻿… Lalouët, don’t leave me﻿ ﻿… Curse you all for a set of cowardly oafs﻿ ﻿… My coach I say﻿ ﻿…”
“The proconsul,” murmured Chauvelin as he hastened forward, Martin-Roget following closely on his heels.
By the time that they had come near enough to the coach to distinguish vaguely in the gloom what was going on, people came rushing to the same spot from end to end of the Place. In a moment there was quite a crowd round the carriage, and the two men had much ado to push their way through by a vigorous play of their elbows.
“Citizen Carrier!” cried Chauvelin at the top of his voice, trying to dominate the hubbub, “one minute﻿ ﻿… I have excellent news for you﻿ ﻿… The English spy﻿ ﻿…”
“Curse you for a set of blundering fools,” came with a husky cry from out the darkness, “you have let that English devil escape﻿ ﻿… I knew it﻿ ﻿… I knew it﻿ ﻿… the assassin is at large﻿ ﻿… the murderer﻿ ﻿… my coach at once﻿ ﻿… my coach﻿ ﻿… Lalouët﻿—do not leave me.”
Chauvelin had by this time succeeded in pushing his way to the forefront of the crowd: Martin-Roget, tall and powerful, had effectually made a way for him.

===Chinese===

即使死亡的阴影笼罩着整个群体，也总会有人驻足看热闹，对着罕见的景致指指点点、插科打诨。
如今，当黑夜的帷幕低垂在人面恶虎的宅邸与其巢穴之上，将猎物与追捕者一同揽入怀抱时，马车周围——在马车与府邸之间——依然围着一小撮闲人，正用呆滞无神的眼睛打量着那些昂贵的排场；这正是那位苦难人民的代表极喜用来装点自身的行头。他们虽只能瞧见马车和骏马沉重敦实的黑影，却能听见车夫砸嘴咂舌的声响与骑手扬鞭的脆响，而这番光景恰恰打破了他们生活中那近乎沉闷窒息的单调。
正是在这群看热闹的闲汉背后，渐渐传来一阵喧闹，仿佛有人在狂怒叫嚷、大发雷霆。肖维兰与马丹-罗热此时正穿过小荷兰广场，从河的一岸奔向另一岸；他们正快步赶往府邸，突然听到了那阵喧哗，紧接着便爆发出一声嘶哑的狂吼和连珠炮般的咒骂。
“我的马车！立刻把我的马车备好……拉卢埃，别离开我……咒死你们这帮胆小如鼠的蠢货……我说备我的马车……”
“南特总督，”肖维兰低声咕哝着加快了脚步，马丹-罗热紧随其后。
等他们走得足够近，能隐约在幽暗中看清端倪时，广场两头的人群纷纷朝这同一个地点聚拢过来。一转眼的工夫，马车四周便围了个水泄不通，两人不得不奋力挥动胳膊肘，好不容易才在人群中挤出一条路来。
“卡里耶公民！”肖维兰扯开嗓子高声大喊，试图压过嘈杂的人声，“稍等片刻……我有绝好的消息要向您禀报……那个英国间谍……”
“咒死你们这帮成事不足的笨蛋！”黑暗中传来一声沙哑的怒嚎，“你们让那个英国恶魔溜了……我就知道……我就知道……刺客还在逍遥法外……那个凶手……立刻备我的马车……我的马车……拉卢埃——别离开我。”
肖维兰这时终于成功挤到了人群最前面；高大强壮的马丹-罗热在前面替他强行开出了一条通道。

===Original===

Through the dense gloom he could see the misshapen form of the proconsul, wildly gesticulating with one arm and with the other clinging convulsively to young Lalouët who already had his hand on the handle of the carriage door.
With a quick, resolute gesture Chauvelin stepped between the door and the advancing proconsul.
“Citizen Carrier,” he said with calm determination, “on my oath there is no cause for alarm. Your life is absolutely safe﻿ ﻿… I entreat you to return to your lodgings﻿ ﻿…”
To emphasise his words he had stretched out a hand and firmly grasped the proconsul’s coat sleeve. This gesture, however, instead of pacifying the apparently terror-stricken maniac, seemed to have the effect of further exasperating his insensate fear. With a loud oath he tore himself free from Chauvelin’s grasp.
“Ten thousand devils,” he cried hoarsely, “who is this fool who dares to interfere with me? Stand aside man﻿ ﻿… stand aside or﻿ ﻿…”
And before Chauvelin could utter another word or Martin-Roget come to his colleague’s rescue, there came the sudden sharp report of a pistol; the horses reared, the crowd was scattered in every direction, Chauvelin was knocked over by a smart blow on the head whilst a vigorous drag on his shoulder alone saved him from falling under the wheels of the coach.
Whilst confusion was at its highest, the carriage door was closed to with a bang and there was a loud, commanding cry hurled through the window at the coachman on his box.
“En avant, citizen coachman! Drive for your life! through the Savenay gate. The English assassins are on our heels.”
The postilion cracked his whip. The horses, maddened by the report, by the pushing, jostling crowd and the confused cries and screams around, plunged forward, wild with excitement. Their hoofs clattered on the hard road. Some of the crowd ran after the coach across the Place, shouting lustily: “The proconsul! the proconsul!”
Chauvelin﻿—dazed and bruised﻿—was picked up by Martin-Roget.
“The cowardly brute!” was all that he said between his teeth, “he shall rue this outrage as soon as I can give my mind to his affairs. In the meanwhile﻿ ﻿…”
The clatter of the horses’ hoofs was already dying away in the distance. For a few seconds longer the rattle of the coach was still accompanied by cries of “The proconsul! the proconsul!”

===Chinese===

透过浓密的幽暗，他能看见南特总督那畸形怪异的身影，一条胳膊狂乱地挥舞着，另一条胳膊死命拽着年轻的拉卢埃；而那少年一只手已经搭在了马车门的把手上。
肖维兰动作敏捷而坚决地迈步上前，挡在车门与迎面走来的南特总督之间。
“卡里耶公民，”他神色从容而坚定地说道，“我敢起誓，绝无惊慌的必要。您的性命万无一失……我恳请您回官邸去……”
为了强调自己的话，他伸出一只手，牢牢抓住了南特总督的衣袖。然而，这个举动不仅没有让这个看似吓破了胆的疯子镇定下来，反而似乎进一步激化了他那不可理喻的恐惧。只听一声刺耳的咒骂，他猛地甩脱了肖维兰的抓握。
“一万个魔鬼！”他声音嘶哑地怒吼道，“是哪个蠢货胆敢拦我的路？滚开，你这家伙……滚开，否则……”
未等肖维兰再说出半个字，也未等马丹-罗热赶来为同僚解围，骤然响起了一声尖锐的手枪枪响；骏马人立而起，人群顿时四散奔逃，肖维兰的头上结结实实挨了一记重击，整个人被当场掀翻在地，多亏有人死命扯了他一把肩膀，才险险免于滚入马车车轮底下。
就在全场乱成一锅粥的节骨眼上，“砰”的一声巨响，车门被狠狠带上，车窗里随即探出一声威严而响亮的怒喝，直冲车座上的车夫砸去：
“前进，车夫公民！拼命赶车！从萨沃奈门冲出去！英国刺客追上来了！”
骑手扬鞭一甩。马匹被枪声、推搡拥挤的人群以及四下里的喧嚣尖叫刺激得近乎发狂，情绪癫狂地向前猛冲。马蹄在坚硬的路面上踏出急促的得得脆响。人群中有些人跟着马车穿过广场追了上去，扯着嗓门高喊：“南特总督！南特总督！”
肖维兰头晕目眩、浑身伤痛，被马丹-罗热从地上扶了起来。
“这个懦弱的畜生！”他咬牙切齿地从牙缝里挤出这么一句，“等我有空腾出手来收拾他的烂摊子，定要叫他为这桩暴行追悔莫及。眼下……”
得得的马蹄声早已在远方渐渐微弱下去。在随后的几秒钟里，马车的辘辘声中依旧隐约夹杂着“南特总督！南特总督！”的呼喊。

===Original===

Fleury at the bridge head, seeing and hearing its approach, had only just time to order his Marats to stand at attention. A salvo should have been fired when the representative of the people, the high and mighty proconsul, was abroad, but there was no time for that, and the coach clattered over the bridge at breakneck speed, whilst Carrier with his head out of the window was hurling anathemas and insults at Fleury for having allowed the paid spies of that cursed British Government to threaten the life of a representative of the people.
“I go to Savenay,” he shouted just at the last, “until that assassin has been thrown in the Loire. But when I return﻿ ﻿… look to yourself commandant Fleury.”
Then the carriage turned down the Quai de la Fosse and a few minutes later was swallowed up by the gloom.

===Chinese===

守在桥头的弗勒里远远望见并听见马车飞驰而来，只来得及下令手下的马拉连士兵立正肃立。按规矩，人民代表、高高在上的南特总督出巡理当鸣枪致敬，但此刻根本来不及照办；马车以极快的速度飞驰过桥，卡里耶探头出窗，劈头盖脸地对着弗勒里破口大骂、百般羞辱，斥责他竟敢放任那个该死的英国政府雇佣的间谍威胁人民代表的性命。
“我去萨沃奈，”他在最后高声怒吼，“直到那个刺客被扔进卢瓦尔河喂鱼为止。可等我回来……你给我小心点，弗勒里指挥官！”
随即，马车拐上了拉福斯码头，几分钟后便被无边夜色彻底吞没。

#### IV / 第四节
===Original===

Chauvelin, supported by Martin-Roget, was hobbling back across the Place. The crowd was still standing about, vaguely wondering why it had got so excited over the departure of the proconsul and the rattle of a coach and pair across the bridge, when on the island there was still an assassin at large﻿—an English spy, the capture of whom would be one of the great events in the chronicles of the city of Nantes.
“I think,” said Martin-Roget, “that we may as well go to bed now, and leave the rest to commandant Fleury. The Englishman may not be captured for some hours, and I for one am overfatigued.”
“Then go to bed an you desire, citizen Martin-Roget,” retorted Chauvelin drily, “I for one will stay here until I see the Englishman in the hands of commandant Fleury.”
“Hark,” interposed Martin-Roget abruptly. “What was that?”
Chauvelin had paused even before Martin-Roget’s restraining hand had rested on his arm. He stood still in the middle of the Place and his knees shook under him so that he nearly fell prone to the ground.
“What is it?” reiterated Martin-Roget with vague puzzlement. “It sounds like young Lalouët’s voice.”
Chauvelin said nothing. He had forgotten his bruises: he no longer hobbled﻿—he ran across the Place to the front of the hotel whence the voice had come which was so like that of young Lalouët.
The youngster﻿—it was undoubtedly he﻿—was standing at the angle of the hotel: above him a lantern threw a dim circle of light on his bare head with its mass of dark curls, and on a small knot of idlers with two or three of the town guard amongst them. The first words spoken by him which Chauvelin distinguished quite clearly were:
“You are all mad﻿ ﻿… or else drunk.﻿ ﻿… The citizen proconsul is upstairs in his room﻿ ﻿… He has just sent me down to hear what news there is of the English spies﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

肖维兰在马丹-罗热的搀扶下一瘸一拐地走回广场。人群依然在四下伫立，心中隐隐纳闷，为何方才会为了南特总督的离去和一辆双马马车疾驰过桥而这般狂热躁动，毕竟此刻岛上仍有一名刺客逍遥法外——一个英国间谍，若能将他生擒，必将载入南特城的大事记中。
“我看，”马丹-罗热说道，“咱们不妨先去歇息吧，剩下的交给弗勒里指挥官去办。那个英国佬几个小时内未必落网，我反正是筋疲力竭了。”
“你要睡就去睡吧，马丹-罗热公民，”肖维兰冷冷地反驳道，“我反正要留在这儿，直到亲眼看到那个英国佬落入弗勒里指挥官之手。”
“听，”马丹-罗热冷不防打断道，“那是什么声音？”
甚至在马丹-罗热阻拦的手搭上他胳膊之前，肖维兰就已停下了脚步。他僵立在广场中央，双膝剧烈颤抖，险些一头栽倒在地上。
“怎么回事？”马丹-罗热满腹狐疑地又问了一句，“听着像是小拉卢埃的声音。”
肖维兰一言不发。他浑然忘却了身上的伤痛；他不再一瘸一拐——他拔腿飞奔穿过广场，直扑府邸门前，那阵酷似小拉卢埃的声音正是从那里传来的。
那个少年——毫无疑问正是他——正站在府邸拐角处：他头顶上的一盏灯笼投下一圈昏黄的光晕，映照着他那头顶黑卷发、未戴帽子的脑袋，也照亮了围在他身边的一小撮闲汉，其中还夹杂着两三名市政卫兵。肖维兰听得真真切切的头几句话便是：
“你们全都疯了……要不就是全喝醉了……总督公民正在楼上房间里歇着呢……他刚派我下楼来打探打探，看看英国间谍有什么动静……”

#### V / 第五节
===Original===

No one made reply. It seemed as if some giant and spectral hand had passed over this mass of people and with its magic touch had stilled their turbulent passions, silenced their imprecations and cooled their ardour﻿—and left naught but a vague fear, a subtle sense of awe as when something unexplainable and supernatural has manifested itself before the eyes of men.
From far away the roll of coach wheels rapidly disappearing in the distance alone broke the silence of the night.
“Is there no one here who will explain what all this means?” queried young Lalouët, who alone had remained self-assured and calm, for he alone knew nothing of what had happened. “Citizen Fleury, are you there?”
Then as once again he received no reply, he added peremptorily:
“Hey! someone there! Are you all louts and oafs that not one of you can speak?”
A timid voice from the rear ventured on explanation.
“The citizen proconsul was here a moment ago﻿ ﻿… We all saw him, and you citizen Lalouët were with him﻿ ﻿…”
An imprecation from young Lalouët silenced the timid voice for the nonce﻿ ﻿… and then another resumed the halting narrative.
“We all could have sworn that we saw you, citizen Lalouët, also the citizen proconsul.﻿ ﻿… He got into his coach with you﻿ ﻿… you﻿ ﻿… that is﻿ ﻿… they have driven off﻿ ﻿…”
“This is some awful and treacherous hoax,” cried the youngster now in a towering passion; “the citizen proconsul is upstairs in bed, I tell you﻿ ﻿… and I have only just come out of the hotel﻿ ﻿… ! Name of a name of a dog! am I standing here or am I not?”
Then suddenly he bethought himself of the many events of the day which had culminated in this gigantic feat of leger-de-main.
“Chauvelin!” he exclaimed. “Where in the name of h‑ll is citizen Chauvelin?”
But Chauvelin for the moment could nowhere be found. Dazed, half-unconscious, wholly distraught, he had fled from the scene of his discomfiture as fast as his trembling knees would allow.

===Chinese===

无人作答。仿佛有一只无形而巨大的幽灵之手拂过了这群人，以其不可思议的魔力平息了他们沸腾的情绪，令漫天的咒骂归于沉寂，浇熄了他们的狂热——只留下一阵莫名的恐惧与一种微妙的敬畏，正如某种无法解释的超自然现象在世人眼前显圣一般。
唯有从极远处传来的马车车轮辘辘声在夜色深处飞速消逝，打破着周遭死一般的寂静。
“难道这里就没一个人能解释清楚这究竟是怎么回事吗？”小拉卢埃质问道，唯有他依旧神色自若、泰然自若，因为唯有他对自己出现前发生的一切浑然不知。“弗勒里公民，你在吗？”
见依然无人应声，他神色严厉地厉声喝道：
“喂！来个人！你们全是一帮蠢驴笨蛋吗，难道就没一个会说话的？”
人群后方传来一个怯生生的声音，大着胆子试图解释：
“总督公民刚才就在这儿……我们全都看见他了，还有你，拉卢埃公民，你刚才就跟他在一起……”
小拉卢埃的一声咒骂登时叫那个怯懦的声音噎了回去……随后另一个人接过了结结巴巴的话茬。
“我们所有人敢赌咒发誓真真切切看见了你，拉卢埃公民，还有总督公民本人……他跟你一起上了马车……你们……也就是说……他们已经驾车冲出去了……”
“这简直是某种骇人听闻的叛逆骗局！”少年此刻勃然大怒，咆哮起来，“我告诉你们，总督公民正在楼上床上躺着呢……而我刚从府邸里出来……！见他娘的鬼！我到底是不是活生生站在这儿？”
旋即，他猛然想起了当天发生的一桩桩事件，正是那些事一步步推演成了这场惊天动地的瞒天过海之计。
“肖维兰！”他失声大叫，“肖维兰公民到底见鬼地死哪儿去了？”
然而此刻肖维兰却踪影全无。他浑浑噩噩、半昏半醒、失魂落魄，正借着颤抖的双腿以最快的速度从这处令他颜面扫地的奇耻大辱之地仓皇逃窜。

===Original===

Carrier searched the city for him high and low, and for days afterwards the soldiers of the Compagnie Marat gave aristos and rebels a rest: they were on the lookout for a small, wizened figure of a man﻿—the man with the pale, keen eyes who had failed to recognise in the pseudo-Paul Friche, in the dirty, out-at-elbows sansculotte﻿—the most exquisite dandy that had ever graced the salons of Bath and of London: they were searching for the man with the acute and sensitive brain who had failed to scent in the pseudo-Carrier and the pseudo-Lalouët his old and arch enemy Sir Percy Blakeney and the charming wife of my lord Anthony Dewhurst.

===Chinese===

卡里耶在全城掘地三尺搜寻他的下落；在此后的数日里，马拉连士兵破天荒地给贵族和叛民放了个假：他们全力搜寻着一个瘦小枯槁的身影——那个生着一双锐利苍白眼睛的男人，竟未能在假扮保罗·弗里什、衣衫褴褛脏乱不堪的无套裤汉身上，认出那位曾令巴斯与伦敦沙龙增光添彩的最讲究的花花公子；他们搜寻着那个拥有敏锐精明头脑的智者，却未能从假卡里耶与假拉卢埃身上，嗅出他的宿敌珀西·布莱克尼爵士以及安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵那迷人妻子的蛛丝马迹。

## X Lord Tony / 第十章 托尼勋爵
===Original===



===Chinese===

第十章 托尼勋爵

#### I / 第一节
===Original===

A quarter of an hour later citizen-commandant Fleury was at last ushered into the presence of the proconsul and received upon his truly innocent head the full torrent of the despot’s wrath. But Martin-Roget had listened to the counsels of prudence: for obvious reasons he desired to avoid any personal contact for the moment with Carrier, whom fear of the English spies had made into a more abject and more craven tyrant than ever before. At the same time he thought it wisest to try and pacify the brute by sending him the ten thousand francs﻿—the bribe agreed upon for his help in the undertaking which had culminated in such a disastrous failure.
At the selfsame hour whilst Carrier﻿—fuming and swearing﻿—was for the hundredth time uttering that furious “How?” which for the hundredth time had remained unanswered, two men were taking leave of one another at the small postern gate which gives on the cemetery of St. Anne. The taller and younger one of the two had just dropped a heavy purse into the hand of the other. The latter stooped and kissed the kindly hand.
“Milor,” he said, “I swear to you most solemnly that M. le duc de Kernogan will rest in peace in hallowed ground. M. le curé de Vertou﻿—ah! he is a saint and a brave man, milor﻿—comes over whenever he can prudently do so and reads the offices for the dead﻿—over those who have died as Christians, and there is a piece of consecrated ground out here in the open which those fiends of Terrorists have not discovered yet.”
“And you will bury M. le duc immediately,” admonished the younger man, “and apprise M. le curé of what has happened.”
“Aye! aye! I’ll do that, milor, within the hour. Though M. le duc was never a very kind master to me in the past, I cannot forget that I served him and his family for over thirty years as coachman. I drove Mlle. Yvonne in the first pony-cart she ever possessed. I drove her﻿—ah! that was a bitter day!﻿—her and M. le duc when they left Kernogan never to return. I drove Mlle. Yvonne on that memorable night when a crowd of miserable peasants attacked her coach, and that brute Pierre Adet started to lead a rabble against the château. That was the beginning of things, milor. God alone knows what has happened to Pierre Adet. His father Jean was hanged by order of M. le duc. Now M. le duc is destined to lie in a forgotten grave.

===Chinese===

一刻钟后，弗勒里司令官公民终于被领进特派员的跟前，他那颗委实无辜的脑袋立时承受了暴君倾泻而下的满腔雷霆之怒。然而马丹-罗热却听从了谨慎的劝告：出于显而易见的原因，他此刻极力想避免与卡里耶有任何当面接触——对英国间谍的恐惧，已将这位暴君变得比以往任何时候都更加卑劣、更加懦怯。与此同时，他认为最明智的做法是设法安抚这个恶魔，于是派人送去了那一万法郎——即当初为换取对方协助而约定的贿赂，尽管这项行动最终落得个一败涂地的下场。
就在同一时刻，当卡里耶还在暴跳如雷、咒骂不休，第一百次厉声喝问那无人能够作答的“究竟是怎么回事？”时，两名男子正在通往圣安娜墓地的一扇小边门前互道告别。两人中个头较高、也更年轻的那位，刚刚将一只沉甸甸的钱袋塞进另一人的手中。后者躬下身去，亲吻了那只仁慈的手。
“大人，”他说道，“我向您郑重起誓，凯尔诺冈公爵大人一定会安息在圣洁的土地上。韦尔图的主任神父——啊！他真是一位圣徒，也是个勇敢的人，大人——只要情势允许、足够谨慎，他总会赶过来为那些以基督徒之礼离世的人念诵亡魂祈祷经；而且在这荒野里有一处经过祝圣的墓地，那群恐怖分子的恶魔至今还没发现呢。”
“你必须立刻将公爵安葬，”年轻男子叮嘱道，“并将发生的一切通报给本堂神父。”
“遵命！遵命！大人，一个钟头之内我定会办妥。虽说过去公爵大人对我算不上什么慈悲的主子，但我绝忘不了自己曾给他和他的家族当了三十多年的车夫。伊冯娜小姐拥有的第一辆矮种马车，就是我赶的。我还赶车送过她——唉！那是多么凄惨的一天啊！——送她和公爵大人离开凯尔诺冈，一去不返。在那个令人难忘的夜晚，也是我赶着车护送伊冯娜小姐，当时一群穷凶极恶的农民袭击了她的马车，皮埃尔·阿代那个畜生开始纠集乌合之众冲向城堡。那便是这一连串灾祸的开端啊，大人。天晓得皮埃尔·阿代如今落得个什么下场。他的父亲让·阿代因公爵的命令被处以绞刑，而如今，公爵大人自己也注定要长眠于一座荒芜湮没的坟茔之中了。

===Original===

I serve this abominable Republic by digging graves for her victims. I would be happier, I think, if I knew what had become of Mlle. Yvonne.”
“Mlle. Yvonne is my wife, old friend,” said the younger man softly. “Please God she has years of happiness before her, if I succeed in making her forget all that she has suffered.”
“Amen to that, milor!” rejoined the man fervently. “Then I pray you tell the noble lady to rest assured. Jean-Marie﻿—her old coachman whom she used to trust implicitly in the past﻿—will see that M. le duc de Kernogan is buried as a gentleman and a Christian should be.”
“You are not running too great a risk by this, I hope, my good Jean-Marie,” quoth Lord Tony gently.
“No greater risk, milor,” replied Jean-Marie earnestly, “than the one which you ran by carrying my old master’s dead body on your shoulders through the streets of Nantes.”
“Bah! that was simple enough,” said the younger man, “the hue and cry is after higher quarry tonight. Pray God the hounds have not run the noble game to earth.”
Even as he spoke there came from far away through the darkness the sound of a fast trotting pair of horses and the rumble of coach-wheels on the unpaved road.
“There they are, thank God!” exclaimed Lord Tony, and the tremor in his voice alone betrayed the torturing anxiety which he had been enduring, ever since he had seen the last both of his adored young wife and of his gallant chief in the squalid taproom of the Rat Mort.
With the dead body of Yvonne’s father on his back he had quietly worked his way out of the tavern in the wake of his chief. He had his orders, and for the members of that gallant League of the Scarlet Pimpernel there was no such word as “disobedience” and no such word as “fail.” Through the darkness and through the tortuous streets of Nantes Lord Anthony Dewhurst﻿—the young and wealthy exquisite, the hero of an hundred fêtes and galas in Bath, in London﻿—staggered under the weight of a burden imposed upon him only by his loyalty and a noble sense of self-prescribed discipline﻿—and that burden the dead body of the man who had done him an unforgivable wrong. Without a thought of revolt he had obeyed﻿—and risked his life and worse in the obedience.
The darkness of the night was his faithful handmaiden, and the excitement of the chase after the other quarry had fortunately drawn every possible enemy from his track.

===Chinese===

我替这个可憎的共和国给受害者掘墓。我想，倘若能知道伊冯娜小姐的下落，我心里也能宽慰许多。”
“老朋友，伊冯娜小姐如今是我的妻子了，”年轻男子温和地说道，“但愿上帝保佑她往后享有漫长的幸福岁月，只要我能让她忘却所遭受的一切苦难。”
“阿门，大人！”那人热切地应道，“那么求您转告高贵的夫人，请她尽管放心。让-马里——她过去全心信赖的老车夫——一定会妥善料理，让凯尔诺冈公爵大人得到符合绅士与基督徒身份的体面安葬。”
“好让-马里，我希望你这么做不会冒太大的风险。”托尼勋爵温言道。
“大人，”让-马里诚挚地回答，“我的风险再大，也绝不会大过您扛着我旧主人的遗体穿过南特街巷所冒的危险。”
“嗨！那倒算不得什么，”年轻人说，“今晚满城的追兵都在搜捕更显要的猎物。但愿老天保佑，猎犬们还没把那尊贵的猎物逼入绝境。”
正说话间，漆黑的夜色深处隐隐传来了双马飞驰的蹄声，以及车轮在未经铺砌的泥路上辘辘滚动的声响。
“他们来了，谢天谢地！”托尼勋爵失声惊呼，他嗓音中难以自抑的轻微颤抖，泄露了他内心的煎熬与焦灼——自从在“死耗子”酒馆那间污秽邋遢的酒吧间里与他深爱的小娇妻和英勇的首领分别以来，这股痛苦的忧惧便一直折磨着他。
当时，他背负着伊冯娜父亲的遗体，紧随首领身后，悄无声息地脱身离开了小酒馆。他受命在身，而对于英勇的红花侠同盟的成员而言，字典里从来就没有“抗命”二字，也绝没有“失败”可言。在漆黑的夜幕下，在南特曲折交错的街道间，安东尼·迪尤赫斯特勋爵——这位年轻富有的翩翩贵公子，巴斯与伦敦无数宴会盛典上的风云人物——步履踉跄地跋涉着，肩上扛着沉重的重负；唯有他对同盟的忠诚与崇高的自律精神，才促使他担负起这一重担——而这具沉重的遗体，生前曾对他犯下无可饶恕的罪孽。他心中毫无半点违拗或不平之念，甘心领命执行——并在恪尽职守中甘冒生命危险乃至更可怕的代价。
浓稠的夜色成了他忠实的帮手，而追捕另一头更引人注目的猎物所激起的狂热喧嚣，万幸将所有可能追踪他的仇敌全都引开了。

===Original===

He had set his teeth and accomplished his task, and even the deathly anxiety for the wife whom he idolised had been crushed, under the iron heel of a grim resolve. Now his work was done, and from far away he heard the rattle of the coach wheels which were bringing his beloved nearer and nearer to him.
Five minutes longer and the coach came to a halt. A cheery voice called out gaily:
“Tony! are you there?”
“Percy!” exclaimed the young man.
Already he knew that all was well. The gallant leader, the loyal and loving friend, had taxed every resource of a boundlessly fertile brain in order to win yet another wreath of immortal laurels for the League which he commanded, and the very tone of his merry voice proclaimed the triumph which had crowned his daring scheme.
The next moment Yvonne lay in the arms of her dear milor. He had stepped into the carriage, even while Sir Percy climbed nimbly on the box and took the reins from the bewildered coachman’s hands.
“Citizen proconsul﻿ ﻿…” murmured the latter, who of a truth thought that he was dreaming.
“Get off the box, you old noodle,” quoth the pseudo-proconsul peremptorily. “Thou and thy friend the postilion will remain here in the road, and on the morrow you’ll explain to whomsoever it may concern that the English spy made a murderous attack on you both and left you half dead outside the postern gate of the cemetery of Ste. Anne. Here,” he added as he threw a purse down to the two men﻿—who half-dazed and overcome by superstitious fear had indeed scrambled down, one from his box, the other from his horse﻿—“there’s a hundred francs for each of you in there, and mind you drink to the health of the English spy and the confusion of your brutish proconsul.”
There was no time to lose: the horses﻿—still very fresh﻿—were fretting to start.
“Where do we pick up Hastings and Ffoulkes?” asked Sir Percy Blakeney finally as he turned toward the interior of the barouche, the hood of which hid its occupants from view.
“At the corner of the rue de Gigan,” came the quick answer. “It is only two hundred metres from the city gate. They are on the look out for you.”
“Ffoulkes shall be postilion,” rejoined Sir Percy with a laugh, “and Hastings sit beside me on the box. And you will see how at the city gate and all along the route soldiers of the guard will salute the equipage of the all-powerful proconsul of Nantes. By Gad!”

===Chinese===

他咬紧牙关，圆满完成了使命；就连对挚爱妻子的致命忧虑，也被他坚如镔铁的沉毅决绝狠狠压制在心底。如今大功告成，他听见远处马车轮子滚动的辘辘声由远及近，正将他心爱的女人一步步带回他的身边。
五分钟后，马车停了下来。一个欢快爽朗的声音兴高采烈地喊道：
“托尼！你在那儿吗？”
“珀西！”年轻人激动地呼喊。
他立刻明白，一切都安然无恙了。这位英勇的首领、忠诚而挚爱的挚友，再次穷尽了他那浩瀚无穷的奇思妙计，为他所统领的同盟赢得了又一顶不朽的桂冠；而他那欢快欢跃的语调本身，便宣告了他那大胆卓绝的计谋已然大获全胜。
下一瞬间，伊冯娜已然扑进了她亲爱的勋爵大人的怀抱。托尼已跨入车厢，而珀西爵士则敏捷地翻身跃上驾驶座，从目瞪口呆的车夫手中夺过了缰绳。
“特派员公民……”车夫喃喃自语，简直以为自己是在做梦。
“滚下车座，你这个老糊涂，”冒牌特派员语气不容置疑地喝道，“你和你那个骑马差役朋友就留在这条路上。明天不管谁来查问，你们就说英国间谍对你们下了毒手，把你们打得半死扔在圣安娜墓地的边门外。拿着，”说着他将一只钱袋扔给那两个人——他们吓得晕头转向，又被迷信的恐惧摄住，当真连滚带爬地下来了，一个从车座上爬下来，另一个从马背上翻下来——“里面有一人一百法郎，记着拿去喝一杯，祝英国间谍福寿安康，祝你们那个畜生特派员倒大霉！”
分秒必争，刻不容缓：两匹挽马依然脚力充沛，正焦躁地踢踏着蹄子，急欲扬蹄飞奔。
“我们在哪儿接上海斯廷斯和福克斯？”珀西·布莱克尼爵士最后转过头，朝四轮轻便敞篷马车里问道。车篷遮挡住了车内乘客的身影。
“在日冈街拐角处，”车内迅速传来回答，“离城门只有两百米，他们正在那儿放哨等您呢。”
“福克斯来当骑马差役，”珀西爵士大笑着应道，“海斯廷斯就坐在车座上陪我。你们且看着吧，在城门口，在这一整条路上，卫戍警卫士兵全都会毕恭毕敬地向南特这位权势滔天的特派员的座驾敬礼！我的老天！”

===Original===

he added under his breath, “I’ve never had a merrier time in all my life﻿—not even when﻿ ﻿…”
He clicked his tongue and gave the horses their heads﻿—and soon the coachman and the postilion and Jean-Marie the gravedigger of the cemetery of Ste. Anne were left gaping out into the night in the direction where the barouche had so quickly disappeared.
“Now for Le Croisic and the *Daydream*,” sighed the daring adventurer contentedly, “… and for Marguerite!” he added wistfully.

===Chinese===

他又低声喃喃自语道，“我这辈子还从没玩得这么畅快过——哪怕是在当年……”
他咂了咂舌头，缰绳一松任由马匹飞奔——眨眼之间，车夫、骑马差役，还有圣安娜墓地的掘墓人让-马里，全都目瞪口呆地伫立在茫茫夜色中，直勾勾望着那辆轻便马车疾驰消失的方向。
“现在，直奔勒克鲁瓦西克和‘白日梦号’！”这位无畏的冒险家心满意足地舒了一口气，“……还有我挚爱的玛格丽特！”他又深情怅惘地补了一句。

#### II / 第二节
===Original===

Under the hood of the barouche Yvonne, wearied but immeasurably happy, was doing her best to answer all her dear milor’s impassioned questions and to give him a fairly clear account of that terrible chase and flight through the streets of the Isle Feydeau.
“Ah, milor, how can I tell you what I felt when I realised that I was being carried along in the arms of the valiant Scarlet Pimpernel? A word from him and I understood. After that I tried to be both resourceful and brave. When the chase after us was at its hottest we slipped into a ruined and deserted house. In a room at the back there were several bundles of what looked like old clothes. ‘This is my storehouse,’ milor said to me; ‘now that we have reached it we can just make long noses at the whole pack of bloodhounds.’ He made me slip into some boy’s clothes which he gave me, and whilst I donned these he disappeared. When he returned I truly did not recognise him. He looked horrible, and his voice﻿ ﻿… ! After a moment or two he laughed, and then I knew him. He explained to me the role which I was to play, and I did my best to obey him in everything. But oh! I hardly lived while we once more emerged into the open street and then turned into the great Place which was full﻿—oh full!﻿—of people. I felt that at every moment we might be suspected. Figure to yourself, my dear milor﻿ ﻿…”
What Yvonne Dewhurst was about to say next will never be recorded. My lord Tony had closed her lips with a kiss.

===Chinese===

在马车的车篷下，伊冯娜虽然疲惫不堪，心中却充溢着无边无际的幸福。她极力回应着亲爱的勋爵大人连珠炮般深情关切的问话，并条理分明地向他讲述了穿过费多岛街巷时那场惊心动魄的追捕与逃亡。
“啊，大人，当我发觉自己正被英勇的红花侠抱在怀中飞奔时，该如何向您形容我当时的心情呢？他只消说一句话，我便全然明白了。自那以后，我便竭力让自己既机敏又勇敢。当追兵搜捕得最紧迫的时候，我们潜入了一间废弃破败的房屋。在后头的一间屋子里，堆着几个看起来像旧衣服的包裹。‘这就是我的秘密库房，’爵士对我说，‘既然已经到了这里，我们尽可以冲着整群恶犬扮鬼脸嘲弄他们了。’他让我换上一套他递给我的男童衣裳，正当我穿戴整齐时，他却不见了踪影。等他再次现身时，我真的一眼都没认出他来。他的模样丑陋可怖，还有他的声音……！过了一两秒，他笑出了声，我这才认出他来。他向我交代了我应当扮演的角色，而我无论何事都竭尽所能听从他的吩咐。可是天啊！当我们重新走上开阔的大街，拐进那座挤满——哦，黑压压挤满！——人群的大广场时，我吓得简直连气都透不过来了。我觉得每分每秒我们都有被识破的危险。您不妨设想一下，我亲爱的大人……”
伊冯娜·迪尤赫斯特接下来还想说些什么，世人将永远无从知晓了。因为托尼勋爵已用深情的一吻，封住了她的双唇。

## Endnotes / 尾注
===Original===

1. This adventure is recorded in *The Elusive Pimpernel*. ↩︎

===Chinese===

1. 本次冒险记载于《红花侠追踪记》（*The Elusive Pimpernel*）。 ↩︎
